# Dots — Plan 3c: Hardening and Packaging Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the Dots desktop app safe to hand to a person: no process outlives its run, the review actions and the policy hold against odd inputs, the app leaves a trace when it fails, the UI's cache never falls behind the backend, and an NSIS installer and a written smoke test exist.

**Architecture:** Nothing new is built on top; every task tightens something that Plans 1 to 3b made. The core gains a Windows Job Object around each run's process tree, a refined walking rule in the policy, one containment check for both review actions, and four runtime checks. The Tauri shell gains start-up and notifier fixes and a contract fixture that also holds command names, error kinds and engine events. The UI gets one helper for marking queries stale that cannot lose a mark, one shared set of review actions, and fixes for screens that block or mislead. The last two tasks configure and build the installer and write the smoke test, with the steps only a person can do left as a checklist.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.95, Tauri 2.12, `windows-sys` 0.61.2 (new, Windows only); Bun 1.3, Vite 8.3, React 19.3, TypeScript 6.0 (strict), TanStack Query 5.104, zod 4.6, Vitest 5.0 with Testing Library and jsdom; NSIS through the Tauri bundler.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-dots-design.md` — §4 "Timeouts, cancellation, recovery", §5 "Path rules on the deny/ask side", §8, §12 "Hardening and packaging (3c)". Amended for this plan in commit `250263a`.

**Plan 3 is split in three.** 3a (core additions, Tauri shell) is PR #2, branch `feat/app`. 3b (the six screens) is PR #3, branch `feat/app-ui`. This is **3c**, the last part.

**Previous plans:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-dots-core.md` (Plan 1), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-dots-local-engine.md` (Plan 2), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-dots-app-3a.md` (Plan 3a), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-dots-app-3b.md` (Plan 3b).

**Where the tasks come from:** spec §12 "Hardening and packaging (3c)"; the findings the reviews of Plan 3a deferred to 3c; and the triage of the final review of Plan 3b. Each task's text names the finding it answers.

**How this plan's code was checked:** the code blocks below were generated from a throwaway worktree of commit `86196fe` (the head of Plan 3b), one commit per task, where they compiled and passed on Windows 11. At the last task: `cargo test -p dots-core` passed 269 tests with 0 failures, `cargo test -p dots-app` passed 35, `bun run --cwd ui test` passed 221 tests in 20 files, `node --test tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs` passed 15, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` reported no warnings, `cargo fmt --check` and `tsc --noEmit` were clean. Every task's own state was checked the same way: the test counts in each task's steps were measured at that task's commit, and where a task has a "verify they fail" step, its result was measured by putting the task's tests onto the commit before it. The plan was then replayed: its code blocks were applied to a fresh checkout of the spec commit, and the resulting tree is the same as the prototype's. Task 10's installer was built once and its generated script was read; the installer was not run. Part A of Task 11's smoke was run once on a debug build of the prototype, with a temporary data folder, the fake CLI and LM Studio: 29 steps, all passed, every one of the 35 commands answered, and the 26 screenshots were looked at. It found one cosmetic defect, listed under "After this plan". Not checked: a build on Linux or macOS, a real symlink in a worktree (this machine refuses to create one), the installer at run time, and everything in Part B of the smoke.

## Global Constraints

- Toolchain: Rust 1.95 stable; Node 24; Bun 1.3 (`bun x`, not `bunx`, which is not on the PATH in Git Bash); WebView2 runtime. Windows 11, Git Bash.
- Branch `feat/app-pack`, stacked on `feat/app-ui` (PR #3), in the worktree `C:\Users\aksha\dots\.claude\worktrees\app`. All commands run from the repo root unless a step says otherwise. Never commit to `master`.
- One new dependency in the whole plan: `windows-sys = "=0.61.2"` for Windows targets in `crates/dots-core` (Task 1), the version already in `Cargo.lock`. No new npm package.
- The UI reaches the backend only through `ui/src/api/commands.ts` and `ui/src/api/events.ts`, plus `open` from `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog`. No `fetch`, no other plugin, no new capability: `capabilities/default.json` stays `core:default` and `dialog:allow-open`.
- Live data is parsed, not cast. A type the UI parses changes only together with the contract fixture: `UPDATE_CONTRACT=1 cargo test -p dots-app contract`, then the same test without the variable, then `ui/src/api/types.ts`.
- From Task 7 on, a query is marked stale after "the backend changed" with `invalidate(qc, key)` from `@/lib/query`, not with a bare `qc.invalidateQueries`. The one exception is a caller that must wait for the reload.
- Every destructive action asks first in an in-app dialog (`@/components/ConfirmButton`). Discard and the review of a worktree are offered only while the core says the worktree exists.
- Files under `ui/src/components/ui/` are generated by the shadcn CLI and are not edited. Imports inside `ui/src` use the `@/` alias.
- Project rules: `sqlx` runtime queries only; schema changes only through `crates/dots-core/migrations/` (this plan has none); timestamps are RFC 3339 UTC with milliseconds from `util::now()`.
- Before each commit: the task's own tests, then, as the change requires, `cargo test` of the crates touched, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` (no warnings), `cargo fmt --check`, `DEBUG_PRINT_LIMIT=0 bun run --cwd ui test`, `bun run --cwd ui typecheck`. Test output must be free of warnings. `cargo test -p dots-app` and clippy need `ui/dist`: in a fresh checkout run `bun install --frozen-lockfile` and `bun run --cwd ui build` first.
- Known on this machine: a `vitest` run started right after an install, a `tsc` run, a cargo build or a large write can fail with `[vitest-pool-runner]: Timeout waiting for worker to respond`, or show 30 s timeouts in the tests that type into forms. Run it again; do not change the pool settings. Run the UI tests before the typecheck.
- Known on this machine: `local_tools_test::shell_stops_at_its_timeout_and_on_cancel` (a 1 s timing test) can fail once under load. Run it alone; if it passes, go on.
- Creating a symlink on Windows needs a privilege or developer mode. Task 3's test with a real symlink prints a `SKIPPED` line when the OS refuses; its junction tests need no privilege.
- A running `dots-app.exe` or `fake-claude.exe` under `target/` blocks cargo builds. A smoke run uses a temporary `DOTS_DATA_DIR`, never the real `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dots`, and stops only the processes it started.
- An agent never runs the installer, never installs or uninstalls anything, and never runs the real `claude` CLI. Those steps of Tasks 10 and 11 are for the person who owns the machine.
- Subscription rule: Dots only spawns the official `claude` CLI and never reads, copies or sends its credentials.
- Commit messages: Conventional Commits; no AI co-author or attribution trailers.

### Changes a user will notice

- On Windows, a background process started by a local dot's `shell` call is stopped when that call returns (Task 1). A local dot can no longer start a server in one call and use it in the next. The spec asks for a Job Object per `shell` call.
- With a policy whose default is `deny`, `list_dir` shows the names of directories that only the default denies, and a search from the workspace root walks the whole tree and counts every hidden file (Task 2).
- A port that another program holds delays the start of Dots by two seconds before it starts without its local server (Task 4).
- A program path with an invisible or text-direction character is refused in Settings (Task 4). That includes the left-to-right mark that Explorer's Properties dialog puts in front of a copied path, and the zero-width joiners used in some scripts.
- A run that parks shows one notification, not two (Task 5).
- At start the app asks for the first screen's data twice (Task 7).
- "Restart Dots" asks first when a run has not ended (Task 9).
- Added while the plan was executed (see "What changed while this plan was executed"): a Claude dot's run, like a local `shell` call, ends every process it started when it ends; a second Dots on the same data folder shows an error page instead of starting; "Restart Dots" shows the window also when Dots was started hidden; a search whose starting point the policy denies is refused with an error.

### Deliberate deviations from the spec

Each row is also written into the spec by the amendment commit named above.

| Spec | Plan 3c | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §4: "cancel/timeout closes the job" | Cancel and timeout call `TerminateJobObject`, then the job is closed | §12 says so, and terminate also works through the fallback |
| §12: the job is "assigned right after spawn" | A child can start a process of its own in the 35 to 72 microseconds before it is assigned; that process is outside the job | `tokio::process` on stable Rust cannot start a child suspended and resume it. Both engines send their work on stdin after the assignment, so nothing the model asked for runs in that window |
| §5: walking tools | A link or a junction to a directory is judged as a file and never entered; `list_dir` lists a default-denied directory by name | The spec is silent on links; the name must be visible for the model to reach the allowed subtree |
| §12: "untracked symlinks are listed, not followed" | A link is shown with the place it points to (git's own patch for a link); files that git names behind a junction are dropped | A reviewer must see where a link made by a dot points |
| §12: review actions | Also: after git removed a worktree, what is left of its folder is deleted (a junction is removed as a link); discard finds the repository from the worktree; the branch must be `dots/<name>/<folder>` | Found while prototyping: `git worktree remove` leaves a junction and its folder behind and still succeeds |
| §8: "port N is in use" | That text only for "address in use"; any other bind failure says `could not listen on port N: <os error>` | The old text was wrong for other failures |
| §12 Chat: "Sending continues from the last terminal run of the thread" (a UI rule) | The core enforces it: `chat` refuses a parent that is not the thread's last run or whose thread is active, under a lock | Two follow-ups sent at once forked the thread in every trial without the lock |
| §12: `claude_path` | Control and Unicode format characters are refused | They can make the path in the confirmation dialog read differently from what will run |
| §12: notifications "on `ApprovalRequested`, and on `RunUpdated` into ... `awaiting_approval`" | A park whose approval request was already announced does not notify again | Two toasts for one event |
| §12: "a panic is written to the log" | The line is appended to the log file directly, not through the logger | The logger writes on a worker thread; a line logged right before an abort reached the file in 14 of 30 trials |
| §12 UI: events patch the keys `run`, `runs`, `thread`, … | The key `run` is removed | No query read it |
| §12 contract | The fixture also lists the command names and the answer of `new_dot_spec`; `CmdError.kind` is an enum in Rust | A command's name and a new dot's defaults could drift with no test failing |
| §12: the manual smoke | The document holds agent-run steps with results and person-run steps as a checklist; the installer is built and inspected, not run, by the agent | The real `claude` CLI, the tray, a toast, the native dialog and an installation belong to the person who owns the machine |

## Review Focus

1. **A process that outlives its run** — a tool starts a child that starts another and exits; the run is cancelled, times out, or Dots is killed. Nothing of the tree may stay alive. → Task 1 `proc_test::terminate_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited`, `proc_test::a_job_ends_when_the_process_holding_it_is_killed`, `claude_engine_test::cancel_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited`, `local_tools_test::shell_cancel_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited`.
2. **A deny rule weakened by the new walking rule** — only a directory denied by the default may be entered; a directory that a deny rule matches must still not be read, under any preset. → Task 2 `a_directory_matched_by_a_deny_rule_is_not_entered`, `denied_by_rule_agrees_with_resolve_for_every_preset`, `read_only_and_sandboxed_walks_skip_git_and_show_dot_claude`.
3. **Git or a delete reaching outside Dots' worktrees** — a run row that names another path or branch, a junction inside a worktree, a folder git left behind. The diff must refuse the row, must not read behind a link, and a discard must never touch a link's target. → Task 3 `diff_and_discard_refuse_a_path_that_is_not_a_direct_child_of_the_worktrees_folder`, `diff_and_discard_refuse_a_branch_dots_did_not_create`, `an_untracked_junction_is_listed_once_and_not_entered`, `discarding_a_worktree_with_a_junction_removes_the_folder_and_spares_the_target`, `a_leftover_outside_the_worktrees_folder_is_reported_and_not_deleted`.
4. **The person confirming something other than what will run, or a conversation that splits** — a program path with hidden characters must never reach the confirmation dialog; two replies to one message must not both be accepted. → Task 4 `claude_path_refuses_characters_that_hide_or_reorder_it`, `a_program_path_that_would_not_show_as_it_is_never_reaches_the_question`, `two_follow_ups_sent_at_once_do_not_fork_the_thread`.
5. **A test or development instance touching the installed app, and a failure nobody can read** — an instance started with `DOTS_DATA_DIR` must not hand over to a running Dots; a panic and a start without a data folder must leave a readable trace. → Task 5 `single_instance_is_skipped_under_a_data_dir_override`, `a_data_dir_override_is_made_absolute`, `a_real_panic_reaches_the_log_and_then_the_hook_before`, `a_missing_local_data_folder_is_a_startup_error_unless_the_override_names_a_folder`.

### Task 1: A Windows Job Object holds each run's process tree

Today cancel and timeout call `proc::kill_tree`, which is `taskkill /T /F`. It follows parent-child links, so a process whose parent has already exited survives, and nothing dies when Dots itself is killed. This task puts the `claude` process of a run, and each `shell` call of the local engine, into a Job Object that kills everything in it when it is closed.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/Cargo.toml`, `crates/dots-core/src/proc.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/engine/claude/mod.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/engine/local/tools/shell.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/bin/fake-claude.rs`, `Cargo.lock`
- Create: `crates/dots-core/tests/proc_test.rs`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/proc_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/claude_engine_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/local_tools_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/common/mod.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `proc::kill_tree`, `proc::hide_window` (Plan 1); the fake CLI (`fake-claude`).
- Produces:
  - `proc::Job` (`Send + Sync`): `Job::assign(child: &tokio::process::Child) -> std::io::Result<Job>`, `Job::fallback(child: &tokio::process::Child) -> Job`, `async fn terminate(&self)`, `fn close(self)`.
  - On Windows `assign` makes a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` and puts the child in it; it fails when a Win32 call fails or the child has already been waited for. `terminate` calls `TerminateJobObject`. Dropping or closing the `Job` closes the only handle, which kills what is left; so does the death of the process that holds it. On other systems, and through `fallback`, a `Job` is only the child's process id: `terminate` is `kill_tree(pid)` and dropping does nothing. Callers need no `cfg`.
  - The Claude engine assigns the job on the line after the spawn, terminates it on cancel and timeout, and closes it as soon as `claude` has ended, before it drains the pipes. When `assign` fails it logs `no job object for claude, using kill_tree: {e}` and goes on with `Job::fallback`.
  - The local `shell` tool makes one job per call, before the script is written to the shell's stdin, and holds it until the call returns. Its warning is `no job object for the shell, using kill_tree: {e}`.
  - Windows-only dependency `windows-sys = "=0.61.2"` (the version already in `Cargo.lock`) with the features `Win32_Security`, `Win32_System_JobObjects`, `Win32_System_Threading`. This is the first `unsafe` in the repository: four Win32 calls, each with a `SAFETY` comment.
  - Test tooling: fake CLI modes `--linger <dir>`, `--orphan <dir>`, `--tree <dir>`, `--hold-job <dir>` and the script step `{"orphan": "<dir>"}`; `tests/common/mod.rs` gains `Lingerer` (`new()`, `dir()`, `alive()`, `started()`, `gone_within(Duration)`), which watches a grandchild through a file lock that process holds. No test kills anything by process id, and a lingerer stops by itself when the guard is dropped, also when a test fails.

A behaviour to know before you start: on Windows, a background process started by a `shell` call no longer outlives that call. The spec asks for it (§12: "to each local `shell` call ... dropping the handle kills the rest").

In `crates/dots-core/src/bin/fake-claude.rs`, replace:

```rust

use std::io::{Read, Write};
use std::time::Duration;

use serde_json::{json, Value};

fn flag(args: &[String], name: &str) -> Option<String> {
```

with:

```rust

use std::io::{Read, Write};
use std::path::Path;
use std::process::Stdio;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

use serde_json::{json, Value};
use tokio::process::{Child, Command};

/// Stays alive until `<dir>/stop` appears, `dir` is removed or 30 s pass, and holds a lock on
/// `<dir>/alive` all the while. The OS releases the lock when the process dies, so a test sees
/// whether it still runs without knowing its process id. `<dir>/ready` appears once the lock is
/// held.
fn linger(dir: &Path) {
    let alive = std::fs::File::create(dir.join("alive")).unwrap();
    alive.lock().unwrap();
    std::fs::write(dir.join("ready"), "").unwrap();
    let end = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(30);
    while Instant::now() < end && dir.exists() && !dir.join("stop").exists() {
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20));
    }
}

/// This program again, in `mode` for `dir`, with no console window and no streams of its own.
/// It still inherits every handle of this process, as children on Windows do, so a lingering
/// process holds the output pipes of whoever started this one, like a real background process.
fn helper(mode: &str, dir: &str) -> Command {
    let mut cmd = Command::new(std::env::current_exe().unwrap());
    cmd.args([mode, dir])
        .stdin(Stdio::null())
        .stdout(Stdio::null())
        .stderr(Stdio::null());
    dots_core::proc::hide_window(&mut cmd);
    cmd
}

/// Starts a lingering process and returns once it is ready. Stdin is read to its end first, so
/// whoever started this process can put it in a job before it has a child.
async fn start_lingerer(dir: &str) -> Child {
    std::io::stdin().read_to_end(&mut Vec::new()).ok();
    let child = helper("--linger", dir).spawn().unwrap();
    let ready = Path::new(dir).join("ready");
    let end = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
    while !ready.exists() {
        assert!(Instant::now() < end, "the lingering process did not start");
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10)).await;
    }
    child
}

fn flag(args: &[String], name: &str) -> Option<String> {
```

```rust
        // Leaves a lingering child behind: a process whose parent has exited.
        (Some("--orphan"), Some(dir)) => {
            start_lingerer(dir).await;
            return;

        // Stays for as long as its lingering child: a tree with every link alive.

            let _ = start_lingerer(dir).await.wait().await;
            return;
        }
        // Holds an orphan in a job and never lets go of it, as Dots does until it is killed.
        (Some("--hold-job"), Some(dir)) => {
            let mut child = helper("--orphan", dir)

            drop(child.stdin.take());
            let _ = child.wait().await;
            tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_secs(30)).await;
            return;
        }
        _ => {}
    }
    let mut prompt = String::new();
    std::io::stdin().read_to_string(&mut prompt).ok();
```

Append to `crates/dots-core/tests/common/mod.rs`:

```rust
/// The directory of one lingering process of the fake CLI (`fake-claude --linger`, started by
/// its `--orphan` and `--tree` modes and by the `orphan` script step). The process is watched
/// through the lock it holds, not through its process id, and is told to stop when this is
/// dropped, so a failed test leaves nothing running and kills nothing.
pub struct Lingerer {
    dir: TempDir,
}

impl Lingerer {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            dir: tempfile::tempdir().unwrap(),
        }
    }

    /// The directory, as the fake CLI takes it.
    pub fn dir(&self) -> String {
        self.dir.path().to_string_lossy().to_string()
    }

    /// Whether the process is running: it holds a lock on `alive` for as long as it lives.
    pub fn alive(&self) -> bool {
        match std::fs::File::open(self.dir.path().join("alive")) {
            Ok(file) => matches!(file.try_lock(), Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock)),
            Err(_) => false,
        }
    }

    /// Waits until the process runs.
    pub async fn started(&self) {
        let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + std::time::Duration::from_secs(20);
        while !self.dir.path().join("ready").exists() {
            assert!(
                std::time::Instant::now() < deadline,
                "the lingering process did not start"
            );
            tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
        }
        assert!(self.alive(), "the lingering process should be running");
    }

    /// Whether the process ends within `limit`.
    pub async fn gone_within(&self, limit: std::time::Duration) -> bool {
        let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + limit;
        while self.alive() {
            if std::time::Instant::now() > deadline {
                return false;
            }
            tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
        }
        true
    }
}

impl Drop for Lingerer {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::write(self.dir.path().join("stop"), "");
        let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + std::time::Duration::from_secs(5);
        while self.alive() && std::time::Instant::now() < deadline {
            std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(20));
        }
    }
}
```

Create `crates/dots-core/tests/proc_test.rs`:

```rust
mod common;

use std::process::Stdio;
use std::time::Duration;

use common::Lingerer;
use dots_core::proc::{self, Job};
use tokio::process::{Child, Command};

/// The fake CLI in one of its process-tree modes. It starts nothing until its stdin closes.
fn fake(mode: &str, lingerer: &Lingerer) -> Child {
    let mut cmd = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-claude"));
    cmd.args([mode, &lingerer.dir()])
        .stdin(Stdio::piped())
        .stdout(Stdio::null())
        .stderr(Stdio::null())
        .kill_on_drop(true);
    proc::hide_window(&mut cmd);
    cmd.spawn().unwrap()
}

/// A job whose only living process is a grandchild: the child that started it has exited,
/// so no parent link leads to it any more.
#[cfg(windows)]
async fn job_with_an_orphan(lingerer: &Lingerer) -> Job {
    let mut child = fake("--orphan", lingerer);
    let job = Job::assign(&child).unwrap();
    drop(child.stdin.take());
    assert!(child.wait().await.unwrap().success());
    assert!(lingerer.alive(), "the grandchild should outlive its parent");
    job
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn terminate_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited() {
    let lingerer = Lingerer::new();
    let job = job_with_an_orphan(&lingerer).await;
    job.terminate().await;
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after terminate"
    );
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn dropping_the_job_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited() {
    let lingerer = Lingerer::new();
    let job = job_with_an_orphan(&lingerer).await;
    drop(job);
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after the job was dropped"
    );
}

/// Dots being killed runs no destructor: the job must end because its handle is gone.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_job_ends_when_the_process_holding_it_is_killed() {
    let lingerer = Lingerer::new();
    let mut holder = fake("--hold-job", &lingerer);
    lingerer.started().await;
    holder.kill().await.unwrap();
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after the holder of its job was killed"
    );
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_process_that_has_exited_cannot_be_assigned() {
    let lingerer = Lingerer::new();
    let mut child = fake("--version", &lingerer);
    child.wait().await.unwrap();
    assert!(Job::assign(&child).is_err());
}

/// Without a job object `terminate` is `kill_tree`: it ends the child, and on Windows
/// everything a parent link still leads to.
#[tokio::test]
async fn the_fallback_kills_a_tree_that_is_still_linked() {
    let lingerer = Lingerer::new();
    let mut child = fake("--tree", &lingerer);
    let job = Job::fallback(&child);
    drop(child.stdin.take());
    lingerer.started().await;
    job.terminate().await;
    let exited = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), child.wait()).await;
    assert!(exited.is_ok(), "the child is still running after terminate");
    #[cfg(windows)]
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after terminate"
    );
}
```

```rust
/// `taskkill /T` follows parent links, so it misses a process whose parent has exited.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn cancel_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited() {
    let lingerer = common::Lingerer::new();
    let e = e2e(
        json!([init(),
               {"orphan": lingerer.dir()},
               {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": "orphaned"}]}}},
               {"sleep_ms": 60000},
               success("never")]),
        None,
    )
    .await;
    let dot = folder_dot(&e, 280).await;
    let run = e
        .runner
        .enqueue(NewRun::new(&dot.id, TriggerKind::Manual))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    // The text follows the `orphan` step, which ends when the grandchild's parent has exited.
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(20);
    loop {
        let evs = e.store.list_events(&run.id, 0).await.unwrap();
        if evs.iter().any(|ev| ev.kind == "assistant_text") {
            break;
        }
        assert!(
            std::time::Instant::now() < deadline,
            "the fake CLI did not get past its orphan step"
        );
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
    }
    assert!(lingerer.alive(), "the grandchild should be running");
    e.runner.cancel(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    wait_status(&e.store, &run.id, RunStatus::Cancelled, 10).await;
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after cancel"
    );
}

async fn a_finished_run_leaves_no_process_behind() {

    let e = e2e(
        json!([init(), {"orphan": lingerer.dir()}, success("done")]),
        None,
    )

    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let run = e
        .runner
        .enqueue(NewRun::new(&dot.id, TriggerKind::Manual))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    wait_status(&e.store, &run.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;

    assert!(
        started.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(5),
        "the run waited for a process its CLI left behind"
    );
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after the run ended"
    );
}
```

```rust
}

/// PowerShell that runs `fake-claude --orphan` and waits for it: when it is done, a grandchild
/// of the shell lingers and the process that started it has exited. `Start-Process` is used
/// because `&` would also wait for the grandchild, which inherits the pipes `&` reads from.
#[cfg(windows)]
fn orphan_script(lingerer: &common::Lingerer) -> String {
    format!(
        "$p = Start-Process -NoNewWindow -PassThru -FilePath '{}' -ArgumentList '--orphan','\"{}\"'; $p.WaitForExit()",
        env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-claude"),
        lingerer.dir()
    )
}

/// `taskkill /T` follows parent links, so it misses a process whose parent has exited.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn shell_cancel_kills_a_grandchild_whose_parent_has_exited() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let lingerer = common::Lingerer::new();
    // The file says that the grandchild's parent has exited.
    let script = format!(
        "{}; Set-Content -Path orphaned -Value 1; Start-Sleep -Seconds 30",
        orphan_script(&lingerer)
    );
    let call = tools::prepare("shell", &json!({ "command": script }), ws.path()).unwrap();
    let cancel = CancellationToken::new();
    let cancel_once_orphaned = async {
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(30);
        while !ws.path().join("orphaned").exists() {
            assert!(Instant::now() < deadline, "the script did not get that far");
            tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
        }
        assert!(lingerer.alive(), "the grandchild should be running");
        cancel.cancel();
        Instant::now()
    };
    let (out, cancelled) =
        tokio::join!(tools::run(&call, ws.path(), &cancel), cancel_once_orphaned);
    assert_eq!(out, ToolOutput::err("cancelled"));
    // The grandchild holds the shell's output pipes; left alone it delays the answer by 5 s.
    assert!(
        cancelled.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(3),
        "cancel waited for the grandchild"
    );
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after cancel"
    );
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn shell_leaves_no_process_behind_when_it_returns() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let lingerer = common::Lingerer::new();
    let script = format!("{}; Write-Output left", orphan_script(&lingerer));
    let out = run(ws.path(), "shell", json!({ "command": script })).await;
    assert!(!out.is_error && out.output.contains("left"), "{out:?}");
    assert!(
        lingerer.gone_within(Duration::from_secs(5)).await,
        "the grandchild is still running after the call returned"
    );
}
```

```rust
use tokio::io::{AsyncRead, AsyncReadExt};

/// Windows `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` process creation flag.
#[cfg(windows)]
pub const CREATE_NO_WINDOW: u32 = 0x0800_0000;

/// Prevents a console window from flashing up when a GUI process spawns a console program.
pub fn hide_window(cmd: &mut tokio::process::Command) {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        cmd.creation_flags(CREATE_NO_WINDOW);
    }
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let _ = cmd;
    }
}

/// Kills a process and all of its descendants. Best effort; errors are ignored.
pub async fn kill_tree(pid: u32) {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let mut cmd = tokio::process::Command::new("taskkill");
        cmd.args(["/PID", &pid.to_string(), "/T", "/F"])
            .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())
            .stderr(std::process::Stdio::null());
        hide_window(&mut cmd);
        let _ = cmd.status().await;
    }
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let _ = tokio::process::Command::new("kill")
            .args(["-KILL", &pid.to_string()])
            .status()
            .await;
    }
}

/// The processes of one spawned child: the child and everything it starts.
///
/// On Windows this is a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`. A process started
/// by a member is a member too, and stays one when its parent exits, which is where `kill_tree`
/// loses it. `terminate` kills every member. So does dropping the `Job`, and so does the end of
/// Dots itself, because the `Job` holds the only handle to the job object.
///
/// On other systems, and when Windows refuses the job object, a `Job` is only the child's
/// process id: `terminate` is `kill_tree` and dropping it does nothing.
pub struct Job {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    object: Option<std::os::windows::io::OwnedHandle>,
    pid: Option<u32>,
}

impl Job {
    /// Puts `child` into a new job. To be called right after the spawn: what the child starts
    /// before this call is outside the job. An error means that Windows gave no job object;
    /// `fallback` is what is left then.
    pub fn assign(child: &tokio::process::Child) -> std::io::Result<Self> {
        #[cfg(windows)]
        {
            Ok(Self {
                object: Some(job_object::holding(child)?),
                pid: child.id(),
            })
        }
        #[cfg(not(windows))]
        {
            Ok(Self::fallback(child))
        }
    }

    /// A job without a job object.
    pub fn fallback(child: &tokio::process::Child) -> Self {
        Self {
            #[cfg(windows)]
            object: None,
            pid: child.id(),
        }
    }

    /// Kills every process of the job. Without a job object that is `kill_tree` on the child's
    /// process id, so the child must not have been dropped yet.
    pub async fn terminate(&self) {
        #[cfg(windows)]
        if let Some(object) = &self.object {
            match job_object::terminate(object) {
                Ok(()) => return,
                Err(e) => tracing::warn!("terminating the job object failed: {e}"),
            }
        }
        if let Some(pid) = self.pid {
            kill_tree(pid).await;
        }
    }

    /// Ends the job at a chosen point, which kills what is still in it. This is dropping it,
    /// under a name that says so; without a job object nothing happens.
    pub fn close(self) {}
}

/// The Win32 calls behind `Job`.
#[cfg(windows)]
mod job_object {
    use std::io;
    use std::os::windows::io::{AsRawHandle, FromRawHandle, OwnedHandle};

    use windows_sys::Win32::System::JobObjects::{
        AssignProcessToJobObject, CreateJobObjectW, JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation,
        SetInformationJobObject, TerminateJobObject, JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
        JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION, JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE,
    };

    /// A new job object with `child` in it. The handle returned is the only one there is: no
    /// process inherits it, so closing it ends the job.
    pub(super) fn holding(child: &tokio::process::Child) -> io::Result<OwnedHandle> {
        let process = child
            .raw_handle()
            .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("the process has already exited"))?;
        // SAFETY: both pointers may be null, for the default security (a handle that child
        // processes do not inherit) and no name.
        let raw = unsafe { CreateJobObjectW(std::ptr::null(), std::ptr::null()) };
        if raw.is_null() {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        // SAFETY: `raw` is an open handle that nothing else owns.
        let job = unsafe { OwnedHandle::from_raw_handle(raw) };
        let limits = JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION {
            BasicLimitInformation: JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION {
                LimitFlags: JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE,
                ..Default::default()
            },
            ..Default::default()
        };
        // SAFETY: `limits` is the structure this information class takes, it outlives the call
        // and the length given is its size.
        let set = unsafe {
            SetInformationJobObject(
                raw,
                JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation,
                std::ptr::from_ref(&limits).cast(),
                size_of_val(&limits) as u32,
            )
        };
        if set == 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        // SAFETY: both handles are open: `job` owns the first, and tokio keeps the second for
        // as long as `child` has not been waited for.
        if unsafe { AssignProcessToJobObject(raw, process) } == 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        Ok(job)
    }

    /// Ends every process in the job; each exits with code 1.
    pub(super) fn terminate(job: &OwnedHandle) -> io::Result<()> {
        // SAFETY: the handle is open for as long as `job` is borrowed.
        if unsafe { TerminateJobObject(job.as_raw_handle(), 1) } == 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Reads `r` to the end and returns its last `max` bytes, lossily decoded as UTF-8.
pub async fn read_tail(mut r: impl AsyncRead + Unpin, max: usize) -> String {
    let mut buf: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();
    let mut chunk = [0u8; 4096];
    loop {
        match r.read(&mut chunk).await {
            Ok(0) | Err(_) => break,
            Ok(n) => {
                buf.extend_from_slice(&chunk[..n]);
                if buf.len() > max {
                    let excess = buf.len() - max;
                    buf.drain(..excess);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf).to_string()
}
```

```rust
/// Kills the shell and its descendants. Outside Windows the job would kill only `sh`, so the
/// shell's process group is killed instead.
async fn stop(job: &crate::proc::Job, pid: Option<u32>) {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let _ = pid;
        job.terminate().await;
    }
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let _ = job;
        if let Some(pid) = pid {
            let _ = Command::new("kill")
                .args(["-KILL", "--", &format!("-{pid}")])
                .status()
                .await;

    }
}
```

---

### Task 2: Walking tools enter a directory unless a deny rule matches it

The local engine's `glob`, `grep` and `list_dir` skip every entry that resolves to `deny`. Under a policy whose default is `deny`, that hides `src` even when `Read(src/**)` is allowed, so an allowed subtree cannot be found from the workspace root. The refined rule (spec §5): a directory is skipped only when a deny rule matches it; a file is skipped whenever it resolves to `deny`, by rule or by default.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/policy.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/engine/local/tools/mod.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/engine/local/tools/search.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/engine/local/tools/fs.rs`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/policy_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/local_tools_test.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Policy::resolve_in`, `PathFilter` (Plans 1 to 3a).
- Produces:
  - `Policy::denied_by_rule(&self, tool: &str, input: &Value, workspace: Option<&Path>) -> bool`: true when a deny rule matches the call; false when the call is denied only by the default, or not denied. It uses the same matcher and the same workspace handling as `resolve_in`, so the directory-itself rule for `/**` patterns and the workspace-joined absolute form apply to it too.
  - `resolve_in` keeps its order (deny rules, allow rules, ask rules, default) and every result. Its body moves into the private `Policy::rule_matches` and `workspace_key`.
  - `PathFilter::new(hides: impl Fn(&str, bool) -> bool + Send + Sync + 'static)` and `PathFilter::hides(&self, rel: &str, is_dir: bool) -> bool`: the new `bool` says whether the entry is a real directory. `PathFilter::from_policy` hides a directory only when `denied_by_rule` is true for it under the walking tool's alias or under `Read`, and hides every other entry whenever `resolve_in` answers `deny` under either.
  - A link or a junction to a directory is not a directory here: it is judged as a file and never entered.
  - What `(<n> entries hidden by policy)` counts: each entry the filter refused, once. A directory matched by a deny rule counts 1, whatever it holds. A directory denied only by the default is entered and counts 0. Each hidden file counts 1. `.git`, entries excluded by `.gitignore` and the search root itself count 0.
  - `list_dir` does not walk: it lists a default-denied directory by name (with its `/`) and leaves out a rule-denied one.

```rust
#[test]
fn denied_by_rule_tells_a_deny_rule_from_the_default() {
    let ws = std::path::Path::new("C:/work/repo");
    let p = custom(
        vec![
            Rule::new("Read(src/**)", Action::Allow),
            Rule::new("Read(secret/**)", Action::Deny),
            Rule::new("Read(C:/work/repo/keys/**)", Action::Deny),
            Rule::new("Read(drafts/**)", Action::Ask),
        ],
        Action::Deny,
    );
    let read = |path: &str| {
        let input = json!({ "file_path": path });
        (
            p.resolve_in("Read", &input, Some(ws)),
            p.denied_by_rule("Read", &input, Some(ws)),
        )
    };
    // A deny rule: the directory a `/**` pattern names, what is inside it, the same name deeper
    // in the tree, and an absolute pattern against a workspace-relative input.
    for path in [
        "secret",
        "secret/key.txt",
        "sub/secret",
        "keys",
        "keys/id_rsa",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(read(path), (Action::Deny, true), "{path}");
    }
    // Denied, but only by the default. `src` itself is not what the allow rule `src/**` names.
    for path in ["src", "other", "other/a.txt", "secrets"] {
        assert_eq!(read(path), (Action::Deny, false), "{path}");
    }
    // Allow and ask rules are not deny rules.
    assert_eq!(read("src/main.rs"), (Action::Allow, false));
    assert_eq!(read("drafts/a.md"), (Action::Ask, false));
    // Without a workspace only the relative deny pattern can match.
    let secret = json!({"file_path": "secret/key.txt"});
    let keys = json!({"file_path": "keys/id_rsa"});
    assert!(p.denied_by_rule("Read", &secret, None));
    assert!(!p.denied_by_rule("Read", &keys, None));
    // A rule for another tool does not count.
    assert!(!p.denied_by_rule("Glob", &json!({"path": "secret"}), Some(ws)));
}

#[test]
fn denied_by_rule_does_not_depend_on_the_default_or_on_allow_rules() {
    let secret = json!({"file_path": "secret/key.txt"});
    for &default in Action::ALL {
        let p = custom(
            vec![
                Rule::new("Read", Action::Allow),
                Rule::new("Read(secret/**)", Action::Deny),
            ],
            default,
        );
        assert!(p.denied_by_rule("Read", &secret, None), "{default:?}");
        assert_eq!(p.resolve("Read", &secret), Action::Deny, "{default:?}");
        assert!(!p.denied_by_rule("Read", &json!({"file_path": "a.txt"}), None));
    }
    // A deny rule without a path spec matches every path.
    let p = custom(vec![Rule::new("Read", Action::Deny)], Action::Allow);
    assert!(p.denied_by_rule("Read", &secret, None));
    // A relative deny rule does not match the workspace's own location.
    let ws = std::path::Path::new("C:/Users/someone/AppData/Local/dots/worktrees/01J0RUN");
    let p = custom(
        vec![Rule::new("Glob(users/**)", Action::Deny)],
        Action::Allow,
    );
    assert!(!p.denied_by_rule("Glob", &json!({"path": "src"}), Some(ws)));
    assert!(p.denied_by_rule("Glob", &json!({"path": "users"}), Some(ws)));
}

#[test]
fn denied_by_rule_agrees_with_resolve_for_every_preset() {
    let ws = Some(std::path::Path::new("C:/ws/run1"));
    let inputs = [
        json!({}),
        json!({"file_path": "src/a.rs"}),
        json!({"file_path": ".git/config"}),
        json!({"file_path": "C:/ws/run1/.claude/settings.json"}),
        json!({"path": ".git"}),
        json!({"path": "."}),
        bash("git status"),
        bash("git push origin main"),
        json!({"url": "https://example.com/"}),
    ];
    let tools = [
        "Read",
        "Write",
        "Edit",
        "NotebookEdit",
        "LS",
        "Glob",
        "Grep",
        "Bash",
        "PowerShell",
        "WebFetch",
        "mcp__files__read",
    ];
    for &preset in Preset::ALL {
        let p = Policy::preset(preset);
        for tool in tools {
            for input in &inputs {
                let action = p.resolve_in(tool, input, ws);
                let by_rule = p.denied_by_rule(tool, input, ws);
                let case = format!("{preset:?} {tool} {input}");
                // A deny rule always resolves to deny.
                assert!(!by_rule || action == Action::Deny, "{case}");
                // A deny that no rule made can only be the default.
                assert!(
                    action != Action::Deny || by_rule || p.default == Action::Deny,
                    "{case}"
                );
            }
        }
    }
    let read_only = Policy::preset(Preset::ReadOnly);
    assert!(read_only.denied_by_rule("Write", &json!({"file_path": "a.txt"}), ws));
    assert!(!read_only.denied_by_rule("WebFetch", &json!({}), ws));
    assert!(!read_only.denied_by_rule("Read", &json!({"file_path": ".git/config"}), ws));
    let sandboxed = Policy::preset(Preset::Sandboxed);
    assert!(sandboxed.denied_by_rule("Write", &json!({"file_path": ".git/config"}), ws));
    assert!(sandboxed.denied_by_rule("Edit", &json!({"file_path": ".claude"}), ws));
    assert!(!sandboxed.denied_by_rule("Read", &json!({"file_path": ".git/config"}), ws));
    assert!(!sandboxed.denied_by_rule("Write", &json!({"file_path": "src/a.rs"}), ws));
}
```

```rust
/// A custom policy that allows the walking tools everywhere, plus `rules`.
fn walking_policy(rules: Vec<Rule>, default: Action) -> Policy {
    let mut all: Vec<Rule> = ["LS", "Glob", "Grep"]
        .iter()
        .map(|t| Rule::new(t, Action::Allow))
        .collect();
    all.extend(rules);
    Policy {
        preset: Preset::Custom,
        rules: all,
        default,
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn walking_tools_reach_an_allowed_subtree_under_default_deny() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    std::fs::create_dir_all(p.join("other/inner")).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join("other/a.txt"), "fn other\n").unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join("other/inner/b.txt"), "fn inner\n").unwrap();
    let policy = walking_policy(vec![Rule::new("Read(src/**)", Action::Allow)], Action::Deny);

    // `src`, `other` and `other/inner` are denied by the default only: they are entered and not
    // counted. The three files outside `src` are denied by the default: each is hidden.
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "fn"})).await;
    assert_eq!(
        out.output,
        "src/deep/lib.rs:1: pub fn Hello() {}\nsrc/main.rs:1: fn main() {}\n\
         (3 entries hidden by policy)"
    );

    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "glob", json!({"pattern": "**/*"})).await;
    assert_eq!(
        out.output,
        "src/deep/lib.rs\nsrc/main.rs\n(3 entries hidden by policy)"
    );

    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "other/\nsrc/\n(1 entry hidden by policy)");
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({"path": "other"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "inner/\n(1 entry hidden by policy)");
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({"path": "src"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "deep/\nmain.rs");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_directory_matched_by_a_deny_rule_is_not_entered() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    std::fs::create_dir_all(p.join("secret/inner")).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join("secret/key.txt"), "hello key\n").unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join("secret/inner/more.txt"), "hello more\n").unwrap();
    // `**/secret` names the directory and nothing inside it, and `Read` is allowed: the two
    // files would be found if the walk entered `secret`.
    for &default in Action::ALL {
        let policy = walking_policy(
            vec![
                Rule::new("Read", Action::Allow),
                Rule::new("Read(**/secret)", Action::Deny),
            ],
            default,
        );
        for file in ["secret/key.txt", "secret/inner/more.txt"] {
            let input = json!({ "file_path": file });
            assert_eq!(policy.resolve_in("Read", &input, Some(p)), Action::Allow);
        }
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "hello"})).await;
        assert_eq!(
            out.output, "README.md:1: hello\n(1 entry hidden by policy)",
            "{default:?}"
        );
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "glob", json!({"pattern": "**/*.txt"})).await;
        assert_eq!(
            out.output, "no files match **/*.txt\n(1 entry hidden by policy)",
            "{default:?}"
        );
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({})).await;
        assert_eq!(
            out.output, "README.md\nsrc/\n(1 entry hidden by policy)",
            "{default:?}"
        );
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn the_hidden_count_is_one_per_denied_directory_and_one_per_hidden_file() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    for dir in ["src/secret", "other"] {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(p.join(dir)).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(p.join(dir).join("a.txt"), "fn a\n").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(p.join(dir).join("b.txt"), "fn b\n").unwrap();
    }
    std::fs::write(p.join("src/deep/key.pem"), "fn key\n").unwrap();
    let policy = walking_policy(
        vec![
            Rule::new("Read(src/**)", Action::Allow),
            Rule::new("Read(secret/**)", Action::Deny),
            Rule::new("Read(**/*.pem)", Action::Deny),
        ],
        Action::Deny,
    );
    // README.md (default): 1. `other` is entered, its two files (default): 2. `src/secret` (deny
    // rule, with two files inside): 1. src/deep/key.pem (deny rule): 1.
    let found = "src/deep/lib.rs:1: pub fn Hello() {}\nsrc/main.rs:1: fn main() {}";
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "fn"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, format!("{found}\n(5 entries hidden by policy)"));
    // Searching inside `src` counts what is hidden there only.
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "fn", "path": "src"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, format!("{found}\n(2 entries hidden by policy)"));
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({"path": "src"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "deep/\nmain.rs\n(1 entry hidden by policy)");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_entry_that_resolves_to_ask_is_not_skipped() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    std::fs::create_dir(p.join("drafts")).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join("drafts/a.txt"), "hello draft\n").unwrap();
    let policy = walking_policy(
        vec![Rule::new("Read(drafts/**)", Action::Ask)],
        Action::Deny,
    );
    // README.md and the two files under `src` are denied by the default.
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "hello"})).await;
    assert_eq!(
        out.output,
        "drafts/a.txt:1: hello draft\n(3 entries hidden by policy)"
    );
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({"path": "drafts"})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "a.txt");

    // Under default ask nothing is denied, so nothing is hidden.
    let policy = walking_policy(vec![], Action::Ask);
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "hello"})).await;
    assert_eq!(
        out.output,
        "README.md:1: hello\ndrafts/a.txt:1: hello draft"
    );
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "README.md\ndrafts/\nsrc/");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_link_to_a_directory_is_judged_as_a_file() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    dir_link(&p.join("src"), &p.join("jump"));
    let policy = walking_policy(vec![Rule::new("Read(src/**)", Action::Allow)], Action::Deny);
    // The link is not a directory to enter: the default denies it like any file. README.md is
    // the other hidden entry.
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "fn"})).await;
    assert_eq!(
        out.output,
        "src/deep/lib.rs:1: pub fn Hello() {}\nsrc/main.rs:1: fn main() {}\n\
         (2 entries hidden by policy)"
    );
    let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({})).await;
    assert_eq!(out.output, "src/\n(2 entries hidden by policy)");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn read_only_and_sandboxed_walks_skip_git_and_show_dot_claude() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    for dir in [".git", ".claude"] {
        std::fs::create_dir(p.join(dir)).unwrap();
    }
    std::fs::write(p.join(".git/config"), "hello git\n").unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.join(".claude/settings.json"), "hello claude\n").unwrap();
    for preset in [Preset::ReadOnly, Preset::Sandboxed] {
        let policy = Policy::preset(preset);
        // `.git` is never walked and is not counted; no read rule denies `.claude`.
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "grep", json!({"pattern": "hello"})).await;
        assert_eq!(
            out.output, ".claude/settings.json:1: hello claude\nREADME.md:1: hello",
            "{preset:?}"
        );
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "glob", json!({"pattern": "**/*"})).await;
        assert_eq!(
            out.output, ".claude/settings.json\nREADME.md\nsrc/deep/lib.rs\nsrc/main.rs",
            "{preset:?}"
        );
        let out = run_filtered(p, &policy, "list_dir", json!({})).await;
        assert_eq!(out.output, ".claude/\nREADME.md\nsrc/", "{preset:?}");
    }
}
```

```rust
    /// Like `resolve`, but absolute file paths inside `workspace` are treated as relative to it.
    pub fn resolve_in(&self, tool: &str, input: &Value, workspace: Option<&Path>) -> Action {
        let ws = workspace_key(workspace);
        for action in [Action::Deny, Action::Allow, Action::Ask] {
            if self.rule_matches(action, tool, input, ws.as_deref()) {
                return action;
            }
        }
        self.default
    }

    /// True when a deny rule matches the call, so `resolve_in` returns `deny` whatever the
    /// other rules and the default say. False for a call that only the default denies. The
    /// walking tools use it to tell a directory the user closed from one no rule mentions.
    pub fn denied_by_rule(&self, tool: &str, input: &Value, workspace: Option<&Path>) -> bool {
        let ws = workspace_key(workspace);
        self.rule_matches(Action::Deny, tool, input, ws.as_deref())
    }

    /// True if a rule with this `action` matches the call. `ws` comes from `workspace_key`.
    fn rule_matches(&self, action: Action, tool: &str, input: &Value, ws: Option<&str>) -> bool {
        self.rules
            .iter()
            .any(|r| r.action == action && pattern_matches(&r.tool, tool, input, action, ws))
    }
```

```rust
/// The normalized workspace path that file rules are matched against; `None` unless absolute.
fn workspace_key(workspace: Option<&Path>) -> Option<String> {
    let (abs, normalized) = lexical_normalize(&workspace?.to_string_lossy());
    abs.then_some(normalized)
}
```

```rust

/// Decides which entries the walking tools (`list_dir`, `glob`, `grep`) leave out, given each
/// entry's workspace-relative path. The default hides nothing.
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct PathFilter(Option<Arc<HideFn>>);

type HideFn = dyn Fn(&str) -> bool + Send + Sync;

impl PathFilter {
    pub fn new(hides: impl Fn(&str) -> bool + Send + Sync + 'static) -> Self {
        Self(Some(Arc::new(hides)))
    }

    /// Hides the entries `policy` denies for the walking tool `alias` or for `Read`, so a
    /// search from the workspace root never reads a denied subtree.
    pub fn from_policy(policy: &Policy, alias: &'static str, workspace: &Path) -> Self {
        let (policy, workspace) = (policy.clone(), workspace.to_path_buf());
        Self::new(move |rel| {
            let ws = Some(workspace.as_path());
            policy.resolve_in(alias, &json!({ "path": rel }), ws) == Action::Deny
                || policy.resolve_in("Read", &json!({ "file_path": rel }), ws) == Action::Deny
        })
    }

    pub fn hides(&self, rel: &str) -> bool {
        self.0.as_ref().is_some_and(|f| f(rel))
    }
}
```

with:

```rust

/// Decides which entries the walking tools (`list_dir`, `glob`, `grep`) leave out, given each
/// entry's workspace-relative path and whether it is a directory. The default hides nothing.
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct PathFilter(Option<Arc<HideFn>>);

type HideFn = dyn Fn(&str, bool) -> bool + Send + Sync;

impl PathFilter {
    pub fn new(hides: impl Fn(&str, bool) -> bool + Send + Sync + 'static) -> Self {
        Self(Some(Arc::new(hides)))
    }

    /// Hides what `policy` denies for the walking tool `alias` or for `Read`. A directory is
    /// hidden only when a deny rule matches it, so a search from the workspace root never
    /// reads a subtree a rule denies, and still reaches an allowed subtree when the default is
    /// `deny`. Every other entry is hidden whenever it resolves to `deny`, by rule or by default.
    pub fn from_policy(policy: &Policy, alias: &'static str, workspace: &Path) -> Self {
        let (policy, workspace) = (policy.clone(), workspace.to_path_buf());
        Self::new(move |rel, is_dir| {
            let ws = Some(workspace.as_path());
            let denied = |tool: &str, input: Value| {
                if is_dir {
                    policy.denied_by_rule(tool, &input, ws)
                } else {
                    policy.resolve_in(tool, &input, ws) == Action::Deny
                }
            };
            denied(alias, json!({ "path": rel })) || denied("Read", json!({ "file_path": rel }))
        })
    }

    /// `is_dir` is true only for a real directory: a link to one is judged as a file.
    pub fn hides(&self, rel: &str, is_dir: bool) -> bool {
        self.0.as_ref().is_some_and(|f| f(rel, is_dir))
    }
}
```

---

### Task 3: Review actions check the run row, follow no link, and leave no folder behind

A run row holds a worktree path and a branch name, and the review actions run git on them. `discard_worktree` checks the row first; `worktree_diff` does not. The diff also reads every untracked file, including what lies behind a link or a junction. This task gives both actions one check, lists links without following them, and fixes four defects around worktrees that the reviews of Plans 3a and 3b found.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/workspace.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/runtime.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/runner.rs`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/workspace_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/worktree_review_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/resume_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/runner_test.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `workspace::git_raw`, `WorktreeDiff.exists` (Plan 3b); `WorkspaceManager`, `Prepared` (Plan 1).
- Produces:
  - `WorkspaceManager::check_owned(&self, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<()>`: the row passes only when its path is a direct child of the manager's root (`<data dir>/worktrees`: one normal path component, not the root, not deeper, not through `..`) and its branch is `dots/<something>/<that folder's name>`. It reads text only, no disk and no git. Refusals are `Invalid("refusing to use <path>: not a dots worktree")` and `Invalid("refusing to use branch <branch>: not the dots branch of worktree <folder>")`.
  - `Runtime::worktree_diff` and `Runtime::discard_worktree` both run that check before any git call (through the private `Runtime::review_worktree`). The answers of `worktree_diff`, in order: unknown run `NotFound`; a folder-mode run or a row without path or base commit `Invalid("run <id> has no worktree")`; a row that fails the check `Invalid`; a row that passes with its folder gone `WorktreeDiff { files: [], truncated: false, exists: false }` as before; otherwise the diff with `exists: true`. The two refusal texts of discard change to the ones above.
  - `workspace::link_on_path(root: &Path, rel: &str) -> Option<String>`: the shortest leading part of `rel` that is a symlink or a junction. `workspace::untracked_entry(root: &Path, rel: &str, link: bool) -> (FileDiff, bool)`: the diff entry of one untracked path; with `link` true the path is not opened.
  - `workspace::diff` lists an untracked link once, under its own path, as `added`, with the patch git itself would show for a link (`new file mode 120000` and one line with the target, read with `read_link`). Nothing behind a link is opened, and the files git names behind a junction are dropped.
  - The untracked list is read with `git_raw`, so a file name that begins with a space keeps it.
  - `cancel_inactive` removes an unchanged worktree before it publishes `cancelled`, like `execute_inner` does.
  - The `RunUpdated` that announces a run's folder and branch is best effort: a failed read is logged (`announcing the workspace failed: …`) and the run goes on.
  - `WorkspaceManager::discard` finds the repository from the worktree itself (`git -C <worktree> rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`) and uses the dot's current `workdir` only when the worktree cannot answer.
  - After git has removed a worktree, its folder is gone. `git worktree remove --force` leaves a junction where it is and still exits 0. The private `WorkspaceManager::remove_worktree`, used by `remove`, `cleanup_if_unchanged` and `discard`, then runs `check_owned` and deletes what is left with `tokio::fs::remove_dir_all`, which removes a junction as the link and leaves its target. When that fails the action fails with `Other("could not remove what git left of worktree <path>: <os error>")` and the thread is not marked reviewed.
  - `Runtime::inbox()` is not changed.

Creating a symlink on Windows needs a privilege or developer mode. The test with a real symlink prints `SKIPPED an_untracked_symlink_is_listed_and_its_target_is_not_read: …` when the OS refuses; the junction tests need no privilege and always run.

```rust
mod common;

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use common::{fake_dot, git_out, init_repo};
use dots_core::model::{FileStatus, WorkspaceMode};
use dots_core::workspace::{diff, link_on_path, untracked_entry, Prepared, WorkspaceManager};
use dots_core::Error;

/// A worktree of a new repository under `tmp`, and a folder outside it that holds `secret.txt`.
async fn worktree_and_outside(tmp: &Path) -> (Prepared, PathBuf) {
    let repo = tmp.join("repo");
    init_repo(&repo);
    let dot = fake_dot("links", &repo, WorkspaceMode::Worktree);
    let p = WorkspaceManager::new(tmp.join("wt"))
        .prepare(&dot, "R1")
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let outside = tmp.join("outside");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&outside).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(outside.join("secret.txt"), "TOP-SECRET\n").unwrap();
    (p, outside)
}

/// Makes `link` a junction to the folder `target`. A junction needs no privilege, so the tests
/// that use one always run on Windows.
#[cfg(windows)]
fn make_junction(link: &Path, target: &Path) {
    let made = std::process::Command::new("cmd")
        .args(["/c", "mklink", "/J"])
        .arg(link)
        .arg(target)
        .output()
        .unwrap();
    assert!(
        made.status.success(),
        "mklink /J failed: {}",
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&made.stderr)
    );
}

/// Says on the terminal that a test did not run. Written straight to stderr, which the test
/// harness does not capture, so the line shows in a passing `cargo test`.
fn report_skipped(test: &str, why: &dyn std::fmt::Display) {
    use std::io::Write;
    let _ = writeln!(std::io::stderr(), "SKIPPED {test}: {why}");
}

        .has_changes(&committed.path, committed.base_commit.as_deref().unwrap())

#[tokio::test]
async fn restore_recreates_a_removed_worktree_at_the_same_path() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let repo = tmp.path().join("repo");
    init_repo(&repo);
    let dot = fake_dot("again", &repo, WorkspaceMode::Worktree);
    let wm = WorkspaceManager::new(tmp.path().join("wt"));

    // Branch deleted by cleanup: recreated from the base commit.
    let p = wm.prepare(&dot, "R1").await.unwrap();
    assert!(wm.cleanup_if_unchanged(&dot, &p).await.unwrap());
    assert!(!p.path.exists());
    let restored = wm.restore(&dot, &p).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(restored, p);
    assert!(p.path.join("README.md").is_file());
    assert_eq!(
        git_out(&p.path, &["rev-parse", "--abbrev-ref", "HEAD"]),
        "dots/again/R1"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        git_out(&p.path, &["rev-parse", "HEAD"]),
        p.base_commit.clone().unwrap()
    );

    // Directory deleted behind git's back, branch still there (with a commit): reused.
    std::fs::write(p.path.join("c.txt"), "y").unwrap();
    git_out(&p.path, &["add", "."]);
    git_out(&p.path, &["commit", "-q", "-m", "work"]);
    let tip = git_out(&p.path, &["rev-parse", "HEAD"]);
    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&p.path).unwrap();
    wm.restore(&dot, &p).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(git_out(&p.path, &["rev-parse", "HEAD"]), tip);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_untracked_name_keeps_its_leading_space() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let (p, _outside) = worktree_and_outside(tmp.path()).await;
    // A space sorts before every other name, so this is the first path git lists.
    std::fs::write(p.path.join(" lead.txt"), "x\n").unwrap();
    std::fs::write(p.path.join("next.txt"), "y\n").unwrap();

    let d = diff(&p.path, p.base_commit.as_deref().unwrap())
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let paths: Vec<&str> = d.files.iter().map(|f| f.path.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(paths, [" lead.txt", "next.txt"]);
    assert!(
        d.files[0].patch.ends_with("@@ -0,0 +1,1 @@\n+x\n"),
        "{:?}",
        d.files[0].patch
    );
}

/// A junction needs no privilege, so this test always runs on Windows. Git walks into a
/// junction and reports the files behind it; the diff must show the junction and stop there.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn an_untracked_junction_is_listed_once_and_not_entered() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let (p, outside) = worktree_and_outside(tmp.path()).await;
    make_junction(&p.path.join("junc"), &outside);

    let d = diff(&p.path, p.base_commit.as_deref().unwrap())
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let paths: Vec<&str> = d.files.iter().map(|f| f.path.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(paths, ["junc"]);
    let link = &d.files[0];
    assert_eq!((link.status, link.binary), (FileStatus::Added, false));
    // Shown as git shows a link: the place it points to, never what is there.
    assert!(
        link.patch.contains("new file mode 120000"),
        "{}",
        link.patch
    );
    assert!(link.patch.contains("outside"), "{}", link.patch);
    assert!(!link.patch.contains("TOP-SECRET"), "{}", link.patch);
    assert!(!d.truncated);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_untracked_symlink_is_listed_and_its_target_is_not_read() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let (p, outside) = worktree_and_outside(tmp.path()).await;
    let target = outside.join("secret.txt");
    let link = p.path.join("link.txt");
    #[cfg(windows)]
    let made = std::os::windows::fs::symlink_file(&target, &link);
    #[cfg(unix)]
    let made = std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&target, &link);
    if let Err(e) = made {
        // Windows without developer mode or the symlink privilege (error 1314). Any other
        // failure is a broken test, and neither may pass silently.
        assert_eq!(e.raw_os_error(), Some(1314), "creating a symlink: {e}");
        report_skipped(
            "an_untracked_symlink_is_listed_and_its_target_is_not_read",
            &e,
        );
        return;
    }

    let d = diff(&p.path, p.base_commit.as_deref().unwrap())
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let paths: Vec<&str> = d.files.iter().map(|f| f.path.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(paths, ["link.txt"]);
    let shown = &d.files[0];
    assert_eq!((shown.status, shown.binary), (FileStatus::Added, false));
    assert!(
        shown.patch.contains("new file mode 120000"),
        "{}",
        shown.patch
    );
    assert!(shown.patch.contains("secret.txt"), "{}", shown.patch);
    assert!(!shown.patch.contains("TOP-SECRET"), "{}", shown.patch);
}

/// The decision itself, without a real link: a path the diff is told is a link is listed and
/// never opened, whatever is behind it.
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_path_marked_as_a_link_is_listed_without_being_opened() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    std::fs::write(tmp.path().join("note.txt"), "TOP-SECRET\n").unwrap();

    let (opened, _) = untracked_entry(tmp.path(), "note.txt", false).await;
    assert!(opened.patch.ends_with("+TOP-SECRET\n"), "{}", opened.patch);

    let (listed, truncated) = untracked_entry(tmp.path(), "note.txt", true).await;
    assert_eq!(
        (listed.path.as_str(), listed.status, listed.binary),
        ("note.txt", FileStatus::Added, false)
    );
    // Not a real link, so there is no target to show; the file itself was not read.
    assert_eq!(listed.patch, "");
    assert!(!truncated);

    // Ordinary files and folders, and paths that are not there, are not links.
    std::fs::create_dir(tmp.path().join("sub")).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(tmp.path().join("sub").join("a.txt"), "a").unwrap();
    for rel in ["note.txt", "sub/a.txt", "missing/b.txt"] {
        assert_eq!(link_on_path(tmp.path(), rel), None, "{rel}");
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[test]
fn a_junction_above_a_path_is_found_as_the_link() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let outside = tmp.path().join("outside");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(outside.join("deep")).unwrap();
    let root = tmp.path().join("root");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(root.join("sub")).unwrap();
    make_junction(&root.join("sub").join("junc"), &outside);
    for rel in ["sub/junc", "sub/junc/deep", "sub/junc/deep/x.txt"] {
        assert_eq!(

            Some("sub/junc"),
            "{rel}"
        );
    }
    assert_eq!(link_on_path(&root, "sub/other.txt"), None);
}

#[test]
fn only_a_direct_child_of_the_worktrees_folder_with_its_own_branch_is_a_dots_worktree() {
    let wm = WorkspaceManager::new("C:/data/worktrees");
    let check = |path: &str, branch: Option<&str>| {
        wm.check_owned(&Prepared {
            path: PathBuf::from(path),
            branch: branch.map(str::to_string),
            base_commit: None,
        })
    };
    check("C:/data/worktrees/R1", Some("dots/triage/R1")).unwrap();
    // A trailing separator names the same folder.
    check("C:/data/worktrees/R1/", Some("dots/triage/R1")).unwrap();

    // Each branch below fits its path's last name, so only the path can be the reason.
    for (path, branch) in [
        ("C:/data/worktrees", "dots/triage/worktrees"),
        ("C:/data/worktrees/R1/sub", "dots/triage/sub"),
        ("C:/data/worktrees/../R1", "dots/triage/R1"),
        ("C:/data/worktrees/R2/../R1", "dots/triage/R1"),
        ("C:/data/worktrees/R1/..", "dots/triage/R1"),
        ("C:/data/worktrees-old/R1", "dots/triage/R1"),
        ("C:/data/R1", "dots/triage/R1"),
        ("C:/repo", "dots/triage/repo"),
        ("worktrees/R1", "dots/triage/R1"),
    ] {
        let err = check(path, Some(branch)).unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "{path}: {err}");
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("not a dots worktree"), "{err}");
    }
    for branch in [
        "feature-x",
        "main",
        "dots/triage/R2",
        "dots/R1",
        "dots//R1",
        "x/dots/triage/R1",
        "dots/triage/R1/",
        "",
    ] {
        let err = check("C:/data/worktrees/R1", Some(branch)).unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "{branch}: {err}");
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("not the dots branch"), "{err}");
    }
    let err = check("C:/data/worktrees/R1", None).unwrap_err();
    assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "{err}");
}

/// success. What is left is removed as links, never through them.

    std::fs::write(p.path.join("new.txt"), "x").unwrap();

    let wm = WorkspaceManager::new(tmp.path().join("wt"));
    wm.remove(&tmp.path().join("repo"), &p).await.unwrap();

    assert_eq!(
        std::fs::read_to_string(outside.join("secret.txt")).unwrap(),
        "TOP-SECRET
"
    );
    assert!(git_out(
        &tmp.path().join("repo"),
        &["branch", "--list", "dots/links/R1"]
    )
    .is_empty());
}

/// What git leaves is only ever deleted inside the manager's own folder; anywhere else the
/// removal fails and says which folder is still there.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_leftover_outside_the_worktrees_folder_is_reported_and_not_deleted() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let (p, outside) = worktree_and_outside(tmp.path()).await;
    make_junction(&p.path.join("junc"), &outside);

    let elsewhere = WorkspaceManager::new(tmp.path().join("elsewhere"));
    let err = elsewhere
        .remove(&tmp.path().join("repo"), &p)
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "{err}");
    assert!(
        err.to_string().contains(&p.path.display().to_string()),
        "{err}"
    );
    assert!(p.path.join("junc").join("secret.txt").is_file());
}

/// A leftover that cannot be deleted is an error that names the folder, never a silent success.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_leftover_that_cannot_be_removed_fails_and_names_the_folder() {
    use std::os::windows::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
    // FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS | FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT: open the junction itself.
    const THE_LINK_ITSELF: u32 = 0x0200_0000 | 0x0020_0000;
    const FILE_SHARE_READ: u32 = 1;
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let (p, outside) = worktree_and_outside(tmp.path()).await;
    make_junction(&p.path.join("junc"), &outside);
    // Held open without sharing delete, the junction cannot be removed.
    let held = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
        .read(true)
        .share_mode(FILE_SHARE_READ)
        .custom_flags(THE_LINK_ITSELF)
        .open(p.path.join("junc"))
        .unwrap();

    let wm = WorkspaceManager::new(tmp.path().join("wt"));
    let err = wm.remove(&tmp.path().join("repo"), &p).await.unwrap_err();
    let text = err.to_string();
    assert!(text.contains("could not remove what git left of"), "{text}");
    assert!(text.contains(&p.path.display().to_string()), "{text}");
    assert!(p.path.join("junc").exists());
    drop(held);
    assert!(outside.join("secret.txt").is_file());
}
```

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn diff_and_discard_refuse_a_path_that_is_not_a_direct_child_of_the_worktrees_folder() {
    let t = start().await;
    let run = edited_run(&t).await;
    let ws = PathBuf::from(run.workspace_path.as_deref().unwrap());
    let branch = run.branch.clone().unwrap();
    let worktrees = ws.parent().unwrap().to_path_buf();
    let name = ws.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy().to_string();
    std::fs::create_dir(ws.join("sub")).unwrap();
    let tampered = [
        // The user's own checkout.
        t.repo.clone(),
        // The worktrees folder itself, a folder deeper inside it, and a way round through `..`.
        worktrees.clone(),
        ws.join("sub"),
        worktrees.join("elsewhere").join("..").join(&name),
        // Outside and not on disk: still refused, not reported as a removed worktree.
        t.dir.path().join("nowhere"),
    ];
    for path in &tampered {
        let path = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
        tamper(&t, &run.id, "workspace_path", &path).await;
        let err = t.rt.worktree_diff(&run.id).await.unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "diff of {path}: {err}");
        let err = t.rt.discard_worktree(&run.id).await.unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, Error::Invalid(_)), "discard of {path}: {err}");
        assert!(t.repo.join("README.md").is_file());
        assert!(ws.join("new.txt").is_file() && branch_exists(&t.repo, &branch));
    }
    assert!(t.rt.get_run(&run.id).await.unwrap().reviewed_at.is_none());

    // The true row still works, and a worktree folder that is simply gone is not an error.
    tamper(&t, &run.id, "workspace_path", &ws.to_string_lossy()).await;
    assert!(t.rt.worktree_diff(&run.id).await.unwrap().exists);
    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&ws).unwrap();
    let gone = t.rt.worktree_diff(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(!gone.exists && gone.files.is_empty() && !gone.truncated);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```
