```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn discard_finds_the_repository_from_the_worktree_when_the_dot_moved() {
    let t = start().await;
    let run = edited_run(&t).await;
    let ws = PathBuf::from(run.workspace_path.as_deref().unwrap());
    let branch = run.branch.clone().unwrap();
    // After the run, the dot is pointed at another repository.
    let other = t.dir.path().join("other");
    init_repo(&other);
    let mut spec = t.rt.get_dot(&run.dot_id).await.unwrap().spec;
    spec.workdir = other.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    t.rt.update_dot(&run.dot_id, spec).await.unwrap();

    let after = t.rt.discard_worktree(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(after.reviewed_at.is_some());
    assert!(!ws.exists());
    assert!(!branch_exists(&t.repo, &branch));
    // The repository the worktree came from no longer lists it.
    let listed = git_out(&t.repo, &["worktree", "list", "--porcelain"]);
    assert_eq!(listed.matches("worktree ").count(), 1, "{listed}");
    assert_eq!(git_out(&other, &["branch", "--list"]), "* main");
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

/// A folder outside every worktree and repository, holding `keep.txt`.
#[cfg(windows)]
fn outside_folder(t: &Rt) -> PathBuf {
    let outside = t.dir.path().join("outside");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&outside).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(outside.join("keep.txt"), "keep me\n").unwrap();
    outside
}

/// Git removes a worktree but leaves a junction inside it where it is, and still reports
/// success. A junction needs no privilege, so these tests always run on Windows.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn discarding_a_worktree_with_a_junction_removes_the_folder_and_spares_the_target() {
    let t = start().await;
    let run = edited_run(&t).await;
    let ws = PathBuf::from(run.workspace_path.as_deref().unwrap());
    let branch = run.branch.clone().unwrap();
    let outside = outside_folder(&t);
    let made = std::process::Command::new("cmd")
        .args(["/c", "mklink", "/J"])
        .arg(ws.join("junc"))
        .arg(&outside)
        .output()
        .unwrap();
    assert!(made.status.success());
    assert!(ws.join("junc").join("keep.txt").is_file());

    let after = t.rt.discard_worktree(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(after.reviewed_at.is_some());
    assert!(!ws.exists(), "the worktree folder was left behind");
    assert!(!branch_exists(&t.repo, &branch));
    // Only the junction went: what it pointed to is as it was.
    assert_eq!(
        std::fs::read_to_string(outside.join("keep.txt")).unwrap(),
        "keep me\n"
    );
    // The removed worktree reads as removed, and a second discard is still harmless.
    let gone = t.rt.worktree_diff(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(!gone.exists && gone.files.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(t.rt.inbox_count().await.unwrap(), 0);
    t.rt.discard_worktree(&run.id).await.unwrap();
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

/// What pnpm makes of `node_modules`: a junction the repository ignores. The run changed
/// nothing git can see, so its worktree is cleaned up when it ends.
#[cfg(windows)]
#[tokio::test]
async fn an_unchanged_run_with_an_ignored_junction_is_cleaned_up_and_stays_out_of_the_inbox() {
    let t = start().await;
    std::fs::write(t.repo.join(".gitignore"), "node_modules\n").unwrap();
    git_out(&t.repo, &["add", ".gitignore"]);
    git_out(&t.repo, &["commit", "-q", "-m", "ignore node_modules"]);
    let outside = outside_folder(&t);
    let link = format!("cmd /c mklink /J node_modules '{}'", outside.display());
    let llm = Llm::start(vec![
        call("c1", "shell", json!({ "command": link })),
        text("Linked."),
    ])
    .await;
    let mut s = spec("linker");
    s.workdir = t.repo.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    s.workspace_mode = WorkspaceMode::Worktree;
    s.engine = dots_core::model::EngineKind::Local;
    s.endpoint_url = Some(llm.endpoint());
    s.model = "m".into();
    s.policy = Policy::preset(Preset::Trusted);
    let dot = t.rt.create_dot(s).await.unwrap();
    let run = t.rt.run_now(&dot.id).await.unwrap();
    let run = wait_status(t.rt.store(), &run.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 30).await;

    // The dot did make the junction.
    let events = t.rt.list_run_events(&run.id, 0).await.unwrap();
    let result = events.iter().find(|e| e.kind == "tool_result").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(result.data["is_error"], json!(false), "{}", result.data);
    assert!(
        result.data["output"]
            .as_str()
            .unwrap()
            .contains("Junction created"),
        "{}",
        result.data
    );

    let ws = PathBuf::from(run.workspace_path.as_deref().unwrap());
    assert!(!ws.exists(), "the worktree folder was left behind");
    assert!(!branch_exists(&t.repo, run.branch.as_deref().unwrap()));
    assert_eq!(t.rt.inbox_count().await.unwrap(), 0);
    assert_eq!(
        std::fs::read_to_string(outside.join("keep.txt")).unwrap(),
        "keep me\n"
    );
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn cancel_of_awaiting_run_publishes_cancelled_after_the_worktree_is_gone() {
    let e = env(vec![ask("git push"), finished()]).await;
    let (parent, path) = parked_worktree_run(&e).await;
    let mut events = e.bus.subscribe();
    let id = parent.id.clone();
    // Looks at the folder the moment the terminal status arrives, as the Inbox does.
    let seen = tokio::spawn(async move {
        loop {
            match events.recv().await {
                Ok(RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated { run })
                    if run.id == id && run.status == RunStatus::Cancelled =>
                {
                    return std::path::Path::new(&path).exists();
                }
                Ok(_) => {}
                Err(err) => panic!("the event stream ended: {err}"),
            }
        }
    });
    e.runner.cancel(&parent.id).await.unwrap();
    let still_there = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), seen)
        .await
        .expect("no cancelled status was published")
        .unwrap();
    assert!(
        !still_there,
        "cancelled was published while the unchanged worktree was still on disk"
    );
}
```

```rust
/// Telling the UI about a run's new workspace needs one read of the run row. Two triggers make
/// exactly that read fail: the row cannot be decoded from the moment its workspace is stored
/// until its status next changes.
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_failed_read_for_the_workspace_announcement_does_not_fail_the_run() {
    let (dir, store) = temp_store().await;
    let pool = sqlx::SqlitePool::connect(&format!(
        "sqlite://{}",
        dir.path().join("dots.db").display()
    ))
    .await
    .unwrap();
    for sql in [
        "CREATE TRIGGER break_row AFTER UPDATE OF workspace_path ON runs \
         BEGIN UPDATE runs SET payload = '{' WHERE id = NEW.id; END",
        "CREATE TRIGGER mend_row AFTER UPDATE OF status ON runs \
         BEGIN UPDATE runs SET payload = NULL WHERE id = NEW.id; END",
    ] {
        sqlx::query(sql).execute(&pool).await.unwrap();
    }
    pool.close().await;
    let bus = new_bus();
    let runner = runner_with(
        &store,
        &bus,
        &dir,
        Arc::new(ScriptedEngine::new(vec![finished()])),
        1,
    );
    let mut events = bus.subscribe();
    runner.spawn_dispatcher(CancellationToken::new());
    let repo = dir.path().join("repo");
    init_repo(&repo);
    let mut s = spec("wt");
    s.workdir = repo.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let dot = store.create_dot(&s).await.unwrap();
    let id = manual(&runner, &dot).await;

    // The row is unreadable for a moment, so `wait_status` cannot be used here.
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
    let run = loop {
        match store.get_run(&id).await {
            Ok(run) if run.status.is_terminal() => break run,
            _ => {}
        }
        assert!(
            std::time::Instant::now() < deadline,
            "the run never finished"
        );
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
    };
    assert_eq!(
        (run.status, run.error.as_deref()),
        (RunStatus::Succeeded, None)
    );
    assert!(run.branch.is_some());
    // The fault did hit the announcement: no update of the running run carried its branch.
    while let Ok(event) = events.try_recv() {
        if let RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated { run } = event {
            assert!(
                !(run.status == RunStatus::Running && run.branch.is_some()),
                "the announcement was read after all"
            );
        }
    }
}
```

```rust
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};

use tokio::process::Command;

use crate::model::{Dot, FileDiff, FileStatus, Run, WorkspaceMode, WorktreeDiff};
use crate::{Error, Result};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Prepared {
    pub path: PathBuf,
    pub branch: Option<String>,
    pub base_commit: Option<String>,
}

impl Prepared {
    pub fn from_run(run: &Run) -> Option<Prepared> {
        Some(Prepared {
            path: PathBuf::from(run.workspace_path.as_ref()?),
            branch: run.branch.clone(),
            base_commit: run.base_commit.clone(),
        })
    }
}

/// Runs `git -C <dir> <args>` and returns trimmed stdout.
pub async fn git(dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> Result<String> {
    Ok(git_raw(dir, args).await?.trim().to_string())
}

/// Like [`git`], but returns stdout exactly as git wrote it. A patch needs this: its last line
/// can be a blank context line (a single space), which trimming would drop.
pub async fn git_raw(dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> Result<String> {
    let mut cmd = Command::new("git");
    cmd.arg("-C").arg(dir).args(args).kill_on_drop(true);
    crate::proc::hide_window(&mut cmd);
    let out = cmd.output().await?;
    if !out.status.success() {
        return Err(Error::Other(format!(
            "git {} failed: {}",
            args.join(" "),
            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr).trim()
        )));
    }
    Ok(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).into_owned())
}

/// The repository a worktree belongs to, as the worktree itself records it: the git directory
/// its repository's worktrees share, in which `git -C` runs worktree and branch commands as it
/// does in the checkout. `None` when the folder is gone or is not a worktree.
async fn repository_of(worktree: &Path) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let args = ["rev-parse", "--path-format=absolute", "--git-common-dir"];
    let dir = git(worktree, &args).await.ok()?;
    (!dir.is_empty()).then(|| PathBuf::from(dir))
}

/// Total patch text a diff returns; later files are listed without their patch.
pub const MAX_DIFF_BYTES: usize = 1024 * 1024;
/// Files with a NUL byte in their first 8000 bytes are treated as binary (as git does).
const BINARY_PROBE: usize = 8000;

/// Splits `git diff` output into one entry per file.
pub fn parse_diff(text: &str) -> Vec<FileDiff> {
    let mut files: Vec<FileDiff> = Vec::new();
    for line in text.split_inclusive('\n') {
        if let Some(header) = line.strip_prefix("diff --git ") {
            let header = header.trim_end();
            let path = header
                .rsplit_once(" b/")
                .map_or(header, |(_, b)| b)
                .to_string();
            files.push(FileDiff {
                path,
                status: FileStatus::Modified,
                patch: String::new(),
                binary: false,
            });
        }
        let Some(file) = files.last_mut() else {
            continue;
        };
        file.patch.push_str(line);
        let line = line.trim_end_matches(['\r', '\n']);
        if line.starts_with("new file mode") {
            file.status = FileStatus::Added;
        } else if line.starts_with("deleted file mode") {
            file.status = FileStatus::Deleted;
        } else if let Some(to) = line.strip_prefix("rename to ") {
            file.status = FileStatus::Renamed;
            file.path = to.to_string();
        } else if line.starts_with("Binary files ") {
            file.binary = true;
        } else if let Some(p) = line.strip_prefix("+++ b/") {
            // git appends one TAB to header paths that contain a space.
            file.path = p.strip_suffix('\t').unwrap_or(p).to_string();
        }
    }
    for f in files.iter_mut().filter(|f| f.binary) {
        f.patch.clear();
    }
    files
}

/// A new-file patch for an untracked path with git file mode `mode`; `None` when it is binary.
fn untracked_patch(rel: &str, mode: &str, bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<String> {
    if bytes.iter().take(BINARY_PROBE).any(|b| *b == 0) {
        return None;
    }
    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(bytes);
    let mut patch =
        format!("diff --git a/{rel} b/{rel}\nnew file mode {mode}\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/{rel}\n");
    let lines: Vec<&str> = text.lines().collect();
    if !lines.is_empty() {
        patch.push_str(&format!("@@ -0,0 +1,{} @@\n", lines.len()));
        for l in &lines {
            patch.push('+');
            patch.push_str(l);
            patch.push('\n');
        }
        if !text.ends_with('\n') {
            patch.push_str("\\ No newline at end of file");
        }
    }
    Some(patch)
}

/// The first part of `rel` (the whole of it included) that is a symlink or a junction, if any.
/// On Windows `git ls-files` walks into a junction and reports the files behind it, so the link
/// can sit above the path git names. Only the entries themselves are examined, never what they
/// point to.
pub fn link_on_path(root: &Path, rel: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let ends = rel.match_indices('/').map(|(i, _)| i).chain([rel.len()]);
    for end in ends {
        let part = &rel[..end];
        match std::fs::symlink_metadata(root.join(part)) {
            Ok(meta) if meta.file_type().is_symlink() => return Some(part.to_string()),
            Ok(_) => {}
            // Not there (any more): nothing below it can be read either.
            Err(_) => return None,
        }
    }
    None
}

/// One untracked path as the diff lists it: always an addition. `link` says the path is a
/// symlink or a junction; it is then shown as git shows a link (mode 120000 and the place it
/// points to) and never opened, because that place can be outside the worktree. The flag is
/// true when the file is too large to show.
pub async fn untracked_entry(root: &Path, rel: &str, link: bool) -> (FileDiff, bool) {
    let full = root.join(rel);
    let mut truncated = false;
    let (patch, binary) = if link {
        let patch = std::fs::read_link(&full)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|to| untracked_patch(rel, "120000", to.to_string_lossy().as_bytes()));
        (patch.unwrap_or_default(), false)
    } else if std::fs::metadata(&full).map_or(0, |m| m.len()) > MAX_DIFF_BYTES as u64 {
        truncated = true;
        (String::new(), false)
    } else {
        match tokio::fs::read(&full).await {
            Ok(bytes) => match untracked_patch(rel, "100644", &bytes) {
                Some(p) => (p, false),
                None => (String::new(), true),
            },
            Err(_) => (String::new(), false),
        }
    };
    let file = FileDiff {
        path: rel.to_string(),
        status: FileStatus::Added,
        patch,
        binary,
    };
    (file, truncated)
}

/// The changes of the worktree at `path` against `base_commit`: committed and uncommitted
/// changes to tracked files, then untracked files (not ignored) as additions. An untracked
/// symlink or junction is listed and not followed. Patch text is capped at `MAX_DIFF_BYTES`;
/// files past the cap are listed with an empty patch.
pub async fn diff(path: &Path, base_commit: &str) -> Result<WorktreeDiff> {
    let tracked = git_raw(
        path,
        &[
            "-c",
            "core.quotePath=false",
            "diff",
            "--no-color",
            "--no-ext-diff",
            "--no-textconv",
            "--find-renames",
            base_commit,
            "--",
        ],
    )
    .await?;
    let mut files = parse_diff(&tracked);
    // Untrimmed: the names are separated by NUL, and a name may begin with a space.
    let listed = git_raw(
        path,
        &[
            "-c",
            "core.quotePath=false",
            "ls-files",
            "--others",
            "--exclude-standard",
            "-z",
        ],
    )
    .await?;
    // A link is listed once, under its own path, however many paths git reported through it.
    let mut untracked: BTreeMap<String, bool> = BTreeMap::new();
    for rel in listed.split('\0').filter(|p| !p.is_empty()) {
        match link_on_path(path, rel) {
            Some(link) => untracked.insert(link, true),
            None => untracked.insert(rel.to_string(), false),
        };
    }
    let mut truncated = false;
    for (rel, link) in &untracked {
        let (file, cut) = untracked_entry(path, rel, *link).await;
        truncated |= cut;
        files.push(file);
    }
    files.sort_by(|a, b| a.path.cmp(&b.path));
    let mut used = 0;
    for f in &mut files {
        if used + f.patch.len() > MAX_DIFF_BYTES {
            f.patch.clear();
            truncated = true;
        }
        used += f.patch.len();
    }
    Ok(WorktreeDiff {
        files,
        truncated,
        exists: true,
    })
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct WorkspaceManager {
    root: PathBuf,
}

impl WorkspaceManager {
    pub fn new(root: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self { root: root.into() }
    }

    pub async fn prepare(&self, dot: &Dot, run_id: &str) -> Result<Prepared> {
        let workdir = PathBuf::from(&dot.spec.workdir);
        if !workdir.is_dir() {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "workdir does not exist: {}",
                workdir.display()
            )));
        }
        if dot.spec.workspace_mode == WorkspaceMode::Folder {
            return Ok(Prepared {
                path: workdir,
                branch: None,
                base_commit: None,
            });
        }
        let inside = git(&workdir, &["rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree"])
            .await
            .unwrap_or_default();
        if inside != "true" {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "workdir is not a git repository (required for worktree mode): {}",
                workdir.display()
            )));
        }
        let start = match git(
            &workdir,
            &[
                "symbolic-ref",
                "--quiet",
                "--short",
                "refs/remotes/origin/HEAD",
            ],
        )
        .await
        {
            Ok(r) if !r.is_empty() => r,
            _ => "HEAD".to_string(),
        };
        let base = git(&workdir, &["rev-parse", &start]).await?;
        tokio::fs::create_dir_all(&self.root).await?;
        let path = self.root.join(run_id);
        let branch = format!("dots/{}/{}", dot.spec.name, run_id);
        let path_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
        if let Err(e) = git(
            &workdir,
            &["worktree", "add", "-b", &branch, &path_str, &base],
        )
        .await
        {
            let _ = tokio::fs::remove_dir_all(&path).await;
            return Err(e);
        }
        Ok(Prepared {
            path,
            branch: Some(branch),
            base_commit: Some(base),
        })
    }

    /// Recreates a removed worktree at its original path so a resumed session finds the
    /// directory it remembers. Reuses the branch if it still exists, otherwise recreates it
    /// from `base_commit`. Returns `prepared` unchanged (same path, branch and base).
    pub async fn restore(&self, dot: &Dot, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<Prepared> {
        let (Some(branch), Some(base)) = (&prepared.branch, &prepared.base_commit) else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(
                "cannot restore a worktree without its branch and base commit".into(),
            ));
        };
        let workdir = PathBuf::from(&dot.spec.workdir);
        if !workdir.is_dir() {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "workdir does not exist: {}",
                workdir.display()
            )));
        }
        // Drop the stale registration of the deleted directory so the path is reusable.
        git(&workdir, &["worktree", "prune"]).await?;
        if let Some(parent) = prepared.path.parent() {
            tokio::fs::create_dir_all(parent).await?;
        }
        let path = prepared.path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
        let branch_ref = format!("refs/heads/{branch}");
        let exists = git(&workdir, &["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", &branch_ref])
            .await
            .is_ok();
        let added = if exists {
            git(&workdir, &["worktree", "add", &path, branch]).await
        } else {
            git(&workdir, &["worktree", "add", "-b", branch, &path, base]).await
        };
        if let Err(e) = added {
            let _ = tokio::fs::remove_dir_all(&prepared.path).await;
            return Err(e);
        }
        Ok(prepared.clone())
    }

    pub async fn has_changes(&self, path: &Path, base_commit: &str) -> Result<bool> {
        if !git(path, &["status", "--porcelain"]).await?.is_empty() {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        let ahead = git(
            path,
            &["rev-list", "--count", &format!("{base_commit}..HEAD")],
        )
        .await?;
        Ok(ahead != "0")
    }

    /// Has git remove the worktree at `prepared.path`, registered in `repo`, and makes sure its
    /// folder is gone afterwards. Git leaves a junction where it is and still reports success,
    /// so a worktree that holds one (a `node_modules` made by pnpm, say) stays behind as a
    /// folder with the junction in it. What is left is deleted here without following any
    /// link: `remove_dir_all` removes a symlink or a junction as the link itself, never what
    /// it points to. That only ever happens to a folder that passes `check_owned`; when the
    /// folder cannot be deleted the error names it.
    async fn remove_worktree(&self, repo: &Path, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<()> {
        let path = prepared.path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
        git(repo, &["worktree", "remove", "--force", &path]).await?;
        if !prepared.path.exists() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        self.check_owned(prepared)?;
        tokio::fs::remove_dir_all(&prepared.path)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| {
                Error::Other(format!(
                    "could not remove what git left of worktree {}: {e}",
                    prepared.path.display()
                ))
            })
    }

    pub async fn remove(&self, workdir: &Path, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<()> {
        self.remove_worktree(workdir, prepared).await?;
        if let Some(branch) = &prepared.branch {
            git(workdir, &["branch", "-D", branch]).await?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Checks that `prepared` is a worktree this manager made: a folder directly inside its
    /// root (one level, no `..`, not the root itself), on the branch `dots/…/<that folder's
    /// name>`. Only the text of the path and the branch is examined, so the check runs before
    /// anything touches the disk or git.
    pub fn check_owned(&self, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<()> {
        let mut inside = prepared
            .path
            .strip_prefix(&self.root)
            .into_iter()
            .flat_map(Path::components);
        let folder = match (inside.next(), inside.next()) {
            (Some(Component::Normal(name)), None) => name.to_str(),
            _ => None,
        };
        let Some(folder) = folder else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "refusing to use {}: not a dots worktree",
                prepared.path.display()
            )));
        };
        // The branch comes from the same row as the path: only accept the one dots made for
        // this worktree (`dots/<dot name>/<run id>` beside folder `<run id>`).
        let branch = prepared.branch.as_deref().unwrap_or_default();
        let ours = branch
            .strip_prefix("dots/")
            .and_then(|rest| rest.rsplit_once('/'))
            .is_some_and(|(dot, run)| !dot.is_empty() && run == folder);
        if !ours {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "refusing to use branch {branch}: not the dots branch of worktree {folder}"
            )));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Removes the worktree (if still there) and its branch (if it still exists), for the
    /// user's "Discard worktree" action. Harmless when both are already gone. The repository
    /// is the one the worktree itself names, because the dot's folder may have been changed
    /// since the run; `workdir` (that folder) is used only when the worktree cannot say.
    pub async fn discard(&self, workdir: &Path, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<()> {
        let repo = match repository_of(&prepared.path).await {
            Some(repo) => repo,
            None => workdir.to_path_buf(),
        };
        if prepared.path.exists() {
            self.remove_worktree(&repo, prepared).await?;
        }
        git(&repo, &["worktree", "prune"]).await?;
        if let Some(branch) = &prepared.branch {
            let branch_ref = format!("refs/heads/{branch}");
            if git(&repo, &["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", &branch_ref])
                .await
                .is_ok()
            {
                git(&repo, &["branch", "-D", branch]).await?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Removes a worktree that has no uncommitted changes and no new commits.
    pub async fn cleanup_if_unchanged(&self, dot: &Dot, prepared: &Prepared) -> Result<bool> {
        if dot.spec.workspace_mode != WorkspaceMode::Worktree {
            return Ok(false);
        }
        let (Some(_), Some(base)) = (&prepared.branch, &prepared.base_commit) else {
            return Ok(false);
        };
        if !prepared.path.exists() || self.has_changes(&prepared.path, base).await? {
            return Ok(false);
        }
        self.remove(Path::new(&dot.spec.workdir), prepared).await?;
        Ok(true)
    }
}
```

```rust
    /// The worktree a run row describes, for the review actions. The row is the only thing

    fn review_worktree(&self, run: &Run) -> Result<(WorkspaceManager, Prepared)> {
```

### Task 4: Runtime checks — a busy port, an honest error, one line of chat, a plain program path

Four checks in the core's runtime. "Restart Dots" can reach the bind before the old process has let go of the socket, and the app then comes back without its local server. The error of a Claude run while the server is down says "port N is in use" whatever went wrong. `chat` lets two follow-ups continue the same run, which forks the conversation. And `claude_path` accepts characters that can make the path in the confirmation dialog read differently from what will run.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/settings.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/server/mod.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/runtime.rs`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/settings_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/server_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/runtime_status_test.rs`, `crates/dots-core/tests/runtime_test.rs`, the test module of `apps/dots-app/src/confirm.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `server::bind`, `Config`, `Runtime::chat`, `Settings::validate` (Plans 1 to 3b); `confirm::save_settings_confirmed` (Plan 3b).
- Produces:
  - `server::BIND_WAIT: Duration` (2 s), `server::bind_retrying(port: u16, wait: Duration) -> std::io::Result<TcpListener>`, `server::retry_while_in_use(wait, attempt)` (a retry loop over any attempt closure, so it is tested without sockets), `server::no_server_text(port: u16, bind_error: &std::io::Error) -> String`. `server::bind` stays one attempt.
  - `Config.bind_wait: Duration` (default `server::BIND_WAIT`). `Runtime::start` retries the bind every 50 ms for that long, and only while the error is "address in use". A free port costs one attempt. A port another program holds delays the start by `bind_wait`, then the start is degraded as before.
  - The error of a Claude run while the server is down: `local server is not running: port {port} is in use. Change the port in Settings and restart Dots.` only for "address in use"; otherwise `local server is not running: could not listen on port {port}: {os error}. Try another port in Settings and restart Dots.`
  - `Runtime::chat` with a parent answers, in this order: `NotFound` for an unknown dot or run; `Invalid("run {pid} belongs to a different dot")`; `Conflict("this chat is waiting for an approval; answer it, then send your message again")`; `Conflict("the dot is still working on this chat; wait until it has finished, then send your message again")`; `Conflict("this chat has a newer message than the one you replied to; open the chat again, then send your message")`; `Conflict("this chat cannot be continued because its last run ended before the dot started working; start a new chat")`. The check and the insert of the new run happen under one lock (`chat_lock`), so two follow-ups sent at once cannot both pass.
  - `Settings::validate` refuses a `claude_path` with a control character (also U+2028 and U+2029) or with a Unicode format character (zero-width characters, the byte order mark, the soft hyphen, every text-direction control). The texts: `claude_path must not contain control characters such as a tab or a line break (found U+0009 at character 9)` and `claude_path must not contain invisible or text-direction characters (found U+202E at character 10)`. Letters of any script and spaces pass.
  - A stored `claude_path` that now fails is ignored when settings are read (the store already drops a row that does not validate and logs it): `PATH` is searched, and the next save writes `null`.

with:

```rust
}

fn with_claude_path(path: &str) -> Settings {
    Settings {
        claude_path: Some(path.into()),
        ..Settings::default()
    }
}

#[test]
fn claude_path_accepts_ordinary_paths() {
    for path in [
        r"C:\Users\ana\.local\bin\claude.exe",
        r"C:\Program Files\Claude Code\claude.exe",
        r"C:\Users\Zoë Müller\工具\claude.exe",
        // An emoji with its variation selector: a folder name Explorer can make.
        "D:/apps/\u{2764}\u{fe0f}/claude.cmd",
        "claude",
    ] {
        with_claude_path(path)
            .validate()
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{path}: {e}"));
    }
}

#[test]
fn claude_path_refuses_characters_that_hide_or_reorder_it() {
    const CONTROL: &str = "control characters such as a tab or a line break";
    const FORMAT: &str = "invisible or text-direction characters";
    let cases = [
        ("C:/tools\tclaude.exe", CONTROL, "U+0009 at character 9"),
        (
            "C:/tools/claude.exe\nD:/x.exe",
            CONTROL,
            "U+000A at character 20",
        ),
        ("C:/tools/a\r.exe", CONTROL, "U+000D at character 11"),
        ("C:/tools/a\0.exe", CONTROL, "U+0000 at character 11"),
        ("C:/tools/a\u{7f}.exe", CONTROL, "U+007F at character 11"),
        ("C:/tools/a\u{85}.exe", CONTROL, "U+0085 at character 11"),
        ("C:/tools/a\u{2028}.exe", CONTROL, "U+2028 at character 11"),
        ("C:/tools/a\u{2029}.exe", CONTROL, "U+2029 at character 11"),
        // Shown as "C:/tools/claude.exe": the override turns the rest around.
        (
            "C:/tools/\u{202e}exe.edualc",
            FORMAT,
            "U+202E at character 10",
        ),
        (
            "C:/tools/cla\u{200b}ude.exe",
            FORMAT,
            "U+200B at character 13",
        ),
        // What "Copy" in a file's Properties dialog puts around a path.
        (
            "\u{202a}C:/tools/claude.exe\u{202c}",
            FORMAT,
            "U+202A at character 1",
        ),
        (
            "\u{feff}C:/tools/claude.exe",
            FORMAT,
            "U+FEFF at character 1",
        ),
        (
            "C:/tools/\u{2066}a\u{2069}.exe",
            FORMAT,
            "U+2066 at character 10",
        ),
        // Counted in characters, not bytes.
        ("C:/工具/a\u{200f}.exe", FORMAT, "U+200F at character 8"),
        ("C:/tools/a\u{e0041}.exe", FORMAT, "U+E0041 at character 11"),
    ];
    for (path, rule, found) in cases {
        let err = with_claude_path(path).validate().unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, dots_core::Error::Invalid(_)), "{err:?}");
        assert_eq!(
            err.to_string(),
            format!("invalid input: claude_path must not contain {rule} (found {found})"),
            "{path:?}"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn claude_path_refuses_every_format_character_and_nothing_next_to_them() {
    // Unicode general category Cf, as inclusive ranges.
    const FORMAT: [(u32, u32); 21] = [
        (0xAD, 0xAD),
        (0x600, 0x605),
        (0x61C, 0x61C),
        (0x6DD, 0x6DD),
        (0x70F, 0x70F),
        (0x890, 0x891),
        (0x8E2, 0x8E2),
        (0x180E, 0x180E),
        (0x200B, 0x200F),
        (0x202A, 0x202E),
        (0x2060, 0x2064),
        (0x2066, 0x206F),
        (0xFEFF, 0xFEFF),
        (0xFFF9, 0xFFFB),
        (0x110BD, 0x110BD),
        (0x110CD, 0x110CD),
        (0x13430, 0x1343F),
        (0x1BCA0, 0x1BCA3),
        (0x1D173, 0x1D17A),
        (0xE0001, 0xE0001),
        (0xE0020, 0xE007F),
    ];
    let refused = |code: u32| {
        let c = char::from_u32(code).unwrap();
        with_claude_path(&format!("C:/a{c}b")).validate().is_err()
    };
    for (first, last) in FORMAT {
        for code in first..=last {
            assert!(refused(code), "U+{code:04X} should be refused");
        }
    }
    // So are the code points not assigned yet inside two of those blocks.
    for code in [0x2065, 0xE0000, 0xE0002, 0xE001F] {
        assert!(refused(code), "U+{code:04X} should be refused");
    }
    // The neighbours of the ranges are ordinary characters, and so are the look-alikes of a
    // space (no-break, hair, narrow no-break, ideographic), which are not format characters.
    for code in [
        0xAC, 0xAE, 0x606, 0x61B, 0x61D, 0x200A, 0x2010, 0x202F, 0x205F, 0x2070, 0xFEFC, 0xFFFC,
        0xA0, 0x3000,
    ] {
        assert!(!refused(code), "U+{code:04X} should be accepted");
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_stored_claude_path_with_a_hidden_character_is_ignored() {
    let (dir, store) = temp_store().await;
    let pool = sqlx::SqlitePool::connect(&format!(
        "sqlite://{}",
        dir.path().join("dots.db").display()
    ))
    .await
    .unwrap();
    // Stored by a version that did not check the path.
    for (k, v) in [
        ("port", "40003".to_string()),
        (
            "claude_path",
            serde_json::to_string("C:/tools/\u{202e}exe.edualc").unwrap(),
        ),
    ] {
        sqlx::query("INSERT INTO settings (key, value) VALUES (?, ?)")
            .bind(k)
            .bind(v)
            .execute(&pool)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    }
    // The app still starts: the path is dropped (PATH is searched), the rest is kept.
    let s = store.get_settings().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.claude_path, None);
    assert_eq!(s.port, 40003);
    // The next save replaces the row.
    store.put_settings(&s).await.unwrap();
    let raw: String = sqlx::query_scalar("SELECT value FROM settings WHERE key = 'claude_path'")
        .fetch_one(&pool)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    pool.close().await;
    assert_eq!(raw, "null");
}
```

```rust
/// Runs `retry_while_in_use` over attempts that fail with `kind` until `succeed_at` (counted
/// from 0). Returns the result and how many attempts were made.
async fn attempts(
    wait: Duration,
    kind: ErrorKind,
    succeed_at: usize,
) -> (std::io::Result<usize>, usize) {
    let calls = AtomicUsize::new(0);
    let result = retry_while_in_use(wait, || {
        let n = calls.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
        async move {
            if n < succeed_at {
                Err(std::io::Error::from(kind))
            } else {
                Ok(n)
            }
        }
    })
    .await;
    (result, calls.into_inner())
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_bind_is_retried_only_while_the_port_is_in_use_and_only_for_the_wait() {
    const NEVER: usize = usize::MAX;
    // In use twice, then free: the third attempt is the answer.
    let (result, calls) = attempts(Duration::from_secs(2), ErrorKind::AddrInUse, 2).await;
    assert_eq!((result.unwrap(), calls), (2, 3));

    // Any other failure is final at once, however long the wait.
    let (result, calls) =
        attempts(Duration::from_secs(2), ErrorKind::PermissionDenied, NEVER).await;
    assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().kind(), ErrorKind::PermissionDenied);
    assert_eq!(calls, 1);

    // No wait: one attempt.
    let (result, calls) = attempts(Duration::ZERO, ErrorKind::AddrInUse, NEVER).await;
    assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().kind(), ErrorKind::AddrInUse);
    assert_eq!(calls, 1);

    // In use for the whole wait: the answer is "in use", once the wait is over.
    let started = Instant::now();
    let wait = Duration::from_millis(200);
    let (result, calls) = attempts(wait, ErrorKind::AddrInUse, NEVER).await;
    assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().kind(), ErrorKind::AddrInUse);
    assert!(started.elapsed() >= wait, "{:?}", started.elapsed());
    assert!(calls > 1, "{calls}");
}

#[test]
fn the_run_error_says_in_use_only_for_a_port_in_use() {
    assert_eq!(
        no_server_text(47321, &std::io::Error::from(ErrorKind::AddrInUse)),
        "local server is not running: port 47321 is in use. Change the port in Settings and \
         restart Dots."
    );
    // Windows answers "access denied" for a port in a range it has reserved.
    let denied = std::io::Error::new(ErrorKind::PermissionDenied, "access denied");
    assert_eq!(
        no_server_text(50000, &denied),
        "local server is not running: could not listen on port 50000: access denied. Try \
         another port in Settings and restart Dots."
    );
}
```

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_port_freed_a_moment_after_the_start_began_still_gets_the_server() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let holder = std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
    let port = holder.local_addr().unwrap().port();
    // The process that "Restart Dots" replaces: it lets go of the port a moment later.
    let old_process = std::thread::spawn(move || {
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(400));
        drop(holder);
    });
    let mut cfg = config(&dir);
    cfg.port = port;
    let rt = Runtime::start(cfg).await.unwrap();
    old_process.join().unwrap();

    let status = rt.status().await;
    assert_eq!((status.port, status.server_error), (port, None));
    let health = reqwest::get(format!("http://127.0.0.1:{port}/health"))
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .text()
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(health, "ok");
    rt.shutdown();
}
```

```rust
const CHAT_BUSY: &str = "the dot is still working on this chat; wait until it has finished, \
                         then send your message again";

/// The text of the `Conflict` a refused follow-up answers with, which the UI shows as it is.
fn refusal<T: std::fmt::Debug>(res: Result<T, Error>) -> String {
    match res {
        Err(Error::Conflict(text)) => text,
        other => panic!("expected a conflict, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn chat_follow_up_must_continue_the_latest_run_of_its_thread() {
    let t = start(None, None).await;
    let dot = t.rt.create_dot(folder_spec(&t.dir, "line")).await.unwrap();
    let first = t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "one", None).await.unwrap();
    wait_status(t.rt.store(), &first.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;
    let second = t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "two", Some(&first.id)).await.unwrap();
    wait_status(t.rt.store(), &second.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;

    // A screen that still ends at the first run: its reply would make a second branch.
    let res = t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "two again", Some(&first.id)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(res),
        "this chat has a newer message than the one you replied to; open the chat again, then \
         send your message"
    );
    assert_eq!(t.rt.run_thread(&first.id).await.unwrap().len(), 2);

    // The normal follow-up continues from the end of the thread.
    let third = t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "three", Some(&second.id)).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(third.parent_run_id.as_deref(), Some(second.id.as_str()));
    assert_eq!(third.root_run_id, first.id);
    wait_status(t.rt.store(), &third.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;
    let thread = t.rt.run_thread(&first.id).await.unwrap();
    let ids: Vec<&str> = thread.iter().map(|r| r.id.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(ids, [&first.id, &second.id, &third.id]);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn chat_follow_up_is_refused_while_its_thread_waits_for_an_approval() {
    // Seeded before start, so the dispatcher never sees these runs: a finished first run and
    // its follow-up, which is parked on an approval nobody has answered.
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let data = dir.path().join("data");
    let (dot, first, parked) = {
        let store = Store::open(&data.join("dots.db")).await.unwrap();
        let dot = store.create_dot(&folder_spec(&dir, "asks")).await.unwrap();
        let first = store
            .create_run(&NewRun::new(&dot.id, TriggerKind::Chat))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        store.claim_run(&first.id).await.unwrap();
        store.set_session(&first.id, "s1").await.unwrap();
        store
            .finish_run(&first.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, Some("ok"), None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
        // Runs are ordered by the millisecond they were queued in.
        tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(5)).await;
        let mut new = NewRun::new(&dot.id, TriggerKind::Chat);
        new.parent_run_id = Some(first.id.clone());
        new.root_run_id = Some(first.id.clone());
        new.session_id = Some("s1".into());
        let parked = store.create_run(&new).await.unwrap();
        store.claim_run(&parked.id).await.unwrap();
        store
            .finish_run(&parked.id, RunStatus::AwaitingApproval, None, None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
        store
            .create_approval(&parked.id, "Bash", &json!({"command": "git push"}))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        (dot, first, parked)
    };
    let mut cfg = Config::new(data);
    cfg.port = 0;
    cfg.claude_path = Some(PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-claude")));
    let rt = Runtime::start(cfg).await.unwrap();

    // Whichever run of the thread the reply names, the thread is not idle.
    for parent in [&parked.id, &first.id] {
        let res = rt.chat(&dot.id, "and?", Some(parent)).await;
        assert_eq!(
            refusal(res),
            "this chat is waiting for an approval; answer it, then send your message again"
        );
    }
    assert_eq!(rt.run_thread(&first.id).await.unwrap().len(), 2);
    rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn two_follow_ups_sent_at_once_do_not_fork_the_thread() {
    let t = start(None, None).await;
    let dot = t.rt.create_dot(folder_spec(&t.dir, "twice")).await.unwrap();
    let first = t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "one", None).await.unwrap();
    wait_status(t.rt.store(), &first.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;

    let (a, b) = tokio::join!(
        t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "two", Some(&first.id)),
        t.rt.chat(&dot.id, "two", Some(&first.id)),
    );
    let (sent, refused) = match (a, b) {
        (Ok(run), refused @ Err(_)) | (refused @ Err(_), Ok(run)) => (run, refused),
        other => panic!("expected one follow-up and one refusal, got {other:?}"),
    };
    assert_eq!(refusal(refused), CHAT_BUSY);
    let thread = t.rt.run_thread(&first.id).await.unwrap();
    let ids: Vec<&str> = thread.iter().map(|r| r.id.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(ids, [&first.id, &sent.id]);
    wait_status(t.rt.store(), &sent.id, RunStatus::Succeeded, 15).await;
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```

```rust
}

/// Refuses a value with a character that would show it as something other than it is. The
/// user confirms `claude_path` by reading it in a native dialog (spec §12), and that only works
/// if every character is seen, in the order it is stored. Two rules, named in the error with
/// the character and its position (counted from 1):
///
/// - no control characters, and no line or paragraph separator: a tab or a line break splits
///   the value or pushes a part of it out of sight;
/// - no format characters: they have no width, and some turn the text around them (U+202E
///   shows `exe.edualc` as `claude.exe`).
///
/// Letters of any script, spaces and the look-alikes of a space pass: a name that reads like
/// another one is not hidden, and no list of characters can rule that out.
fn check_plain_text(field: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    for (i, c) in value.chars().enumerate() {
        let rule = if c.is_control() || matches!(c, '\u{2028}' | '\u{2029}') {
            "control characters such as a tab or a line break"
        } else if is_format_char(c) {
            "invisible or text-direction characters"
        } else {
            continue;
        };
        return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
            "{field} must not contain {rule} (found U+{:04X} at character {})",
            c as u32,
            i + 1
        )));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Unicode general category Cf (format), which holds the zero-width characters, the byte order
/// mark, the soft hyphen, the tag characters and every bidirectional control. `std` knows only
/// the control category, so the ranges are listed here (Unicode 15.0; the code points not
/// assigned yet inside U+2060..U+206F and U+E0000..U+E007F are included).
fn is_format_char(c: char) -> bool {
    matches!(
        c,
        '\u{ad}'
            | '\u{600}'..='\u{605}'
            | '\u{61c}'
            | '\u{6dd}'
            | '\u{70f}'
            | '\u{890}'..='\u{891}'
            | '\u{8e2}'
            | '\u{180e}'
            | '\u{200b}'..='\u{200f}'
            | '\u{202a}'..='\u{202e}'
            | '\u{2060}'..='\u{206f}'
            | '\u{feff}'
            | '\u{fff9}'..='\u{fffb}'
            | '\u{110bd}'
            | '\u{110cd}'
            | '\u{13430}'..='\u{1343f}'
            | '\u{1bca0}'..='\u{1bca3}'
            | '\u{1d173}'..='\u{1d17a}'
            | '\u{e0000}'..='\u{e007f}'
    )
}
```

```rust
}

/// How long a start keeps trying a port that is in use, unless `Config::bind_wait` says
/// otherwise.
pub const BIND_WAIT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2);
const BIND_RETRY_EVERY: Duration = Duration::from_millis(50);

/// `bind`, tried again every 50 ms for up to `wait` while the port is in use. "Restart Dots"
/// starts the new process while the old one may still hold the socket for a moment; without
/// the wait that start would come up without its server. A port that is free costs one attempt.
pub async fn bind_retrying(port: u16, wait: Duration) -> std::io::Result<TcpListener> {
    retry_while_in_use(wait, || TcpListener::bind(("127.0.0.1", port))).await
}

/// Runs `attempt` until it answers anything but "address in use", or until `wait` has passed
/// since the first attempt: then the last answer is returned. Every other error is returned at
/// once, since waiting does not change it.
pub async fn retry_while_in_use<T, F, Fut>(wait: Duration, mut attempt: F) -> std::io::Result<T>
where
    F: FnMut() -> Fut,
    Fut: Future<Output = std::io::Result<T>>,
{
    let give_up = Instant::now() + wait;
    loop {
        match attempt().await {
            Err(e) if e.kind() == ErrorKind::AddrInUse && Instant::now() < give_up => {
                tokio::time::sleep(BIND_RETRY_EVERY).await;
            }
            answer => return answer,
        }
    }
}

/// The error of a Claude run while the local server is down (spec §8): such a run needs the
/// server's MCP endpoint. Says "in use" only when that is what the bind answered.
pub fn no_server_text(port: u16, bind_error: &std::io::Error) -> String {
    if bind_error.kind() == ErrorKind::AddrInUse {
        format!(
            "local server is not running: port {port} is in use. Change the port in Settings \
             and restart Dots."
        )
    } else {
        format!(
            "local server is not running: could not listen on port {port}: {bind_error}. Try \
             another port in Settings and restart Dots."
        )
    }
}

    listener: TcpListener,
```

```rust
use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Arc;
use std::time::Duration;

use serde_json::json;
use tokio::sync::{broadcast, Mutex};
use tokio_util::sync::CancellationToken;

use crate::approvals::{ApprovalHub, DecideEffect};
use crate::engine::claude::args::resolve_claude;
use crate::engine::claude::{cli_version, ClaudeEngine};
use crate::engine::local::client::DEFAULT_RETRY_DELAYS;
use crate::engine::local::{self, LocalEngine};
use crate::engine::{Engine, UnavailableEngine};
use crate::events::{new_bus, Bus, RuntimeEvent};
use crate::model::{
    Approval, ClaudeStatus, Dot, DotOverview, DotSpec, EngineKind, Inbox, NewRun, PendingApproval,
    ReviewRun, Run, RunEventRecord, RunStatus, SettingsSaved, Status, TriggerKind, WorktreeDiff,
};
use crate::runner::{Runner, RunnerConfig};
use crate::scheduler::Scheduler;
use crate::server::{self, ServerState};
use crate::settings::Settings;
use crate::store::Store;
use crate::workspace::{Prepared, WorkspaceManager};
use crate::{Error, Result};

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Config {
    pub data_dir: PathBuf,
    pub port: u16,
    /// How long `Runtime::start` keeps trying a port that is in use before it starts without
    /// the server (`server::bind_retrying`). A test that holds the port on purpose shortens it.
    pub bind_wait: Duration,
    pub max_concurrent_runs: usize,
    pub claude_path: Option<PathBuf>,
    pub claude_env: Vec<(String, String)>,
    /// Delays between attempts when a local endpoint is unreachable or answers 5xx.
    pub local_retry_delays: Vec<Duration>,
}

impl Config {
    pub fn new(data_dir: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self {
            data_dir: data_dir.into(),
            port: 47321,
            bind_wait: server::BIND_WAIT,
            max_concurrent_runs: 2,
            claude_path: None,
            claude_env: Vec::new(),
            local_retry_delays: DEFAULT_RETRY_DELAYS.to_vec(),
        }
    }

    /// The start configuration the app derives from stored settings.
    pub fn from_settings(data_dir: impl Into<PathBuf>, settings: &Settings) -> Self {
        let mut cfg = Self::new(data_dir);
        cfg.port = settings.port;
        cfg.max_concurrent_runs = settings.max_concurrent_runs;
        cfg.claude_path = settings.claude_path.as_ref().map(PathBuf::from);
        cfg
    }
}

pub struct Runtime {
    store: Store,
    bus: Bus,
    hub: Arc<ApprovalHub>,
    runner: Arc<Runner>,
    scheduler: Arc<Scheduler>,
    port: u16,
    server_error: Option<String>,
    claude_program: Option<PathBuf>,
    claude_version: Option<String>,
    data_dir: PathBuf,
    /// The stored settings when the runtime started, and now.
    started: Settings,
    settings: Mutex<Settings>,
    /// Makes `chat` check a thread and add to it in one step.
    chat_lock: Mutex<()>,
    shutdown: CancellationToken,
}

impl Runtime {
    pub async fn start(cfg: Config) -> Result<Runtime> {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&cfg.data_dir)?;
        let store = Store::open(&cfg.data_dir.join(crate::store::DB_FILE)).await?;
        let settings = store.get_settings().await?;
        let bus = new_bus();
        let hub = Arc::new(ApprovalHub::new(store.clone(), bus.clone()));
        // A taken port must not keep the app from starting (spec §8): run without the server.
        // The bind waits a moment for a port in use, which after "Restart Dots" is the old
        // process on its way out.
        let (listener, bind_error) = match server::bind_retrying(cfg.port, cfg.bind_wait).await {
            Ok(l) => (Some(l), None),
            Err(e) => (None, Some(e)),
        };
        let server_error = bind_error
            .as_ref()
            .map(|e| format!("could not listen on 127.0.0.1:{}: {e}", cfg.port));
        if let Some(msg) = &server_error {
            tracing::error!("{msg}; webhooks are off and Claude dots cannot run");
        }
        let port = match &listener {
            Some(l) => l.local_addr()?.port(),
            None => cfg.port,
        };
        let recovered = store.recover_interrupted().await?;
        if !recovered.is_empty() {
            tracing::warn!("marked {} interrupted run(s) as failed", recovered.len());
        }

        let mut engines: HashMap<EngineKind, Arc<dyn Engine>> = HashMap::new();
        let claude_program = resolve_claude(cfg.claude_path.as_deref());
        let claude_version = match &claude_program {
            Some(program) => cli_version(program, &cfg.claude_env).await,
            None => None,
        };
        let claude: Arc<dyn Engine> = match (&claude_program, &bind_error) {
            // Claude runs need the MCP permission endpoint of the local server.
            (_, Some(e)) => Arc::new(UnavailableEngine(server::no_server_text(port, e))),
            (None, None) => {
                tracing::warn!(
                    "claude CLI not found; Claude dots will fail until it is configured"
                );
                Arc::new(UnavailableEngine(
                    "claude CLI not found: install Claude Code or set its path in Settings".into(),
                ))
            }
            (Some(program), None) => {
                let mut engine = ClaudeEngine::new(program.clone(), cfg.data_dir.join("mcp"));
                for (k, v) in &cfg.claude_env {
                    engine = engine.with_env(k, v);
                }
                Arc::new(engine)
            }
        };
        engines.insert(EngineKind::Claude, claude);
        engines.insert(
            EngineKind::Local,
            Arc::new(LocalEngine::with_retry_delays(
                store.clone(),
                hub.clone(),
                cfg.local_retry_delays.clone(),
            )),
        );

        let runner = Runner::new(
            store.clone(),
            bus.clone(),
            engines,
            RunnerConfig {
                max_concurrent: cfg.max_concurrent_runs,
                mcp_url: format!("http://127.0.0.1:{port}/mcp"),
                worktrees_dir: cfg.data_dir.join("worktrees"),
                cancel_grace: Duration::from_secs(10),
            },
        );
        // Decisions parked before a restart (decided, but the resume never happened) are
        // turned into resume runs now; failures are logged and never block startup.
        match store.list_runs_by_status(RunStatus::AwaitingApproval).await {
            Ok(waiting) => {
                for run in waiting {
                    if let Err(e) = runner.maybe_resume(&run.id).await {
                        tracing::error!(run = %run.id, "startup resume check failed: {e}");
                    }
                }
            }
            Err(e) => tracing::error!("listing awaiting runs failed: {e}"),
        }
        let shutdown = CancellationToken::new();
        if let Some(listener) = listener {
            server::serve(
                listener,
                ServerState {
                    store: store.clone(),
                    runner: runner.clone(),
                    hub: hub.clone(),
                },
                shutdown.clone(),
            );
        }
        runner.spawn_dispatcher(shutdown.clone());
        let scheduler = Scheduler::new(store.clone(), runner.clone());
        scheduler.set_paused(settings.schedules_paused);
        scheduler.spawn(shutdown.clone());
        Ok(Runtime {
            store,
            bus,
            hub,
            runner,
            scheduler,
            port,
            server_error,
            claude_program,
            claude_version,
            data_dir: cfg.data_dir.clone(),
            started: settings.clone(),
            settings: Mutex::new(settings),
            chat_lock: Mutex::new(()),
            shutdown,
        })
    }

    pub async fn status(&self) -> Status {
        let settings = self.settings.lock().await.clone();
        Status {
            port: self.port,
            server_error: self.server_error.clone(),
            claude: ClaudeStatus {
                program: self
                    .claude_program
                    .as_ref()
                    .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
                version: self.claude_version.clone(),
                mismatch: self
                    .claude_version
                    .as_ref()
                    .is_some_and(|v| *v != settings.tested_claude_version),
                tested_version: settings.tested_claude_version.clone(),
            },
            schedules_paused: self.scheduler.is_paused(),
            restart_required: self.started.restart_required(&settings),
            data_dir: self.data_dir.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
        }
    }

    /// Records the detected `claude` version as tested, which clears the mismatch warning.
    pub async fn mark_claude_tested(&self) -> Result<Status> {
        let Some(version) = self.claude_version.clone() else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid("no claude CLI version was detected".into()));
        };
        self.change_settings(|s| s.tested_claude_version = version)
            .await?;
        Ok(self.status().await)
    }

    pub async fn settings(&self) -> Settings {
        self.settings.lock().await.clone()
    }

    /// Validates and stores `next`. Port, concurrency and the `claude` path apply after a
    /// restart (`restart_required`); autostart is the app's job. The stored `schedules_paused`
    /// and `tested_claude_version` are kept whatever `next` says: only `set_schedules_paused`
    /// and `mark_claude_tested` change them, so a settings form holding an old copy cannot undo
    /// a pause or a "mark as tested".
    pub async fn update_settings(&self, next: Settings) -> Result<SettingsSaved> {
        let settings = self
            .change_settings(|s| {
                *s = Settings {
                    schedules_paused: s.schedules_paused,
                    tested_claude_version: std::mem::take(&mut s.tested_claude_version),
                    ..next
                };
            })
            .await?;
        Ok(SettingsSaved {
            restart_required: self.started.restart_required(&settings),
            settings,
        })
    }

    pub async fn set_schedules_paused(&self, paused: bool) -> Result<Status> {
        self.change_settings(|s| s.schedules_paused = paused)
            .await?;
        Ok(self.status().await)
    }

    /// One read-modify-write of the settings under their lock, so two changes cannot overwrite
    /// each other. Nothing is stored when the result does not validate.
    async fn change_settings(&self, change: impl FnOnce(&mut Settings) + Send) -> Result<Settings> {
        let mut current = self.settings.lock().await;
        let mut next = current.clone();
        change(&mut next);
        self.store.put_settings(&next).await?;
        self.scheduler.set_paused(next.schedules_paused);
        *current = next.clone();
        Ok(next)
    }

    pub async fn list_dots(&self) -> Result<Vec<Dot>> {
        self.store.list_dots().await
    }

    pub async fn get_dot(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Dot> {
        self.store.get_dot(id).await
    }

    /// Every dot with its active and last run and its next scheduled fire time.
    pub async fn dot_overview(&self) -> Result<Vec<DotOverview>> {
        let now = chrono::Local::now();
        let mut rows = Vec::new();
        for dot in self.store.list_dots().await? {
            let next_fire = match (&dot.spec.schedule, dot.spec.enabled) {
                (Some(expr), true) => crate::scheduler::next_fire(expr, now)
                    .ok()
                    .flatten()
                    .map(|t| t.to_rfc3339()),
                _ => None,
            };
            rows.push(DotOverview {
                active_run: self.store.latest_run(&dot.id, true).await?,
                last_run: self.store.latest_run(&dot.id, false).await?,
                next_fire,
                dot,
            });
        }
        Ok(rows)
    }

    /// The next `count` (1 to 10) fire times of a cron expression, RFC 3339 in local time.
    pub fn preview_schedule(expr: &str, count: usize) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
        let schedule = crate::scheduler::parse_cron(expr)?;
        Ok(schedule
            .after(&chrono::Local::now())
            .take(count.clamp(1, 10))
            .map(|t| t.to_rfc3339())
            .collect())
    }

    /// Newest first; `limit` is clamped to 1..=500.
    pub async fn list_runs(&self, dot_id: Option<&str>, limit: i64) -> Result<Vec<Run>> {
        self.store.list_runs(dot_id, limit.clamp(1, 500)).await
    }

    pub async fn get_run(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Run> {
        self.store.get_run(id).await
    }

    /// The whole lineage (root, resumes, chat follow-ups) `run_id` belongs to, oldest first.
    pub async fn run_thread(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<Vec<Run>> {
        let run = self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        self.store.list_thread(&run.root_run_id).await
    }

    pub async fn list_run_events(
        &self,
        run_id: &str,
        after_seq: i64,
    ) -> Result<Vec<RunEventRecord>> {
        self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        self.store.list_events(run_id, after_seq).await
    }

    pub async fn approvals_for_run(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<Vec<Approval>> {
        self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        self.store.approvals_for_run(run_id).await
    }

    /// Pending approvals, plus the finished, unreviewed thread ends that failed or kept a
    /// worktree with changes.
    pub async fn inbox(&self) -> Result<Inbox> {
        let mut names = std::collections::HashMap::new();
        for dot in self.store.list_dots().await? {
            names.insert(dot.id, dot.spec.name);
        }
        let name = |dot_id: &str| names.get(dot_id).cloned().unwrap_or_default();
        let mut approvals = Vec::new();
        for approval in self.store.list_pending_approvals().await? {
            let run = self.store.get_run(&approval.run_id).await?;
            approvals.push(PendingApproval {
                dot_name: name(&run.dot_id),
                dot_id: run.dot_id,
                approval,
            });
        }
        let review_runs = self
            .store
            .unreviewed_thread_ends()
            .await?
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|run| {
                let worktree_exists = run.branch.is_some()
                    && run
                        .workspace_path
                        .as_deref()
                        .is_some_and(|p| Path::new(p).is_dir());
                (run.status == RunStatus::Failed || worktree_exists).then(|| ReviewRun {
                    dot_name: name(&run.dot_id),
                    run,
                    worktree_exists,
                })
            })
            .collect();
        Ok(Inbox {
            approvals,
            review_runs,
        })
    }

    pub async fn inbox_count(&self) -> Result<usize> {
        let inbox = self.inbox().await?;
        Ok(inbox.approvals.len() + inbox.review_runs.len())
    }

    /// Takes the thread of `run_id` out of the Inbox. Refused while the thread is active.
    pub async fn mark_reviewed(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<Run> {
        let run = self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        self.ensure_thread_idle(&run.root_run_id).await?;
        for changed in self.store.mark_thread_reviewed(&run.root_run_id).await? {
            let _ = self.bus.send(RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated { run: changed });
        }
        self.store.get_run(run_id).await
    }

    /// The worktree a run row describes, for the review actions. The row is the only thing
    /// between the database and git commands in the user's repository, so before any of them
    /// it must name a worktree dots made (`WorkspaceManager::check_owned`). `Invalid` for a
    /// folder run and for a row that fails the check.
    fn review_worktree(&self, run: &Run) -> Result<(WorkspaceManager, Prepared)> {
        let Some(prepared) = Prepared::from_run(run).filter(|p| p.branch.is_some()) else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!("run {} has no worktree", run.id)));
        };
        let workspaces = WorkspaceManager::new(self.data_dir.join("worktrees"));
        workspaces.check_owned(&prepared)?;
        Ok((workspaces, prepared))
    }

    /// The changes the run's thread made in its worktree. Empty, with `exists` false, once the
    /// worktree is removed. Refused for folder runs and for any row that does not name a
    /// worktree of dots, whether or not its folder exists.
    pub async fn worktree_diff(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<WorktreeDiff> {
        let run = self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        let (_, prepared) = self.review_worktree(&run)?;
        let Some(base) = &prepared.base_commit else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!("run {run_id} has no worktree")));
        };
        if !prepared.path.is_dir() {
            return Ok(WorktreeDiff::default());
        }
        crate::workspace::diff(&prepared.path, base).await
    }

    /// Removes the thread's worktree and branch and marks the thread reviewed. Refused while
    /// the thread is active, for folder runs, and for any row that does not name a worktree of
    /// dots.
    pub async fn discard_worktree(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<Run> {
        let run = self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        self.ensure_thread_idle(&run.root_run_id).await?;
        let (workspaces, prepared) = self.review_worktree(&run)?;
        let dot = self.store.get_dot(&run.dot_id).await?;
        workspaces
            .discard(Path::new(&dot.spec.workdir), &prepared)
            .await?;
        self.mark_reviewed(run_id).await
    }

    /// The folder a run worked in, for "Open folder".
    pub async fn run_folder(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
        let run = self.store.get_run(run_id).await?;
        let Some(path) = run.workspace_path.map(PathBuf::from) else {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!("run {run_id} has not started yet")));
        };
        if !path.is_dir() {
            return Err(Error::NotFound(format!(
                "folder {} no longer exists",
                path.display()
            )));
        }
        Ok(path)
    }

    async fn ensure_thread_idle(&self, root_run_id: &str) -> Result<()> {
        if let Some(r) = self
            .store
            .list_thread(root_run_id)
            .await?
            .into_iter()
            .find(|r| !r.status.is_terminal())
        {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(format!(
                "run {} of this thread is still {}",
                r.id,
                r.status.as_str()
            )));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn port(&self) -> u16 {
        self.port
    }

    pub fn store(&self) -> &Store {
        &self.store
    }

    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<RuntimeEvent> {
        self.bus.subscribe()
    }

    pub fn claude_program(&self) -> Option<&Path> {
        self.claude_program.as_deref()
    }

    /// The dot's trigger URL. `Conflict` while the local server is not running: the port then
    /// belongs to another program, and a webhook sent there would hand it the dot's token.
    pub fn webhook_url(&self, dot: &Dot) -> Result<String> {
        if let Some(reason) = &self.server_error {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(format!(
                "webhooks are off because the local server is not running: {reason}"
            )));
        }
        Ok(format!(
            "http://127.0.0.1:{}/dots/{}/trigger",
            self.port, dot.id
        ))
    }

    pub async fn create_dot(&self, spec: DotSpec) -> Result<Dot> {
        let dot = self.store.create_dot(&spec).await?;
        self.scheduler.reload();
        Ok(dot)
    }

    pub async fn update_dot(&self, id: &str, spec: DotSpec) -> Result<Dot> {
        let dot = self.store.update_dot(id, &spec).await?;
        self.scheduler.reload();
        Ok(dot)
    }

    pub async fn delete_dot(&self, id: &str) -> Result<()> {
        self.store.delete_dot_if_idle(id).await?;
        self.scheduler.reload();
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn set_dot_enabled(&self, id: &str, enabled: bool) -> Result<Dot> {
        let dot = self.store.set_dot_enabled(id, enabled).await?;
        self.scheduler.reload();
        Ok(dot)
    }

    pub async fn regenerate_webhook_token(&self, id: &str) -> Result<String> {
        let token = self.store.regenerate_webhook_token(id).await?;
        self.scheduler.reload();
        Ok(token)
    }

    pub async fn run_now(&self, dot_id: &str) -> Result<Run> {
        self.store.get_dot(dot_id).await?;
        self.runner
            .enqueue(NewRun::new(dot_id, TriggerKind::Manual))
            .await
    }

    pub async fn chat(
        &self,
        dot_id: &str,
        message: &str,
        parent_run_id: Option<&str>,
    ) -> Result<Run> {
        if message.trim().is_empty() {
            return Err(Error::Invalid("message must not be empty".into()));
        }
        self.store.get_dot(dot_id).await?;
        let mut new = NewRun::new(dot_id, TriggerKind::Chat);
        new.payload = Some(json!({ "message": message }));
        // Held until the new run is stored: of two follow-ups sent at once, the second must
        // find the first one's run in the thread.
        let _one_at_a_time = self.chat_lock.lock().await;
        if let Some(pid) = parent_run_id {
            let named = self.store.get_run(pid).await?;
            if named.dot_id != dot_id {
                return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                    "run {pid} belongs to a different dot"
                )));
            }
            // A thread is one line of runs: a follow-up continues from its end, and only once
            // every run of it has ended. Anything else would give one run two children, each
            // resuming the same session in the same folder. The UI keeps its composer locked
            // until then, so these refusals answer a stale screen or a second window; their
            // text is shown to the user as it is.
            let thread = self.store.list_thread(&named.root_run_id).await?;
            if let Some(active) = thread.iter().find(|r| !r.status.is_terminal()) {
                return Err(Error::Conflict(
                    match active.status {
                        RunStatus::AwaitingApproval => {
                            "this chat is waiting for an approval; answer it, then send your \
                             message again"
                        }
                        _ => {
                            "the dot is still working on this chat; wait until it has \
                             finished, then send your message again"
                        }
                    }
                    .into(),
                ));
            }
            // The parent is taken from this reading of the thread, in which it has ended.
            let Some(parent) = thread.last().filter(|r| r.id == pid) else {
                return Err(Error::Conflict(
                    "this chat has a newer message than the one you replied to; open the chat \
                     again, then send your message"
                        .into(),
                ));
            };
            if parent.session_id.is_none() {
                return Err(Error::Conflict(
                    "this chat cannot be continued because its last run ended before the dot \
                     started working; start a new chat"
                        .into(),
                ));
            }
            new.parent_run_id = Some(parent.id.clone());
            new.root_run_id = Some(parent.root_run_id.clone());
            new.session_id = parent.session_id.clone();
            new.workspace_path = parent.workspace_path.clone();
            new.branch = parent.branch.clone();
            new.base_commit = parent.base_commit.clone();
        }
        self.runner.enqueue(new).await
    }

    pub async fn cancel_run(&self, run_id: &str) -> Result<Run> {
        self.runner.cancel(run_id).await
    }

    pub async fn decide_approval(
        &self,
        id: &str,
        approved: bool,
        note: Option<String>,
    ) -> Result<Approval> {
        match self.hub.decide(id, approved, note).await? {
            DecideEffect::Live(a) => Ok(a),
            DecideEffect::Parked(a) => {
                if let Err(e) = self.runner.maybe_resume(&a.run_id).await {
                    tracing::error!(run = %a.run_id, "resume after approval failed: {e}");
                }
                Ok(a)
            }
        }
    }

    /// Checks that `model` at the OpenAI-compatible `endpoint` answers with a well-formed tool
    /// call (the Settings "Test endpoint" button). Returns a short success message.
    pub async fn test_endpoint(&self, endpoint: &str, model: &str) -> Result<String> {
        local::probe(endpoint, model).await
    }

    pub fn shutdown(&self) {
        self.shutdown.cancel();
    }
}
```
