### Task 5: The app leaves a trace when it cannot start, keeps test instances apart, and notifies once

The Tauri shell's start-up and its notifier. A release build has no console, so the log file is the only place a failed start or a panic can be read. A smoke or development instance must never hand over to an installed Dots and its real data. And the notifier must not show two toasts for one park, or trust what it remembers after it missed events.

**Files:**
- Modify: `apps/dots-app/src/startup.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/events.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/state.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/lib.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/tray.rs`
- Test: the `#[cfg(test)]` modules of `startup.rs`, `events.rs` and `state.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Config::from_settings` with Task 4's `bind_wait` default; `AppState` and the failed-start path of Plan 3a (fatal page, no tray, closing the window exits); `dots_core::util::now`.
- Produces:
  - `startup::data_dir<E: Display>(local_data: Result<PathBuf, E>, env_override: Option<OsString>) -> Result<PathBuf, String>`. A `DOTS_DATA_DIR` value goes through `std::path::absolute`, so one folder has one spelling on every start (Task 3's check compares a run's stored path with the data folder as text). Without the override, a failure to find the local data folder answers `Dots could not find the local application data folder: {error}`.
  - On that failure `setup` skips logging and the runtime start and manages `AppState::new(Err(message), None)`: the fatal page opens, there is no tray, closing the window exits. `AppState::new` takes `log_dir: Option<PathBuf>`; `startup_error()` then gives `log_dir: ""`, and the fatal page already leaves the log line out for an empty value.
  - The log folder is created before the appender is built (private `startup::appender`).
  - `startup::log_panics(log_dir: PathBuf)` installs a hook in front of the one already there. `startup::record_panic(log_dir, thread, location, message)` appends one line to today's log file, for example `2026-10-13T08:00:00.000Z ERROR dots_app: thread 'main' panicked at apps\dots-app\src\x.rs:10:5: index out of bounds`, then the earlier hook runs. The line is written to the file directly, not through `tracing`: the logger writes on a worker thread, and a line logged right before an abort does not reliably reach the file.
  - `startup::single_instance(env_override: Option<OsString>) -> bool`: false when `DOTS_DATA_DIR` names a folder. `run()` registers the `single-instance` plugin only when it is true.
  - `Notifier::forget(&mut self)` clears what the notifier remembers. The forwarder calls it when the event stream lagged.
  - One toast per park: the notifier remembers the runs whose approval request it announced. When such a run moves into `awaiting_approval`, that update does not notify. A park whose request was not seen still notifies.
  - `state::runtime_of(state: Option<&AppState>) -> Option<Arc<Runtime>>`. The tray menu and the exit handler use it with `app.try_state::<AppState>()`, so neither can panic when no state was managed.

The tests of this task live in the same files as the code. Two of them are helpers named `child_*` that another test runs again in a child process: one to read the child's stderr when the log folder does not exist yet, one to see a real panic reach the log file.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the tests and the code**

Write each file's test module first. Until its functions exist the crate does not compile, for example `cannot find function 'single_instance' in this scope`.

Replace the contents of `apps/dots-app/src/startup.rs` with:

```rust
//! Everything between process start and a running `Runtime`, kept free of Tauri types so it
//! can be tested headless.

use std::ffi::OsString;
use std::fmt::Display;
use std::io::Write;
use std::panic::Location;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Arc;

use dots_core::settings::{self, Settings};
use dots_core::{Config, Runtime};
use tracing_appender::non_blocking::WorkerGuard;
use tracing_appender::rolling::{Builder, RollingFileAppender, Rotation};
use tracing_subscriber::EnvFilter;

/// Passed by the autostart entry: start in the tray without showing the window.
pub const HIDDEN_ARG: &str = "--hidden";
const LOG_FILES_KEPT: usize = 7;

/// Overrides the data directory, for smoke tests and a second development instance.
pub const DATA_DIR_ENV: &str = "DOTS_DATA_DIR";

/// The folder `DOTS_DATA_DIR` names. An empty value counts as not set.
fn data_dir_override(env_override: Option<OsString>) -> Option<OsString> {
    env_override.filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
}

/// `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dots` (spec §3) given `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, unless `DOTS_DATA_DIR` is set; then
/// `local_data` is not needed. The override is made absolute here, from the current folder
/// and without touching the disk: the core hands paths below the data folder to git, which
/// runs in a dot's folder, and compares stored worktree paths with it, so one folder must
/// have one spelling. The error is the text the fatal-error page shows.
pub fn data_dir<E: Display>(
    local_data: Result<PathBuf, E>,
    env_override: Option<OsString>,
) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    match data_dir_override(env_override) {
        Some(dir) => std::path::absolute(&dir).map_err(|e| {
            format!(
                "Dots could not use the folder {} from {DATA_DIR_ENV}: {e}",
                Path::new(&dir).display()
            )
        }),
        None => local_data
            .map(|local| local.join("dots"))
            .map_err(|e| format!("Dots could not find the local application data folder: {e}")),
    }
}

/// Whether a second launch hands over to the running app. Not under `DOTS_DATA_DIR`: the
/// plugin knows the app, not its data folder, so a smoke or development instance would hand
/// over to an installed Dots and its real data (spec §12).
pub fn single_instance(env_override: Option<OsString>) -> bool {
    data_dir_override(env_override).is_none()
}

pub fn log_dir(data_dir: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    data_dir.join("logs")
}

pub fn start_hidden(mut args: impl Iterator<Item = String>) -> bool {
    args.any(|a| a == HIDDEN_ARG)
}

/// Today's log file in `log_dir`: daily files, the last seven kept. The folder is created
/// first: the appender looks for old files before it opens today's, and prints an error when
/// the folder is not there yet.
fn appender(log_dir: &Path) -> Option<RollingFileAppender> {
    std::fs::create_dir_all(log_dir).ok()?;
    Builder::new()
        .rotation(Rotation::DAILY)
        .filename_prefix("dots")
        .filename_suffix("log")
        .max_log_files(LOG_FILES_KEPT)
        .build(log_dir)
        .ok()
}

/// Daily log files in `log_dir`, the last seven kept. `None` if logging could not start;
/// the app runs without a log then. Keep the guard alive for the whole run.
pub fn init_logging(log_dir: &Path) -> Option<WorkerGuard> {
    let appender = appender(log_dir)?;
    let (writer, guard) = tracing_appender::non_blocking(appender);
    tracing_subscriber::fmt()
        .with_writer(writer)
        .with_ansi(false)
        .with_env_filter(EnvFilter::try_from_default_env().unwrap_or_else(|_| "info".into()))
        .try_init()
        .ok()?;
    Some(guard)
}

/// Writes a panic to today's log file, as a line shaped like the logger's. It goes straight
/// to the file and not through `tracing`: the logger hands its lines to a writer thread, and
/// a panic can end the process before that thread has written them. Nothing here may panic.
pub fn record_panic(
    log_dir: &Path,
    thread: Option<&str>,
    location: Option<&Location<'_>>,
    message: Option<&str>,
) {
    let place = location.map_or_else(|| "an unknown place".to_string(), Location::to_string);
    let line = format!(
        "{} ERROR dots_app: thread '{}' panicked at {place}: {}\n",
        dots_core::util::now(),
        thread.unwrap_or("<unnamed>"),
        message.unwrap_or("(no message)"),
    );
    if let Some(mut log) = appender(log_dir) {
        let _ = log.write_all(line.as_bytes());
    }
}

/// From here on a panic leaves a line in the log. The hook that was installed before still
/// runs afterwards (the default one prints the panic to stderr, which a release build does
/// not have).
pub fn log_panics(log_dir: PathBuf) {
    let before = std::panic::take_hook();
    std::panic::set_hook(Box::new(move |info| {
        record_panic(
            &log_dir,
            std::thread::current().name(),
            info.location(),
            info.payload_as_str(),
        );
        before(info);
    }));
}

/// Reads the stored settings and starts the runtime with them. The error is the text the
/// fatal-error page shows.
pub async fn start_runtime(data_dir: &Path) -> Result<(Arc<Runtime>, Settings), String> {
    let settings = settings::load(data_dir).await.map_err(|e| {
        format!(
            "Dots could not read its settings in {}: {e}",
            data_dir.display()
        )
    })?;
    let runtime = Runtime::start(Config::from_settings(data_dir, &settings))
        .await
        .map_err(|e| format!("Dots could not start: {e}"))?;
    Ok((Arc::new(runtime), settings))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::state::{runtime_of, AppState};

    /// `%LOCALAPPDATA%` as Tauri found it.
    fn found(local: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
        Ok(local.to_path_buf())
    }

    /// The text of the only file in `log_dir`, which must be one of the logger's daily files.
    fn only_log_file(log_dir: &Path) -> String {
        let files: Vec<PathBuf> = std::fs::read_dir(log_dir)
            .unwrap()
            .map(|e| e.unwrap().path())
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(files.len(), 1, "{files:?}");
        let name = files[0].file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
        assert!(
            name.starts_with("dots.") && name.ends_with(".log"),
            "{name}"
        );
        std::fs::read_to_string(&files[0]).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn paths_and_the_hidden_flag() {
        let local = Path::new("C:/Users/u/AppData/Local");
        assert_eq!(data_dir(found(local), None), Ok(local.join("dots")));
        assert_eq!(
            data_dir(found(local), Some("".into())),
            Ok(local.join("dots"))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            log_dir(&local.join("dots")),
            local.join("dots").join("logs")
        );
        let args = |a: &[&str]| {
            a.iter()
                .map(|s| s.to_string())
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                .into_iter()
        };
        assert!(start_hidden(args(&["dots.exe", "--hidden"])));
        assert!(!start_hidden(args(&["dots.exe"])));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_data_dir_override_is_made_absolute() {
        let local = Path::new("C:/Users/u/AppData/Local");
        let cwd = std::env::current_dir().unwrap();
        // Compared as text: the core compares stored worktree paths with this folder.
        let given =
            |dir: &str| data_dir(found(local), Some(dir.into())).map(PathBuf::into_os_string);
        assert_eq!(given("smoke-data"), Ok(cwd.join("smoke-data").into()));
        let absolute = cwd.join("elsewhere");
        assert_eq!(
            given(&absolute.to_string_lossy()),
            Ok(absolute.clone().into())
        );
        if cfg!(windows) {
            // One spelling for one folder, however the variable spells it.
            assert_eq!(given("./smoke-data"), Ok(cwd.join("smoke-data").into()));
            assert_eq!(
                given("nested/../smoke-data"),
                Ok(cwd.join("smoke-data").into())
            );
            assert_eq!(
                given(&absolute.to_string_lossy().replace('\\', "/")),
                Ok(absolute.into())
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_local_data_folder_is_a_startup_error_unless_the_override_names_a_folder() {
        let missing = || Err::<PathBuf, _>("unknown path");
        assert_eq!(
            data_dir(missing(), None),
            Err("Dots could not find the local application data folder: unknown path".into())
        );
        assert_eq!(
            data_dir(missing(), Some("".into())),
            Err("Dots could not find the local application data folder: unknown path".into())
        );
        let cwd = std::env::current_dir().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            data_dir(missing(), Some("smoke-data".into())),
            Ok(cwd.join("smoke-data"))
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn single_instance_is_skipped_under_a_data_dir_override() {
        assert!(single_instance(None));
        // An empty value is "not set", as it is for `data_dir`.
        assert!(single_instance(Some("".into())));
        assert!(!single_instance(Some("D:/smoke".into())));
        assert!(!single_instance(Some("smoke-data".into())));
    }

    const CHILD_LOG_DIR: &str = "DOTS_TEST_CHILD_LOG_DIR";

    /// Runs one test of this binary, a `child_…` one below, in a process of its own with
    /// `log_dir` handed over: its stderr can be read there, and it may panic for real.
    /// Returns whether it passed, and its stdout and stderr.
    fn run_child(test: &str, log_dir: &Path) -> (bool, String, String) {
        let child = std::process::Command::new(std::env::current_exe().unwrap())
            .args(["--exact", &format!("startup::tests::{test}"), "--nocapture"])
            .env(CHILD_LOG_DIR, log_dir)
            .output()
            .unwrap();
        (
            child.status.success(),
            String::from_utf8_lossy(&child.stdout).into_owned(),
            String::from_utf8_lossy(&child.stderr).into_owned(),
        )
    }

    /// Does nothing on its own; see `the_log_folder_is_created_before_the_appender`.
    #[test]
    fn child_opens_the_log() {
        if let Some(dir) = std::env::var_os(CHILD_LOG_DIR) {
            assert!(appender(Path::new(&dir)).is_some());
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_log_folder_is_created_before_the_appender() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let logs = dir.path().join("dots").join("logs");
        let (passed, out, err) = run_child("child_opens_the_log", &logs);
        assert!(passed && out.contains("1 passed"), "{out}{err}");
        assert_eq!(only_log_file(&logs), "");
        // The appender looks for old files before it opens today's, and reports a folder
        // that is not there yet as an error.
        assert_eq!(err, "");
    }

    /// Does nothing on its own; see `a_real_panic_reaches_the_log_and_then_the_hook_before`.
    #[test]
    fn child_panics() {
        if let Some(dir) = std::env::var_os(CHILD_LOG_DIR) {
            log_panics(PathBuf::from(dir));
            panic!("boom from the child");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_real_panic_reaches_the_log_and_then_the_hook_before() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let logs = dir.path().join("logs");
        let (passed, out, err) = run_child("child_panics", &logs);
        assert!(!passed && out.contains("1 failed"), "{out}{err}");
        let text = only_log_file(&logs);
        let line = text.strip_suffix('\n').unwrap();
        assert!(!line.contains('\n'), "{text}");
        assert!(line.contains(" ERROR dots_app: thread '"), "{text}");
        assert!(line.contains("startup.rs:"), "{text}");
        assert!(line.ends_with(": boom from the child"), "{text}");
        // The default hook still printed it.
        assert!(
            err.contains("panicked at") && err.contains("boom from the child"),
            "{err}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_panic_is_written_to_the_log_file() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let logs = dir.path().join("logs");
        // The logger's own file for today, with a line in it.
        appender(&logs).unwrap().write_all(b"earlier\n").unwrap();
        let here = Location::caller();
        record_panic(&logs, Some("main"), Some(here), Some("index out of bounds"));
        record_panic(&logs, None, None, None);
        let text = only_log_file(&logs);
        let lines: Vec<&str> = text.lines().collect();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 3, "{text}");
        assert_eq!(lines[0], "earlier");
        let (ts, rest) = lines[1].split_once(' ').unwrap();
        assert!(
            ts.ends_with('Z') && ts.len() == "2026-10-13T08:00:00.000Z".len(),
            "{ts}"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            rest,
            format!("ERROR dots_app: thread 'main' panicked at {here}: index out of bounds")
        );
        assert!(
            lines[2].ends_with(
                " ERROR dots_app: thread '<unnamed>' panicked at an unknown place: (no message)"
            ),
            "{text}"
        );
        // A log folder that cannot be made must not turn one panic into two.
        let file = dir.path().join("not-a-dir");
        std::fs::write(&file, "x").unwrap();
        record_panic(&file.join("logs"), Some("main"), Some(here), Some("boom"));
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&file).unwrap(), "x");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn starts_with_the_stored_settings() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let data = dir.path().join("dots");
        // A port that is free right now, stored the way the Settings screen stores it.
        let port = std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
            .unwrap()
            .local_addr()
            .unwrap()
            .port();
        let store = dots_core::store::Store::open(&data.join("dots.db"))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        store
            .put_settings(&Settings {
                port,
                ..Settings::default()
            })
            .await
            .unwrap();
        store.close().await;

        let (rt, settings) = start_runtime(&data).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(settings.port, port);
        let status = rt.status().await;
        assert_eq!(status.port, port);
        assert_eq!(status.data_dir, data.to_string_lossy());
        // What the tray menu and the exit handler get to shut down.
        let state = AppState::new(Ok(rt.clone()), Some(log_dir(&data)));
        assert!(runtime_of(Some(&state)).is_some_and(|r| Arc::ptr_eq(&r, &rt)));
        rt.shutdown();
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_data_dir_that_cannot_be_created_is_a_startup_error() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let file = dir.path().join("not-a-dir");
        std::fs::write(&file, "x").unwrap();
        let err = start_runtime(&file.join("dots")).await.err().unwrap();
        assert!(err.starts_with("Dots could not read its settings"), "{err}");
    }
}
```

Replace the contents of `apps/dots-app/src/events.rs` with:

```rust
//! Forwards `RuntimeEvent`s to the webview, turns some into OS notifications, and keeps the
//! tray's Inbox count current.

use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
use std::sync::Arc;

use dots_core::events::RuntimeEvent;
use dots_core::model::{Run, RunStatus, TriggerKind};
use dots_core::Runtime;
use tauri::{AppHandle, Emitter};
use tauri_plugin_notification::NotificationExt;
use tokio::sync::broadcast::error::RecvError;

use crate::tray;

/// Every `RuntimeEvent`, as the same JSON (`{"type": "run_updated", ...}`).
pub const RUNTIME_EVENT: &str = "runtime";
/// Events were dropped (the webview fell behind); the UI refetches everything.
pub const LAGGED_EVENT: &str = "runtime-lagged";
/// The tray asks the UI to show a route, e.g. `/inbox`.
pub const NAVIGATE_EVENT: &str = "navigate";
/// The status changed outside the UI (the tray paused schedules); payload is the `Status`.
pub const STATUS_EVENT: &str = "status";

/// Something worth an OS notification.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Notice {
    ApprovalNeeded { run_id: String, tool: String },
    RunEnded { run: Box<Run> },
}

/// Decides which events notify: approval requests, and a run moving from queued or running
/// into succeeded, failed or awaiting approval. Chat runs never notify (the user is watching
/// them). A run first seen in a final state (e.g. marked reviewed) and a parked run closed by
/// a decision do not notify either. A run that parks on an approval request this notifier
/// has announced does not notify a second time: one park, one notification.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Notifier {
    active: HashMap<String, RunStatus>,
    /// Runs with an announced approval request that have neither parked nor ended since.
    asked: HashSet<String>,
}

impl Notifier {
    /// Events were missed (the stream lagged): what this notifier remembers of live runs may
    /// be out of date, so it starts again. A run then notifies only once it is seen queued or
    /// running again.
    pub fn forget(&mut self) {
        self.active.clear();
        self.asked.clear();
    }

    pub fn decide(&mut self, ev: &RuntimeEvent) -> Option<Notice> {
        match ev {
            RuntimeEvent::ApprovalRequested { approval } => {
                self.asked.insert(approval.run_id.clone());
                Some(Notice::ApprovalNeeded {
                    run_id: approval.run_id.clone(),
                    tool: approval.tool.clone(),
                })
            }
            RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated { run } => {
                let prev = if run.status.is_terminal() {
                    self.active.remove(&run.id)
                } else {
                    self.active.insert(run.id.clone(), run.status)
                };
                // A run parks on a request (`ApprovalHub::ask`), and the request was announced:
                // that was the notice for this park. Parking or ending closes the request.
                let told = !matches!(run.status, RunStatus::Queued | RunStatus::Running)
                    && self.asked.remove(&run.id);
                let was_working = matches!(prev, Some(RunStatus::Queued | RunStatus::Running));
                let ended = match run.status {
                    RunStatus::Succeeded | RunStatus::Failed => true,
                    RunStatus::AwaitingApproval => !told,
                    _ => false,
                };
                (was_working && ended && run.trigger != TriggerKind::Chat).then(|| {
                    Notice::RunEnded {
                        run: Box::new(run.clone()),
                    }
                })
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// First non-empty line of `text`, at most 200 characters.
fn first_line(text: Option<&str>, fallback: &str) -> String {
    let line = text
        .and_then(|t| t.lines().map(str::trim).find(|l| !l.is_empty()))
        .unwrap_or(fallback);
    if line.chars().count() > 200 {
        line.chars().take(199).collect::<String>() + "…"
    } else {
        line.to_string()
    }
}

/// (title, body) of the notification.
pub fn notice_text(notice: &Notice, dot_name: &str) -> (String, String) {
    match notice {
        Notice::ApprovalNeeded { tool, .. } => (
            format!("{dot_name} needs approval"),
            format!("{tool} is waiting in the Inbox."),
        ),
        Notice::RunEnded { run } => match run.status {
            RunStatus::Succeeded => (
                format!("{dot_name} finished"),
                first_line(run.summary.as_deref(), "The run succeeded."),
            ),
            RunStatus::Failed => (
                format!("{dot_name} failed"),
                first_line(run.error.as_deref(), "The run failed."),
            ),
            _ => (
                format!("{dot_name} is waiting for approval"),
                "Open the Inbox to decide.".to_string(),
            ),
        },
    }
}

/// Whether the Inbox count may have changed.
pub fn affects_inbox(ev: &RuntimeEvent) -> bool {
    !matches!(ev, RuntimeEvent::RunEvent { .. })
}

pub fn spawn_forwarder(app: AppHandle, rt: Arc<Runtime>) {
    let mut rx = rt.subscribe();
    tauri::async_runtime::spawn(async move {
        let mut notifier = Notifier::default();
        tray::refresh_count(&app, &rt).await;
        loop {
            match rx.recv().await {
                Ok(ev) => {
                    if let Err(e) = app.emit(RUNTIME_EVENT, &ev) {
                        tracing::warn!("emitting a runtime event failed: {e}");
                    }
                    if let Some(notice) = notifier.decide(&ev) {
                        notify(&app, &rt, &notice).await;
                    }
                    if affects_inbox(&ev) {
                        tray::refresh_count(&app, &rt).await;
                    }
                }
                Err(RecvError::Lagged(n)) => {
                    tracing::warn!("UI event forwarder skipped {n} events");
                    notifier.forget();
                    let _ = app.emit(LAGGED_EVENT, ());
                    tray::refresh_count(&app, &rt).await;
                }
                Err(RecvError::Closed) => break,
            }
        }
    });
}

async fn notify(app: &AppHandle, rt: &Runtime, notice: &Notice) {
    let run_id = match notice {
        Notice::ApprovalNeeded { run_id, .. } => run_id,
        Notice::RunEnded { run } => &run.id,
    };
    let name = match rt.get_run(run_id).await {
        Ok(run) => rt.get_dot(&run.dot_id).await.map(|d| d.spec.name).ok(),
        Err(_) => None,
    };
    let (title, body) = notice_text(notice, name.as_deref().unwrap_or("A dot"));
    if let Err(e) = app.notification().builder().title(title).body(body).show() {
        tracing::warn!("showing a notification failed: {e}");
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use dots_core::model::{Approval, ApprovalStatus, RunEventRecord};
    use serde_json::json;
    use RunStatus::*;

    fn run(id: &str, status: RunStatus, trigger: TriggerKind) -> Run {
        Run {
            id: id.into(),
            dot_id: "d".into(),
            root_run_id: id.into(),
            parent_run_id: None,
            trigger,
            payload: None,
            status,
            session_id: None,
            workspace_path: None,
            branch: None,
            base_commit: None,
            summary: Some("\n  All green.\nmore".into()),
            error: Some("exit 3".into()),
            tokens_in: 0,
            tokens_out: 0,
            queued_at: "t".into(),
            started_at: None,
            ended_at: None,
            reviewed_at: None,
        }
    }

    /// Feeds `seq` as RunUpdated events for one run; which of them notified.
    fn notified(n: &mut Notifier, id: &str, trigger: TriggerKind, seq: &[RunStatus]) -> Vec<bool> {
        seq.iter()
            .map(|s| {
                let ev = RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated {
                    run: run(id, *s, trigger),
                };
                n.decide(&ev).is_some()
            })
            .collect()
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_run_notifies_once_when_it_ends_or_parks() {
        let mut n = Notifier::default();
        let m = TriggerKind::Manual;
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "a", m, &[Queued, Running, Succeeded, Succeeded]),
            [false, false, true, false]
        );
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "b", m, &[Queued, Running, Failed]),
            [false, false, true]
        );
        // Parking notifies; closing the parked run after a decision does not.
        assert_eq!(
            notified(
                &mut n,
                "c",
                m,
                &[Queued, Running, AwaitingApproval, Succeeded]
            ),
            [false, false, true, false]
        );
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "d", m, &[Queued, Cancelled]),
            [false, false]
        );
    }

    /// An approval request of run `run_id`.
    fn asked(run_id: &str) -> RuntimeEvent {
        RuntimeEvent::ApprovalRequested {
            approval: Approval {
                id: "a1".into(),
                run_id: run_id.into(),
                tool: "Bash".into(),
                input: json!({}),
                input_hash: "h".into(),
                status: ApprovalStatus::Pending,
                note: None,
                parked: false,
                resolved: false,
                created_at: "t".into(),
                decided_at: None,
            },
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn one_park_is_one_notification() {
        let mut n = Notifier::default();
        let s = TriggerKind::Schedule;
        // The request notifies. When nobody answers and the run parks on it, the user has
        // been told already; closing the parked run after the decision is quiet as before.
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "r", s, &[Queued, Running]), [false, false]);
        assert!(matches!(
            n.decide(&asked("r")),
            Some(Notice::ApprovalNeeded { .. })
        ));
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "r", s, &[AwaitingApproval, Succeeded]),
            [false, false]
        );
        // Each request notifies; the park after the second one still does not.
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "two", s, &[Queued, Running]),
            [false, false]
        );
        assert!(n.decide(&asked("two")).is_some());
        assert!(n.decide(&asked("two")).is_some());
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "two", s, &[AwaitingApproval]), [false]);
        // A request answered while the run waited hides nothing: the run's end notifies.
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "live", s, &[Queued, Running]),
            [false, false]
        );
        assert!(n.decide(&asked("live")).is_some());
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "live", s, &[Succeeded]), [true]);
        // Only the parked run is still remembered, and no request.
        assert_eq!(n.active.keys().collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["two"]);
        assert!(n.asked.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_lag_clears_the_memory_of_live_runs() {
        let mut n = Notifier::default();
        let m = TriggerKind::Manual;
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "a", m, &[Queued, Running]), [false, false]);
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "b", m, &[Queued, Running]), [false, false]);
        assert!(n.decide(&asked("b")).is_some());
        n.forget();
        // "a" may have ended during the gap and be published again by "Mark reviewed".
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "a", m, &[Succeeded]), [false]);
        // The request seen before the gap may not be the one "b" parks on.
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "b", m, &[Running, AwaitingApproval]),
            [false, true]
        );
        // A run seen working after the gap is trusted again.
        assert_eq!(notified(&mut n, "c", m, &[Running, Failed]), [false, true]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn chat_runs_and_runs_first_seen_finished_stay_quiet() {
        let mut n = Notifier::default();
        assert_eq!(
            notified(
                &mut n,
                "c",
                TriggerKind::Chat,
                &[Queued, Running, Succeeded]
            ),
            [false, false, false]
        );
        // e.g. "Mark reviewed" publishes an old run again.
        assert_eq!(
            notified(&mut n, "old", TriggerKind::Schedule, &[Succeeded]),
            [false]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn approval_requests_notify_and_run_events_do_not() {
        let mut n = Notifier::default();
        assert_eq!(
            n.decide(&asked("r1")),
            Some(Notice::ApprovalNeeded {
                run_id: "r1".into(),
                tool: "Bash".into()
            })
        );
        let ev = RuntimeEvent::RunEvent {
            event: RunEventRecord {
                run_id: "r1".into(),
                seq: 1,
                ts: "t".into(),
                kind: "assistant_text".into(),
                data: json!({}),
            },
        };
        assert_eq!(n.decide(&ev), None);
        assert!(!affects_inbox(&ev));
    }

    #[test]
    fn notification_texts() {
        let ended = |s| Notice::RunEnded {
            run: Box::new(run("r", s, TriggerKind::Manual)),
        };
        assert_eq!(
            notice_text(&ended(Succeeded), "nightly"),
            ("nightly finished".into(), "All green.".into())
        );
        assert_eq!(
            notice_text(&ended(Failed), "nightly"),
            ("nightly failed".into(), "exit 3".into())
        );
        assert_eq!(
            notice_text(&ended(AwaitingApproval), "nightly").0,
            "nightly is waiting for approval"
        );
        let ask = Notice::ApprovalNeeded {
            run_id: "r".into(),
            tool: "Bash".into(),
        };
        assert_eq!(
            notice_text(&ask, "nightly"),
            (
                "nightly needs approval".into(),
                "Bash is waiting in the Inbox.".into()
            )
        );
        let long = "x".repeat(300);
        let body = first_line(Some(&long), "");
        assert_eq!(body.chars().count(), 200);
        assert!(body.ends_with('…'));
        assert_eq!(first_line(Some("  \n"), "fallback"), "fallback");
    }
}
```

### Task 6: The contract also holds engine events, error kinds, command names and new-dot defaults

The UI parses every answer and event with strict schemas, checked against a fixture that a Rust test writes. Four things can still drift with no test failing: a new engine event, a new error kind, the name of a command, and the defaults of a new dot.

**Files:**
- Modify: `apps/dots-app/src/error.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/lib.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/contract.rs`, `apps/dots-app/src/confirm.rs` (tests only), `apps/dots-app/src/state.rs` (tests only), `ui/src/test/fixtures.ts`, the generated `ui/src/api/__fixtures__/contract.json`
- Test: `ui/src/api/commands.test.ts`, `ui/src/api/contract.test.ts`, the `#[cfg(test)]` modules of `error.rs` and `lib.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `commands::new_dot_spec`, `CmdError`, the contract test (Plans 3a and 3b).
- Produces:
  - `error::CmdErrorKind`: an enum (`NotFound`, `Invalid`, `Conflict`, `Internal`, `Cancelled`; serialized in `snake_case`) with `CmdErrorKind::ALL`, both made by the private macro `kinds!` from one list. `CmdError.kind` is that enum, no longer a `String`; the JSON is unchanged. `error::KINDS` is removed. A test maps every kind to something that produces it, with no wildcard arm.
  - `dots_app::COMMANDS: &[&str]`: the 35 command names. The private macro `command_list!` in `lib.rs` makes both `COMMANDS` and the handler (`invoke_handler()`, which holds the `tauri::generate_handler!` call) from one list of function names. A command is registered by adding its name to that list and in no other way. A Rust test reads `commands.rs` and checks that the functions marked `#[tauri::command]` are exactly `COMMANDS`.
  - `contract.rs`: `engine_event_sample(samples: &[EngineEvent; 9], e)` matches every `EngineEvent` variant with no wildcard, and each arm names one position of the fixed-length array. A tenth variant in the core does not compile here until it has a tenth sample.
  - Fixture keys `Commands` (the 35 names) and `NewDotSpec` (the answer of the `new_dot_spec` command itself). No existing key changes.
  - UI: a Vitest test calls every wrapper of `commands` against the stubbed `invoke` and checks that the names invoked are exactly `Commands`. `blankSpec` in `ui/src/test/fixtures.ts` is `T.DotSpec.parse(contract.NewDotSpec)`; its name, type and values stay the same.

In `apps/dots-app/src/error.rs`, replace:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CmdError {
    /// One of [`KINDS`].
    pub kind: String,
    pub message: String,
}

/// Every `kind` a command can answer with. `cancelled`: the user declined a native
/// confirmation and nothing was changed.
pub const KINDS: [&str; 5] = ["not_found", "invalid", "conflict", "internal", "cancelled"];

impl CmdError {
    pub fn internal(message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            kind: "internal".into(),
            message: message.into(),
        }
```

with:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CmdError {
    pub kind: CmdErrorKind,
    pub message: String,
}

/// Declares [`CmdErrorKind`] and its `ALL` from one list: a command cannot answer with a kind
/// that the contract fixture, and so the UI's tests, does not list.
macro_rules! kinds {
    ($($(#[$doc:meta])* $variant:ident),+ $(,)?) => {
        /// Every `kind` a command can answer with; snake_case in JSON.
        #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
        #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
        pub enum CmdErrorKind {
            $( $(#[$doc])* $variant ),+
        }

        impl CmdErrorKind {
            /// Every kind, in declaration order.
            pub const ALL: &'static [CmdErrorKind] = &[ $( CmdErrorKind::$variant ),+ ];
        }
    };
}

kinds! {
    NotFound,
    Invalid,
    Conflict,
    Internal,
    /// The user declined a native confirmation and nothing was changed.
    Cancelled,
}

impl CmdError {
    pub fn internal(message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            kind: CmdErrorKind::Internal,
            message: message.into(),
        }
```

```rust
            (Error::NotFound("run x".into()), "not_found", "run x"),
            (Error::Invalid("bad cron".into()), "invalid", "bad cron"),
            (Error::Conflict("busy".into()), "conflict", "busy"),
            (Error::Other("boom".into()), "internal", "boom"),
        ];

```

with:

```rust
    fn core_errors_map_to_kinds_and_plain_messages() {
        let cases = [
            (
                Error::NotFound("run x".into()),
                CmdErrorKind::NotFound,
                "run x",
            ),
            (
                Error::Invalid("bad cron".into()),
                CmdErrorKind::Invalid,
                "bad cron",
            ),
            (
                Error::Conflict("busy".into()),
                CmdErrorKind::Conflict,
                "busy",
            ),
            (Error::Other("boom".into()), CmdErrorKind::Internal, "boom"),
        ];
        for (e, kind, message) in cases {
            let c = CmdError::from(e);
            assert_eq!((c.kind, c.message.as_str()), (kind, message));
        }
    }

    /// What answers with each kind. No wildcard arm: a kind nothing produces does not compile.
    fn produced(kind: CmdErrorKind) -> CmdError {
        match kind {
            CmdErrorKind::NotFound => Error::NotFound("x".into()).into(),
            CmdErrorKind::Invalid => Error::Invalid("x".into()).into(),
            CmdErrorKind::Conflict => Error::Conflict("x".into()).into(),
            CmdErrorKind::Internal => CmdError::internal("x"),
            CmdErrorKind::Cancelled => CmdError::cancelled("x"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_listed_kind_is_produced_by_something() {
        for &kind in CmdErrorKind::ALL {
            assert_eq!(produced(kind).kind, kind);
        }
    }
```

```rust
/// The commands of `commands.rs`, listed once: `invoke_handler` registers exactly these, and
/// `COMMANDS` names them in the contract fixture, where the UI's tests compare them with the
/// names `ui/src/api/commands.ts` invokes.
macro_rules! command_list {
    ($($name:ident),+ $(,)?) => {
        /// The name of every registered command, as the UI invokes it.
        pub const COMMANDS: &[&str] = &[ $( stringify!($name) ),+ ];

        fn invoke_handler() -> impl Fn(tauri::ipc::Invoke) -> bool + Send + Sync + 'static {
            tauri::generate_handler![ $( commands::$name ),+ ]
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    /// The name of every function in `commands.rs` that is marked as a command. Only the plain
    /// attribute counts: with `rename`, the name the UI invokes would not be the function's
    /// name, which is what `COMMANDS` lists.
    fn defined() -> Vec<&'static str> {
        include_str!("commands.rs")
            .split("#[tauri::command]")
            .skip(1)
            .map(|after| {
                let name = after.split_once("fn ").expect("a command is a function").1;
                &name[..name.find('(').expect("a function has parameters")]
            })
            .collect()
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_command_in_commands_rs_is_registered_once() {
        let mut defined = defined();
        let mut registered = super::COMMANDS.to_vec();
        defined.sort_unstable();
        registered.sort_unstable();
        assert_eq!(registered, defined);
    }
}
```

```rust
//! One sample of every type the UI receives or sends, written to
//! `ui/src/api/__fixtures__/contract.json`. The UI's contract test parses each sample with its
//! schema, so a field renamed or added on one side fails the other side's tests.
//! Regenerate after changing a type: `UPDATE_CONTRACT=1 cargo test -p dots-app contract`.

use dots_core::engine::EngineEvent;
use dots_core::events::RuntimeEvent;
use dots_core::model::*;
use dots_core::policy::{Action, Policy, Preset};
use dots_core::settings::{Settings, MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS};
use serde_json::{json, Value};

use crate::error::{CmdError, CmdErrorKind};
use crate::state::StartupError;

const TS: &str = "2026-10-09T12:00:00.000Z";

fn to(v: impl serde::Serialize) -> Value {
    serde_json::to_value(v).expect("sample serializes")
}

fn spec() -> DotSpec {
    let mut s = DotSpec::new(
        "nightly-tests",
        "Run the tests and summarize failures.",
        "C:/dev/app",
    );
    s.schedule = Some("0 0 2 * * *".into());
    s.mcp_servers = Some(json!({"github": {"type": "http", "url": "http://127.0.0.1:9000/mcp"}}));
    s
}

fn local_spec() -> DotSpec {
    let mut s = DotSpec::new("notes", "Tidy the notes folder.", "C:/notes");
    s.engine = EngineKind::Local;
    s.model = "google/gemma-4-12b-qat".into();
    s.endpoint_url = Some("http://127.0.0.1:1234".into());
    s.workspace_mode = WorkspaceMode::Folder;
    s.policy = Policy::preset(Preset::ReadOnly);
    s
}

fn dot() -> Dot {
    Dot {
        id: "01J0DOT".into(),
        webhook_token: "tok_abc".into(),
        created_at: TS.into(),
        updated_at: TS.into(),
        spec: spec(),
    }
}

fn run() -> Run {
    Run {
        id: "01J0RUN".into(),
        dot_id: "01J0DOT".into(),
        root_run_id: "01J0RUN".into(),
        parent_run_id: None,
        trigger: TriggerKind::Schedule,
        payload: Some(json!({"scheduled_for": "2026-10-09T02:00:00+01:00"})),
        status: RunStatus::Succeeded,
        session_id: Some("sess-1".into()),
        workspace_path: Some("C:/Users/u/AppData/Local/dots/worktrees/01J0RUN".into()),
        branch: Some("dots/nightly-tests/01J0RUN".into()),
        base_commit: Some("0123abcd".into()),
        summary: Some("All tests pass.".into()),
        error: None,
        tokens_in: 1200,
        tokens_out: 340,
        queued_at: TS.into(),
        started_at: Some(TS.into()),
        ended_at: Some(TS.into()),
        reviewed_at: None,
    }
}

fn approval() -> Approval {
    Approval {
        id: "01J0APR".into(),
        run_id: "01J0RUN".into(),
        tool: "Bash".into(),
        input: json!({"command": "git push origin main"}),
        input_hash: "h1".into(),
        status: ApprovalStatus::Pending,
        note: None,
        parked: false,
        resolved: false,
        created_at: TS.into(),
        decided_at: None,
    }
}

fn event() -> RunEventRecord {
    RunEventRecord {
        run_id: "01J0RUN".into(),
        seq: 3,
        ts: TS.into(),
        kind: "tool_call".into(),
        data: json!({"kind": "tool_call", "id": "t1", "tool": "Read", "input": {"file_path": "a.rs"}}),
    }
}

fn status() -> Status {
    Status {
        port: 47321,
        server_error: None,
        claude: ClaudeStatus {
            program: Some("C:/Users/u/AppData/Roaming/npm/claude.exe".into()),
            version: Some("2.1.300".into()),
            tested_version: "2.1.295".into(),
            mismatch: true,
        },
        schedules_paused: false,
        restart_required: false,
        data_dir: "C:/Users/u/AppData/Local/dots".into(),
    }
}

/// The sample of `e`'s own variant. The match has no wildcard arm and the array's length is
/// part of its type, so a new `EngineEvent` variant does not compile until it has a name
/// here and a sample in `engine_events`.
fn engine_event_sample<'a>(samples: &'a [EngineEvent; 9], e: &EngineEvent) -> &'a EngineEvent {
    let [started, text, call, result, usage, raw, message, finished, failed] = samples;
    match e {
        EngineEvent::SessionStarted { .. } => started,
        EngineEvent::AssistantText { .. } => text,
        EngineEvent::ToolCall { .. } => call,
        EngineEvent::ToolResult { .. } => result,
        EngineEvent::Usage { .. } => usage,
        EngineEvent::Raw { .. } => raw,
        EngineEvent::Message { .. } => message,
        EngineEvent::Finished { .. } => finished,
        EngineEvent::Failed { .. } => failed,
    }
}

/// One sample of every engine event, as stored in a run event's `data`, in the order
/// `engine_event_sample` names them.
fn engine_events() -> Value {
    let samples = [
        EngineEvent::SessionStarted {
            session_id: "s-1".into(),
        },
        EngineEvent::AssistantText {
            text: "Running the tests.".into(),
        },
        EngineEvent::ToolCall {
            id: "t1".into(),
            tool: "Bash".into(),
            input: json!({"command": "cargo test"}),
        },
        EngineEvent::ToolResult {
            id: "t1".into(),
            output: "test result: ok".into(),
            is_error: false,
        },
        EngineEvent::Usage {
            tokens_in: 1200,
            tokens_out: 340,
        },
        EngineEvent::Raw {
            line: "{\"type\":\"future\"}".into(),
        },
        EngineEvent::Message {
            message: json!({"role": "user", "content": "Run the tests."}),
        },
        EngineEvent::Finished {
            summary: "All tests pass.".into(),
        },
        EngineEvent::Failed {
            error: "timeout".into(),
        },
    ];
    for sample in &samples {
        assert!(
            std::ptr::eq(engine_event_sample(&samples, sample), sample),
            "the `{}` sample is not at its variant's place in `engine_events`",
            sample.kind()
        );
    }
    to(samples)
}

/// The samples, keyed by the UI schema that parses them. `Enums` lists every variant of every
/// shared enum and `Limits` the validation limits the editor mirrors: the UI parses live data
/// with its schemas, so a variant or limit it does not know must fail a test, not a screen.
/// `NewDotSpec` is the answer of the `new_dot_spec` command, the blank dot of the UI's tests,
/// and `Commands` names every registered command.
pub fn samples() -> Value {
    let settings = Settings {
        local_endpoints: vec!["http://127.0.0.1:1234".into()],
        ..Settings::default()
    };
    json!({
        "DotSpec": to(local_spec()),
        "Dot": to(dot()),
        "Run": to(run()),
        "RunEventRecord": to(event()),
        "Approval": to(approval()),
        "RuntimeEvent": [
            to(RuntimeEvent::RunUpdated { run: run() }),
            to(RuntimeEvent::RunEvent { event: event() }),
            to(RuntimeEvent::ApprovalRequested { approval: approval() }),
            to(RuntimeEvent::ApprovalDecided { approval: Approval {
                status: ApprovalStatus::Denied,
                note: Some("not on main".into()),
                parked: true,
                decided_at: Some(TS.into()),
                ..approval()
            }}),
        ],
        "Status": to(status()),
        "Settings": to(&settings),
        "SettingsSaved": to(SettingsSaved { settings: settings.clone(), restart_required: true }),
        "DotOverview": to(DotOverview {
            dot: dot(),
            active_run: None,
            last_run: Some(run()),
            next_fire: Some("2026-10-10T02:00:00+01:00".into()),
        }),
        "Inbox": to(Inbox {
            approvals: vec![PendingApproval {
                approval: approval(),
                dot_id: "01J0DOT".into(),
                dot_name: "nightly-tests".into(),
            }],
            review_runs: vec![ReviewRun {
                run: run(),
                dot_name: "nightly-tests".into(),
                worktree_exists: true,
            }],
        }),
        "WorktreeDiff": to(WorktreeDiff {
            files: vec![
                FileDiff {
                    path: "src/lib.rs".into(),
                    status: FileStatus::Modified,
                    patch: "diff --git a/src/lib.rs b/src/lib.rs\n@@ -1 +1 @@\n-a\n+b\n".into(),
                    binary: false,
                },
                FileDiff {
                    path: "logo.png".into(),
                    status: FileStatus::Added,
                    patch: String::new(),
                    binary: true,
                },
            ],
            truncated: false,
            exists: true,
        }),
        "CmdError": to(CmdError { kind: CmdErrorKind::Conflict, message: "run 01J0RUN is already succeeded".into() }),
        "RunEventData": engine_events(),
        "NewDotSpec": to(crate::commands::new_dot_spec()),
        "Commands": crate::COMMANDS,
        "Enums": {
            "EngineKind": EngineKind::ALL,
            "WorkspaceMode": WorkspaceMode::ALL,
            "TriggerKind": TriggerKind::ALL,
            "RunStatus": RunStatus::ALL,
            "ApprovalStatus": ApprovalStatus::ALL,
            "Action": Action::ALL,
            "Preset": Preset::ALL,
            "FileStatus": FileStatus::ALL,
            "CmdErrorKind": CmdErrorKind::ALL,
        },
        "Limits": {
            "max_timeout_secs": MAX_TIMEOUT_SECS,
            "max_approval_wait_secs": MAX_APPROVAL_WAIT_SECS,
            "max_concurrent_runs": MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS,
        },
        "StartupError": to(StartupError {
            message: "Dots could not start: database disk image is malformed".into(),
            log_dir: "C:/Users/u/AppData/Local/dots/logs".into(),
        }),
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::path::PathBuf;

    fn fixture() -> PathBuf {
        PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
            .join("../../ui/src/api/__fixtures__/contract.json")
    }

    #[test]
    fn contract_fixture_is_up_to_date() {
        let text = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&super::samples()).unwrap() + "\n";
        if std::env::var_os("UPDATE_CONTRACT").is_some() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(fixture().parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
            std::fs::write(fixture(), &text).unwrap();
        }
        let on_disk = std::fs::read_to_string(fixture())
            .unwrap_or_default()
            .replace("\r\n", "\n");
        assert!(
            on_disk == text,
            "ui/src/api/__fixtures__/contract.json is stale; run \
             `UPDATE_CONTRACT=1 cargo test -p dots-app contract` and fix ui/src/api/types.ts"
        );
    }
}
```

### Task 7: The UI's cache follows the backend in every case

The query client keeps data until a runtime event or a mutation marks it stale. TanStack Query keeps a first load that is in flight when its query is invalidated, and clears the mark when that load succeeds, so an invalidation made during a first load is lost. The same happens to a loaded query with a fetch in flight and nobody showing it. Events emitted before the listeners are attached are lost too. This task closes those gaps and tidies the query keys and the test set-up.

**Files:**
- Modify: `ui/src/lib/query.ts`, `ui/src/lib/runtime-events.ts`, `ui/src/api/events.ts`, `ui/src/screens/DotEditorScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/DotsScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/InboxScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/RunScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/dot-editor/WebhookPanel.tsx`, `ui/src/test/backend.ts`, `ui/src/test/render.tsx`, `ui/src/test/setup.ts`
- Create: `ui/src/test/setup.test.ts`
- Test: `ui/src/test/setup.test.ts`, `ui/src/lib/runtime-events.test.ts`, `ui/src/app/Layout.test.tsx`, `ui/src/api/events.test.ts`, `ui/src/screens/DotsScreen.test.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/DotEditorScreen.test.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/InboxScreen.test.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/RunScreen.test.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `keys`, `invalidateDots`, `eventsQuery` (`@/lib/query`), `applyRuntimeEvent`, `useRuntimeEvents` (`@/lib/runtime-events`), `onRuntimeEvent` (`@/api/events`), the test harness (Plan 3b).
- Produces:
  - `invalidate(qc: QueryClient, queryKey?: QueryKey): void` in `@/lib/query`: marks the matching queries stale (every query when no key is given). For each matching query that is fetching, it marks the query stale again when that fetch has settled, so the mark is not lost. It returns nothing; a caller that must wait for the reload (the chat composer) still calls `qc.invalidateQueries` itself.
  - Every invalidation in `runtime-events.ts` goes through `invalidate` (run updates, both approval events, the gap branch of `run_event`, a lagged stream, an unreadable event). So does `invalidateDots`.
  - `run_updated` also marks `keys.approvals(run.id)` stale.
  - `useRuntimeEvents` refetches everything once when all its listeners are attached. The first screen's queries are therefore asked twice at start.
  - The `status` event (a pause or resume from the tray) also marks `keys.overview` stale.
  - `onRuntimeEvent(handler, onInvalid)`: the second argument is required. The log line for an unreadable event is `event "<name>" of type "<type>" in a shape this UI does not know` when the payload has a text `type`, and carries every zod issue.
  - `keys.newDotSpec`, `keys.schedulePreview(expr)`, `keys.webhookUrl(dotId)` replace three ad hoc keys. `keys.run` and its three writes are removed (no query read it). `keys.runs(dotId: string)` no longer takes `null`.
  - `setDotEnabledMutation` (`mutationKey: ["set-dot-enabled"]`) and `useSwitchingDot(dotId): boolean` in `@/lib/query`. The editor's dot query is disabled while a switch of that dot is pending, so the editor fills from the switch's answer.
  - Test harness: `later<T>()` from `@/test/backend` (`{ answer, give(value), fail(error) }`); `backend.holdListeners(): () => void`; `renderApp(path).listening(): Promise<void>`, which resolves when the app has attached its listeners and finished the refetch that follows. A test that counts backend calls of the first screen waits for it first. Toasts are dismissed after every test.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the test tooling and the failing tests**

with:

```ts
type Listener = (event: { payload: unknown }) => void;

/// An answer the test gives or refuses later: a command still on its way, or one that read its
/// rows before something changed.
export function later<T>() {
  let give!: (value: T) => void;
  let fail!: (error: unknown) => void;
  const answer = new Promise<T>((resolve, reject) => {
    give = resolve;
    fail = reject;
  });
  return { answer, give, fail };
}

class FakeBackend {
  private handlers = new Map<string, Handler>();
  private listeners = new Map<string, Set<Listener>>();
  /// While set, `listen` waits for it before it attaches a listener.
  private attaching: Promise<void> | null = null;
  /// Every command invoked, in order, with its arguments.
  calls: [string, Record<string, unknown>][] = [];
```

with:

```ts
  }

  /// Makes `listen` as slow as the real bridge can be: from now on a listener hears nothing
  /// until the returned function is called, and what is emitted before that is lost.
  holdListeners(): () => void {
    const held = later<void>();
    this.attaching = held.answer;
    return () => held.give();
  }

  reset(): void {
    this.handlers.clear();
    this.listeners.clear();
    this.attaching = null;
    this.calls = [];
  }
```

```ts
  async listen(event: string, listener: Listener): Promise<() => void> {
```

Create `ui/src/test/setup.test.ts`:

```ts
import { act, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { toast } from "sonner";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

import { renderApp } from "@/test/render";

// One check in two tests: the second looks at what the first left behind. sonner hands every
// new toaster the toasts that are still live, so without the clearing in setup.ts a test would
// see the toasts of the tests before it.
describe("the toasts between tests", () => {
  it("shows a toast", async () => {
    renderApp("/dots");
    toast.success("Left over");
    expect(await screen.findByText("Left over")).toBeVisible();
  });

  it("starts without the toast of the test before", async () => {
    expect(toast.getToasts()).toEqual([]);
    renderApp("/dots");
    await screen.findByRole("navigation", { name: "Main" });
    // A new toaster shows the toasts it is handed on a timer.
    await act(() => new Promise<void>((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 20)));
    expect(screen.queryByText("Left over")).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

```ts
  it("marks a list nobody shows stale when a gap opened during its refetch", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    qc.setQueryData(key, [event(1), event(2)]);
    const read = later<RunEventRecord[]>();
    backend.on("list_run_events", () => read.answer);
    // The screen asks for more and closes: the fetch goes on with nobody showing the list.
    const unwatch = watch(qc);
    const refetch = qc.refetchQueries({ queryKey: key });
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(asked()).toEqual([{ runId, afterSeq: 2 }]));

    arrive(qc, 5);
    read.give([event(3)]);
    await refetch;

    // Stale, so the screen that shows it next asks for what is after event 3.
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(stale(qc, key)).toBe(true));

describe("a change announced while a fetch is in flight", () => {
  const empty: Inbox = { approvals: [], review_runs: [] };
  /// Shows the Inbox as the app's frame does.
  const watchInbox = (qc: Client) =>
    new QueryObserver(qc, { queryKey: keys.inbox, queryFn: commands.inbox }).subscribe(() => {});
  const loads = () => backend.callsTo("inbox").length;
  /// The first `inbox` call is answered by the test, every later one with `then`.
  function heldFirst(then: Inbox) {
    const read = later<Inbox>();
    backend.on("inbox", () => (loads() === 1 ? read.answer : then));
    return read;
  }

  it("loads the Inbox again when a run changed during its first load", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    const read = heldFirst(sample.inbox);
    const unwatch = watchInbox(qc);
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(loads()).toBe(1));

    // The first load read an empty Inbox; a run fails and waits for a review before it answers.
    applyRuntimeEvent(qc, { type: "run_updated", run: { ...sample.run, status: "failed" } });
    read.give(empty);

    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.inbox)).toEqual(sample.inbox));
    expect(loads()).toBe(2);
    unwatch();
  });

  it("asks once more after a first load, however many changes it missed", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    const read = heldFirst(sample.inbox);
    const unwatch = watchInbox(qc);
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(loads()).toBe(1));

    applyRuntimeEvent(qc, { type: "approval_requested", approval: sample.approval });
    applyRuntimeEvent(qc, { type: "approval_decided", approval: sample.approval });
    invalidate(qc);
    read.give(empty);

    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.inbox)).toEqual(sample.inbox));
    expect(stale(qc, keys.inbox)).toBe(false);
    expect(loads()).toBe(2);
    unwatch();
  });

  it("asks again after a first load that failed", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    const read = heldFirst(sample.inbox);
    const unwatch = watchInbox(qc);
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(loads()).toBe(1));

    invalidate(qc, keys.inbox);
    read.fail({ kind: "internal", message: "database is locked" });

    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.inbox)).toEqual(sample.inbox));
    expect(loads()).toBe(2);
    unwatch();
  });

  it("restarts a refetch of a list on screen and asks no third time", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    qc.setQueryData(keys.inbox, empty);
    const unwatch = watchInbox(qc);
    const read = heldFirst(sample.inbox);
    void qc.refetchQueries({ queryKey: keys.inbox });
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(loads()).toBe(1));

    // TanStack drops the running refetch and starts one that reads after the change.
    invalidate(qc, keys.inbox);
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.inbox)).toEqual(sample.inbox));
    read.give(empty);
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.isFetching()).toBe(0));

    expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.inbox)).toEqual(sample.inbox);
    expect(stale(qc, keys.inbox)).toBe(false);
    expect(loads()).toBe(2);
    unwatch();
  });

  it("loads the Dots list again when a dot changed during its first load", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    const renamed = { ...sample.overview, dot: { ...sample.dot, name: "renamed" } };
    const read = later<DotOverview[]>();
    const asked = () => backend.callsTo("dot_overview").length;
    backend.on("dot_overview", () => (asked() === 1 ? read.answer : [renamed]));
    const unwatch = new QueryObserver(qc, {
      queryKey: keys.overview,
      queryFn: commands.dotOverview,
    }).subscribe(() => {});
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(asked()).toBe(1));

    // The list read the dot before it was saved under a new name. No runtime event says so.
    invalidateDots(qc);
    read.give([sample.overview]);

    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(qc.getQueryData(keys.overview)).toEqual([renamed]));
    expect(asked()).toBe(2);
    unwatch();
  });

  it("leaves a query that is removed during its first load alone", async () => {
    const qc = createQueryClient();
    const read = heldFirst(sample.inbox);
    const load = qc.prefetchQuery({ queryKey: keys.inbox, queryFn: commands.inbox });
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(loads()).toBe(1));

    invalidate(qc, keys.inbox);
    qc.removeQueries({ queryKey: keys.inbox });
    read.give(empty);
    await load;

    expect(qc.getQueryState(keys.inbox)).toBeUndefined();
    expect(loads()).toBe(1);
  });
});
```

```tsx
  it("refetches after a lagged stream and after an event it cannot read", async () => {
    const { listening } = renderApp("/dots");
    await screen.findByRole("navigation", { name: "Main" });
    await listening();
    const loads = backend.callsTo("inbox").length;
    act(() => backend.emit("runtime-lagged", null));
    await waitFor(() => expect(backend.callsTo("inbox")).toHaveLength(loads + 1));

    const logged = vi.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {});
    act(() => backend.emit("runtime", { type: "from_a_newer_core" }));
    await waitFor(() => expect(backend.callsTo("inbox")).toHaveLength(loads + 2));
    expect(logged).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    logged.mockRestore();
  });

  it("loads a screen again when the stream lagged during its first load", async () => {
    const { user, listening } = renderApp("/settings");
    await screen.findByRole("heading", { name: "Settings" });
    await listening();

    const idle: DotOverview = { ...sample.overview, active_run: null };
    const running = { ...idle, active_run: { ...sample.run, status: "running" as const } };
    const read = later<DotOverview[]>();
    backend.on("dot_overview", () =>
      backend.callsTo("dot_overview").length === 1 ? read.answer : [running],
    );
    await user.click(screen.getByRole("link", { name: "Dots" }));
    await waitFor(() => expect(backend.callsTo("dot_overview")).toHaveLength(1));

    // The first load read an idle dot. Its run starts, and the event that says so is dropped.
    act(() => backend.emit("runtime-lagged", null));
    await act(async () => read.give([idle]));

    const name = `Open the active run of ${idle.dot.name}`;
    expect(await screen.findByRole("link", { name })).toHaveTextContent("Running");
    expect(backend.callsTo("dot_overview")).toHaveLength(2);
  });

  it("asks again once it listens, for what the backend emitted before", async () => {
    const attach = backend.holdListeners();
    let paused = false;
    backend.on("status", () => ({ ...quietStatus, schedules_paused: paused }));
    const { listening } = renderApp("/dots");
    await screen.findByRole("navigation", { name: "Main" });
    await waitFor(() => expect(backend.callsTo("status")).toHaveLength(1));

    // The tray pauses the schedules while nothing listens yet: its event is lost.
    paused = true;
    act(() => backend.emit("status", { ...quietStatus, schedules_paused: true }));
    await act(async () => attach());

    expect(await screen.findByText(/Schedules are paused/)).toBeVisible();
    // Once: listening does not start a second round of questions.
    await listening();
    expect(backend.callsTo("status")).toHaveLength(2);
    expect(backend.callsTo("inbox")).toHaveLength(2);
  });
```

```ts
/// the Inbox (a deleted dot takes its approvals and runs with it) and every list of runs. No
```

with:

```ts
};

type DotSwitch = { id: string; enabled: boolean };

/// The mutation that switches a dot on or off (the Dots list). It has a key so that the dot's
/// editor can see one in flight (`useSwitchingDot`).
export const setDotEnabledMutation = {
  mutationKey: ["set-dot-enabled"] as const,
  mutationFn: (v: DotSwitch) => commands.setDotEnabled(v.id, v.enabled),
};

/// Whether a switch of this dot is on its way to the core. Until it has answered, a copy of the
/// dot loaded from the core may be the one from before the switch.
export function useSwitchingDot(dotId: string): boolean {
  return (
    useIsMutating({
      mutationKey: setDotEnabledMutation.mutationKey,
      predicate: (m) => (m.state.variables as DotSwitch | undefined)?.id === dotId,
    }) > 0
  );
}

/// Marks the queries under `queryKey` stale, every query without one, and loads those on screen
/// again: what follows a change in the backend.
///
/// `invalidateQueries` alone loses the mark of a query whose fetch is in flight and is not
/// restarted, which is a first load, and a refetch of a query no screen shows. That fetch may
/// have read the backend before the change, and its answer clears the mark. So each query that
/// is fetching now is marked again when that fetch has settled. A fetch running by then started
/// after the change, so it is joined, not restarted: several changes share one question. This
/// holds for a fetch that was restarted too: its promise, taken before, settles at once.
export function invalidate(qc: QueryClient, queryKey?: QueryKey): void {
  const fetching = qc
    .getQueryCache()
    .findAll({ queryKey, fetchStatus: "fetching" })
    .map((query) => ({ key: query.queryKey, settled: query.promise }));
  void qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey });
  for (const { key, settled } of fetching) {
    const again = () =>
      void qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: key, exact: true }, { cancelRefetch: false });
    void settled?.then(again, again);
  }
}

/// the Inbox (a deleted dot takes its approvals and runs with it) and every list of runs. No
```

Replace the contents of `ui/src/lib/runtime-events.ts` with:

```ts
import { useQueryClient, type QueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router";

import { onLagged, onNavigate, onRuntimeEvent, onStatus } from "@/api/events";
import type { RunEventRecord, RuntimeEvent } from "@/api/types";
import { invalidate, keys, mergeEvents } from "@/lib/query";

/// Brings the query caches in step with one runtime event.
export function applyRuntimeEvent(qc: QueryClient, event: RuntimeEvent): void {
  switch (event.type) {
    case "run_updated":
      // Lists and views a run shows up in. The prefixes cover every dot's and thread's key. The
      // run's approvals are among them: a run that ends takes its pending ones with it.
      for (const queryKey of [
        ["thread"],
        ["runs"],
        ["diff"],
        keys.overview,
        keys.inbox,
        keys.approvals(event.run.id),
      ]) {
        invalidate(qc, queryKey);
      }
      break;
    case "run_event": {
      const queryKey = keys.events(event.event.run_id);
      const have = qc.getQueryData<RunEventRecord[]>(queryKey);
      if (have === undefined) {
        // Not loaded. A first load in flight may have read the backend before this event was
        // stored, so the list is marked stale, which holds once that load has settled, and then
        // asks for what is after its last event. A list that is not loading either is left
        // alone: it loads in full when asked for.
        if (qc.getQueryState(queryKey)?.fetchStatus === "fetching") invalidate(qc, queryKey);
        break;
      }
      // `seq` counts up from 1 without gaps within a run.
      const last = have.at(-1)?.seq ?? 0;
      if (event.event.seq === last + 1) {
        qc.setQueryData<RunEventRecord[]>(queryKey, mergeEvents(have, [event.event]));
      } else if (event.event.seq > last + 1) {
        // An event in between is missing: ask for everything after the last one loaded.
        invalidate(qc, queryKey);
      }
      break;
    }
    case "approval_requested":
    case "approval_decided":
      invalidate(qc, keys.inbox);
      invalidate(qc, keys.approvals(event.approval.run_id));
      break;
  }
}

/// Subscribes the app to the backend: runtime events patch the caches, a lagged or unreadable
/// stream refetches everything, the tray navigates, and a status changed elsewhere is taken.
export function useRuntimeEvents(): void {
  const qc = useQueryClient();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  useEffect(() => {
    let subscribed = true;
    const refetch = () => invalidate(qc);
    const subscriptions = [
      onRuntimeEvent((event) => applyRuntimeEvent(qc, event), refetch),
      onLagged(refetch),
      onNavigate((path) => void navigate(path)),
      onStatus((status) => {
        qc.setQueryData(keys.status, status);
        // A pause or a resume moves every schedule's next run, which the overview holds.
        invalidate(qc, keys.overview);
      }),
    ];
    // The backend attaches a listener some time after it was asked to, and what it emits until
    // then is lost. Anything loaded so far may be older than such an event, so once every
    // listener is attached, everything is asked for again, one time.
    void Promise.all(subscriptions).then(() => {
      if (subscribed) refetch();
    });
    return () => {
      subscribed = false;
      for (const s of subscriptions) void s.then((unlisten) => unlisten());
    };
  }, [qc, navigate]);
}
```

```ts
import type { RuntimeEvent, Status } from "./types";

function parse<S extends z.ZodType>(event: string, schema: S, payload: unknown) {
  const parsed = schema.safeParse(payload);
  if (parsed.success) return parsed.data;
  console.error(`event "${event}" in a shape this UI does not know`, parsed.error.issues[0]);
  return undefined;
}
```

with:

```ts
import type { RuntimeEvent, Status } from "./types";

const Typed = z.object({ type: z.string() });

function parse<S extends z.ZodType>(event: string, schema: S, payload: unknown) {
  const parsed = schema.safeParse(payload);
  if (parsed.success) return parsed.data;
  // The log names the type that arrived, when the payload has one: a newer core can send a
  // type this UI has no schema for, and the issues alone do not say which.
  const typed = Typed.safeParse(payload);
  const of = typed.success ? ` of type "${typed.data.type}"` : "";
  console.error(`event "${event}"${of} in a shape this UI does not know`, parsed.error.issues);
  return undefined;
}
```

### Task 8: One set of review actions, and a run view that says what is true

"Mark reviewed" and "Discard worktree" exist twice, in the Inbox and in the run view, and the two copies have already drifted once. The run view also says things that are not so: a tool call "running" in a run that has ended, "Took" on a run that is still going, "Open folder" for a folder that is gone, and the same error twice.

**Files:**
- Create: `ui/src/components/ReviewActions.tsx`
- Modify: `ui/src/components/Transcript.tsx`, `ui/src/components/ApprovalCard.tsx`, `ui/src/lib/chat-threads.ts`, `ui/src/screens/RunScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/InboxScreen.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/ChatScreen.tsx`
- Test: `ui/src/screens/RunScreen.test.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/InboxScreen.test.tsx`, `ui/src/screens/ChatScreen.test.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 7 `invalidate`, `keys`, `later`, `renderApp(...).listening()`; `ConfirmButton`, `onSingleClick`, `WorktreeDiff.exists`, `ReviewRun.worktree_exists` (Plan 3b).
- Produces:
  - `<ReviewActions runId branch canReview canDiscard>` in `@/components/ReviewActions`: "Mark reviewed" and "Discard worktree" with the confirmation, both mutations, the toasts and the double-click guard. It renders a fragment with zero, one or two buttons. After either action settles, also after a refusal, it marks `["thread"]`, `["diff"]` and `keys.inbox` stale with `invalidate`. Both buttons are disabled while either action is on its way. The Inbox row and the run view render it and only say which actions their run can take.
  - `<Transcript runs events shownError?>`: a tool call without a result says `running` only while its own run is active, and `no result` in a run that has ended. The last run's stored `failed` event is left out when its text is exactly `shownError`, the error the screen already shows.
  - The run view's meta row says `Running for` while a run of the thread is active and has started, and `Took` after.
  - "Open folder" is not offered for a worktree run once the diff has answered that the worktree does not exist. Folder-mode runs keep it.
  - When the diff failed to load, an ended, unreviewed run still gets "Mark reviewed". "Discard worktree" stays hidden, because only the diff says that the worktree exists.
  - `isActive(run)` in `@/lib/chat-threads` is the only definition; `RunScreen.tsx` and `Transcript.tsx` import it.
  - `ApprovalCard` marks its lists stale with `invalidate`.

Replace the contents of `ui/src/screens/RunScreen.test.tsx` with:

```tsx
import { act, fireEvent, screen, waitFor, within } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

import type { Approval, Run, RunEventRecord, WorktreeDiff } from "@/api/types";
import { backend, later } from "@/test/backend";
import { sample } from "@/test/fixtures";
import { renderApp } from "@/test/render";

const run = sample.run; // a succeeded scheduled worktree run
const folderRun: Run = { ...run, branch: null, base_commit: null };
/// What the core answers for a worktree that was removed.
const gone: WorktreeDiff = { files: [], truncated: false, exists: false };
let seq = 0;
const ev = (data: Record<string, unknown>, runId = run.id): RunEventRecord => ({
  run_id: runId,
  seq: ++seq,
  ts: run.queued_at,
  kind: String(data.kind),
  data,
});

/// Answers the run view's queries for one thread.
function thread(runs: Run[], events: RunEventRecord[] = [], approvals: Approval[] = []) {
  backend.on("run_thread", () => runs);
  backend.on("get_dot", () => sample.dot);
  backend.on("list_run_events", (args) =>
    events.filter((e) => e.run_id === args.runId && e.seq > (args.afterSeq as number)),
  );
  backend.on("approvals_for_run", (args) => approvals.filter((a) => a.run_id === args.runId));
  backend.on("worktree_diff", () => sample.diff);
}

/// Lets what a command's answer set off reach the screen.
const settle = () => act(() => new Promise<void>((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 20)));

describe("the run view", () => {
  it("shows the run's state, numbers and summary", async () => {
    thread([run]);
    renderApp(`/runs/${run.id}`);
    expect(await screen.findByRole("heading", { name: `${sample.dot.name} run` })).toBeVisible();
    expect(screen.getByText("Succeeded")).toBeVisible();
    expect(screen.getByText("1200 in, 340 out")).toBeVisible();
    expect(screen.getByText(run.branch!)).toBeVisible();
    expect(within(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Summary" })).getByText(run.summary!)).toBeVisible();
    expect(screen.getByRole("link", { name: sample.dot.name })).toHaveAttribute(
      "href",
      `/dots/${sample.dot.id}`,
    );
  });

  it("shows the conversation with each tool call folded around its result", async () => {
    thread(
      [run],
      [
        ev({ kind: "assistant_text", text: "Running the **tests**." }),
        ev({ kind: "tool_call", id: "t1", tool: "Bash", input: { command: "cargo test" } }),
        ev({ kind: "tool_result", id: "t1", output: "test result: ok", is_error: false }),
        ev({ kind: "message", message: { role: "user", content: "hidden" } }),
        ev({ kind: "raw", line: "garbled" }),
      ],
    );
    const { user } = renderApp(`/runs/${run.id}`);
    expect(await screen.findByText("tests")).toBeVisible();
    const call = screen.getByText("Bash").closest("details")!;
    expect(call).not.toHaveAttribute("open");
    expect(within(call).getByText("cargo test")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(within(call).getByText("done")).toBeInTheDocument();
    await user.click(screen.getByText("Bash"));
    expect(call).toHaveAttribute("open");
    expect(within(call).getByText("test result: ok")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText("hidden")).toBeNull();
    expect(screen.getByText("Unparsed output").closest("details")).not.toHaveAttribute("open");
  });

  it("says that a tool call without a result is running only while its run is going", async () => {
    // The first run was stopped in the middle of a call; the second is in the middle of one.
    const stopped: Run = { ...folderRun, id: "run-a", status: "failed", error: "timeout after 5s" };
    const live: Run = {
      ...folderRun,
      id: "run-b",
      parent_run_id: "run-a",
      trigger: "resume",
      status: "running",
      ended_at: null,
      summary: null,
    };
    thread(
      [stopped, live],
      [
        ev({ kind: "tool_call", id: "t1", tool: "Bash", input: { command: "sleep 60" } }, "run-a"),
        ev({ kind: "tool_call", id: "t2", tool: "Read", input: { file_path: "a.rs" } }, "run-b"),
      ],
    );
    renderApp("/runs/run-b");
    const first = (await screen.findByText("Bash")).closest("details")!;
    const second = (await screen.findByText("Read")).closest("details")!;
    expect(within(first).getByText("no result")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(within(first).queryByText("running")).toBeNull();
    expect(within(second).getByText("running")).toBeInTheDocument();

    const cancelled: Run = { ...live, status: "cancelled", ended_at: run.ended_at };
    backend.on("run_thread", () => [stopped, cancelled]);
    act(() => backend.emit("runtime", { type: "run_updated", run: cancelled }));
    expect(
      await within(screen.getByText("Read").closest("details")!).findByText("no result"),
    ).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText("running")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("says how long a run has been going, and how long it took once it has ended", async () => {
    const started = Date.now() - 4000;
    const live: Run = {
      ...folderRun,
      status: "running",
      started_at: new Date(started).toISOString(),
      ended_at: null,
      summary: null,
    };
    thread([live]);
    renderApp(`/runs/${run.id}`);
    expect((await screen.findByText("Running for")).nextElementSibling).toHaveTextContent(/^\ds$/);
    expect(screen.queryByText("Took")).toBeNull();

    const done: Run = {
      ...live,
      status: "succeeded",
      ended_at: new Date(started + 300_000).toISOString(),
```
