### Task 11: The smoke test of Plan 3 — a helper that clicks, the document, the agent's steps run

Plan 3b ended with a short smoke that called commands through `invoke` and never clicked. This task writes the full smoke of Plan 3 as one document in two parts: Part A is 29 steps an agent runs from a terminal against the real app, each with its result; Part B is 48 steps that need a person (the real `claude` CLI, the tray, a toast, autostart, the native dialog, the installer), left as a checklist. To run Part A the window helper has to click, type and wait like a person, and it has to say what went wrong in one line; the review of Plan 3b found three ways in which it did not.

**Files:**
- Modify: `tools/smoke/webview.mjs`
- Create: `tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`, `docs/verification/2026-10-13-plan3-smoke.md`
- Test: `tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the finished app of Tasks 1 to 10; `DOTS_DATA_DIR`, `DOTS_CONFIRM` (debug builds only), `FAKE_CLAUDE_SCRIPT`, `FAKE_CLAUDE_VERSION`; the fake CLI's `orphan` step (Task 1); the 35 commands (Task 6); the labels and texts of Tasks 7 to 9; LM Studio's CLI at `~/.lmstudio/bin/lms.exe` with the model `google/gemma-4-12b-qat` already downloaded.
- Produces:
  - `node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> <action> …` (Node 22 or newer). Exit codes: 0 done, 1 failed with one line on stderr, 2 wrong arguments with the usage line.

    | Action | Output on success |
    | --- | --- |
    | `eval "<async JS expression>"` | the value as JSON (unchanged); `invoke` is the Tauri bridge |
    | `text` | the page's visible text (unchanged) |
    | `goto <route>` | nothing (unchanged; no leading slash) |
    | `shot <file.png>` | `saved <file>` (unchanged) |
    | `click "<name>" [n]` | `clicked "<name>"` |
    | `dblclick "<name>" [n]` | `clicked "<name>"; the second click met "<name2>"`, or `…met "<name2>", which is disabled`, or `…met no control` |
    | `fill "<name>" "<text>"` | `filled "<name>"` (the text must not be empty) |
    | `wait "<text>" [seconds]` | nothing; 15 seconds unless given, polled every 250 ms |

  - A control's name for `click` and `dblclick`: its `aria-label`, else its text, else its label's text; among the visible `button` and `a[href]` elements (Radix tabs and switches are buttons). `n` counts from 1 in page order and is needed when several controls have the name. The click is a real mouse event sent through the debugger (`Input.dispatchMouseEvent`), so a disabled or covered control is refused and the page's click event has the real `detail` (1, then 2).
  - A field's name for `fill`: its `aria-label`, else its label's text, else its placeholder; among the visible `input` and `textarea` elements. The text is typed with `Input.insertText`, so React's `onChange` runs. A `select` cannot be filled.
  - The error lines: `the debugger refused <method>: <message>`; `the page closed before it answered` (unchanged; still the expected answer of `invoke("restart_app")`); `the page threw: <JSON of what was thrown>`; `nothing answers on debugging port <p>: start the app with WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=<p>`; `no page on debugging port <p>`; `could not connect to the page on debugging port <p>`; `the debugger gave no picture`; `no control named "<name>" (the page has: "<a>", "<b>", …)`; `<k> controls are named "<name>": add a number, 1 to <k>`; `only <k> controls are named "<name>"`; `"<name>" is disabled`; `"<name>" is covered by another element`; `<k> fields are named "<name>" (the page has: …)`; `"<name>" cannot be typed in`; `"<name>" holds "<value>" after the typing`; `the page did not show "<text>" within <s> s`.
  - On a page that is not the app yet, `invoke` is `undefined` and the helper does not throw, so `text` and `wait` work while the app loads.
  - `tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`: 15 tests with `node:test` against a stand-in for the debugging port (an HTTP server and a hand-written WebSocket answerer; no dependency). They pin the calls that go out and every way to fail. They run no page: what `click` finds in a real page is shown by Part A.
  - `docs/verification/2026-10-13-plan3-smoke.md`: "How the agent steps were run", Part A (A1 to A29, each a `bash` block, an "Expect" line and a "Result" line), the table of the 35 commands, "Findings of Part A", "Notes on this run", "What Part A does not show", Part B (P1 to P8, unticked).

Rules of this task, on top of the Global Constraints:

- The app is never started without `DOTS_DATA_DIR` and `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` pointing into a fresh temporary folder, and never with the real `claude` on its `PATH`. The blocks of Part A see to all three; do not start the app by hand.
- Keep the guards of the blocks word for word: `set -u` at the top of the state file, `. "${E:?}"` at the top of every block, `[ -d "$WS" ] && { … }` in A8. A block that ran with an empty variable while these steps were written put two files and a junction into Git's own folder.
- When A1 prints `STOP: a Dots process is running`, run nothing more and stop nothing: report it. The later blocks find the app by its image name, so a Dots that somebody else started would be ended by A21 and A29.
- Part B is not yours. Do not tick a box, do not run the installer, do not start the real `claude`.
- This task changes no application code. A step whose output differs from its "Expect" line is a finding: write it down as it was and report it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the helper's failing tests**

Create `tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`:

```js
// Tests of webview.mjs against a stand-in for WebView2's debugging port: an HTTP server that
// lists one page, and a WebSocket on it that answers each DevTools call as the test says.
//
//   node --test tools/smoke/
//
// The stand-in runs no page: what `click` and `wait` look for in the page is checked by the
// smoke run itself (docs/verification). Here: the calls that go out, and every way to fail.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { execFile } from "node:child_process";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { existsSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { test } from "node:test";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const HELPER = fileURLToPath(new URL("./webview.mjs", import.meta.url));

/// One WebSocket frame from the start of `bytes`, or `null` while it is incomplete.
function readFrame(bytes) {
  if (bytes.length < 2) return null;
  let length = bytes[1] & 0x7f;
  let at = 2;
  if (length === 126) {
    if (bytes.length < 4) return null;
    length = bytes.readUInt16BE(2);
    at = 4;
  } else if (length === 127) {
    if (bytes.length < 10) return null;
    length = Number(bytes.readBigUInt64BE(2));
    at = 10;
  }
  const masked = (bytes[1] & 0x80) !== 0;
  const end = at + (masked ? 4 : 0) + length;
  if (bytes.length < end) return null;
  const payload = Buffer.from(bytes.subarray(end - length, end));
  if (masked) for (let i = 0; i < length; i++) payload[i] ^= bytes[at + (i % 4)];
  return { opcode: bytes[0] & 0x0f, text: payload.toString("utf8"), size: end };
}

function textFrame(text) {
  const payload = Buffer.from(text, "utf8");
  const head =
    payload.length < 126
      ? Buffer.from([0x81, payload.length])
      : Buffer.from([0x81, 126, payload.length >> 8, payload.length & 0xff]);
  return Buffer.concat([head, payload]);
}

/// Starts the stand-in. `answer(method, params)` gives the reply to one call: `{ result }`,
/// `{ error }`, or nothing for a call that is never answered. `targets` replaces the page list.
/// It is closed when the test `t` ends, passed or failed: an open server would keep the test
/// process from ending.
async function browser(t, answer, targets) {
  const calls = [];
  const sockets = new Set();
  const server = createServer((_request, response) => {
    const list = targets ?? [
      { type: "page", webSocketDebuggerUrl: `ws://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}/page` },
    ];
    response.setHeader("content-type", "application/json");
    response.end(JSON.stringify(list));
  });
  server.on("upgrade", (request, socket) => {
    sockets.add(socket);
    const accept = createHash("sha1")
      .update(`${request.headers["sec-websocket-key"]}258EAFA5-E914-47DA-95CA-C5AB0DC85B11`)
      .digest("base64");
    socket.write(
      "HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols\r\nUpgrade: websocket\r\nConnection: Upgrade\r\n" +
        `Sec-WebSocket-Accept: ${accept}\r\n\r\n`,
    );
    let waiting = Buffer.alloc(0);
    socket.on("data", (chunk) => {
      waiting = Buffer.concat([waiting, chunk]);
      for (let frame = readFrame(waiting); frame; frame = readFrame(waiting)) {
        waiting = waiting.subarray(frame.size);
        if (frame.opcode !== 1) continue;
        const call = JSON.parse(frame.text);
        calls.push(call);
        const reply = answer(call.method, call.params);
        if (reply) socket.write(textFrame(JSON.stringify({ id: call.id, ...reply })));
      }
    });
    socket.on("error", () => {});
  });
  await new Promise((resolve) => server.listen(0, "127.0.0.1", resolve));
  const port = server.address().port;
  let closed;
  const close = () => {
    closed ??= new Promise((resolve) => {
      for (const socket of sockets) socket.destroy();
      server.close(resolve);
      server.closeAllConnections();
    });
    return closed;
  };
  t.after(close);
  return { port, calls, close };
}

/// Runs the helper and returns its exit code and what it printed. A helper that does not end
/// is stopped after 20 s, and its code is then `null`.
function helper(...args) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    const options = { timeout: 20_000 };
    execFile(process.execPath, [HELPER, ...args.map(String)], options, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
      resolve({ code: error ? error.code : 0, stdout, stderr });
    });
  });
}

/// The reply to `Runtime.evaluate` when the page's expression gave `value`, and when it threw.
const evaluated = (value) => ({ result: { result: { value: JSON.stringify({ value }) } } });
const threw = (thrown) => ({ result: { result: { value: JSON.stringify({ thrown }) } } });

/// `text` is one line, and not a stack trace.
function assertOneLine(text) {
  assert.match(text, /^[^\n]+\n$/);
  assert.doesNotMatch(text, /^\s+at /m);
}

test("eval prints the value the page gave", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, () => evaluated({ port: 47321, server_error: null }));
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "eval", 'invoke("status")');
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 0,
    stdout: '{\n  "port": 47321,\n  "server_error": null\n}\n',
    stderr: "",
  });
  assert.equal(fake.calls[0].method, "Runtime.evaluate");
  assert.ok(fake.calls[0].params.expression.includes('invoke("status")'));
});

test("a call the debugger refuses is an error, not 'undefined'", async (t) => {
  const refusal = { error: { code: -32000, message: "Execution context was destroyed." } };
  const fake = await browser(t, () => refusal);
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "eval", "1 + 1");
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: "the debugger refused Runtime.evaluate: Execution context was destroyed.\n",
  });
});

test("a command the backend refuses is reported with its kind and message", async (t) => {
  // A refused `invoke` rejects with an object, which the debugger describes only as "Object":
  // the page hands over what was thrown itself.
  const fake = await browser(t, () => threw({ kind: "not_found", message: "dot x not found" }));
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "eval", "invoke('get_dot', { id: 'x' })");
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: 'the page threw: {"kind":"not_found","message":"dot x not found"}\n',
  });
  assert.ok(fake.calls[0].params.expression.includes("catch"));
});

test("an expression the page cannot run is reported", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, () => ({
    result: {
      exceptionDetails: {
        text: "Uncaught",
        exception: { description: "SyntaxError: Unexpected token ')'" },
      },
    },
  }));
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "eval", "invoke(");
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: "the page threw: \"SyntaxError: Unexpected token ')'\"\n",
  });
});

test("shot saves the picture the debugger gave", async (t) => {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "webview-test-"));
  const file = join(dir, "page.png");
  const fake = await browser(t, () => ({ result: { data: Buffer.from("png").toString("base64") } }));
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "shot", file);
  await fake.close();
  assert.equal(fake.calls[0].method, "Page.captureScreenshot");
  assert.deepEqual(run, { code: 0, stdout: `saved ${file}\n`, stderr: "" });
  assert.equal(readFileSync(file, "utf8"), "png");
  rmSync(dir, { recursive: true });
});

test("shot says why a capture failed and writes no file", async (t) => {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "webview-test-"));
  const file = join(dir, "page.png");
  const fake = await browser(t, () => ({ error: { code: -32000, message: "Unable to capture" } }));
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "shot", file);
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: "the debugger refused Page.captureScreenshot: Unable to capture\n",
  });
  assert.ok(!existsSync(file));
  rmSync(dir, { recursive: true });
});

test("an action without its argument is a usage error, before anything is asked", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, () => evaluated(null));
  const incomplete = [["shot"], ["eval"], ["click"], ["dblclick"], ["wait"], ["fill"], ["fill", "Name"]];
  // And what is not a number where one is expected, and an action that does not exist.
  for (const args of [...incomplete, ["click", "Approve", "second"], ["wait", "Done", "0"], ["press"]]) {
    const run = await helper(fake.port, ...args);
    assert.equal(run.code, 2, args.join(" "));
    assert.equal(run.stdout, "", args.join(" "));
    assert.match(run.stderr, /^usage: /, args.join(" "));
  }
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(fake.calls, []);
});

test("a port nothing listens on is one line that says how to open it", async (t) => {
  // A port that was free a moment ago.
  const fake = await browser(t, () => undefined);
  await fake.close();
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "text");
  assert.equal(run.code, 1);
  assert.equal(run.stdout, "");
  assertOneLine(run.stderr);
  assert.ok(run.stderr.includes(`nothing answers on debugging port ${fake.port}`), run.stderr);
  assert.ok(run.stderr.includes(`--remote-debugging-port=${fake.port}`), run.stderr);
});

test("a debugging port without a page is one line", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, () => undefined, [{ type: "service_worker" }]);
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "text");
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: `no page on debugging port ${fake.port}\n`,
  });
});

test("click presses and releases the left button once where the control is", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, (method) =>
    method === "Runtime.evaluate" ? evaluated({ x: 410, y: 96 }) : { result: {} },
  );
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "click", "Mark reviewed", 2);
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, { code: 0, stdout: 'clicked "Mark reviewed"\n', stderr: "" });
  const [find, ...mouse] = fake.calls;
  // The name and the number reach the page as data, not as code.
  assert.ok(find.params.expression.includes('"Mark reviewed"'), find.params.expression);
  assert.deepEqual(
    mouse.map((call) => [call.method, call.params.type, call.params.clickCount ?? null]),
    [
      ["Input.dispatchMouseEvent", "mouseMoved", null],
      ["Input.dispatchMouseEvent", "mousePressed", 1],
      ["Input.dispatchMouseEvent", "mouseReleased", 1],
    ],
  );
  for (const call of mouse) {
    assert.deepEqual([call.params.x, call.params.y], [410, 96]);
  }
});

test("dblclick clicks twice at one point and names what the second click met", async (t) => {
  let finds = 0;
  const fake = await browser(t, (method) => {
    if (method !== "Runtime.evaluate") return { result: {} };
    finds += 1;
    // First the control is found; after the first click the page is asked what is there now.
    return finds === 1
      ? evaluated({ x: 300, y: 200 })
      : evaluated({ name: "Approve", disabled: true });
  });
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "dblclick", "Approve", 1);
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 0,
    stdout: 'clicked "Approve"; the second click met "Approve", which is disabled\n',
    stderr: "",
  });
  const presses = fake.calls
    .filter((call) => call.method === "Input.dispatchMouseEvent" && call.params.type !== "mouseMoved")
    .map((call) => [call.params.type, call.params.clickCount, call.params.x, call.params.y]);
  assert.deepEqual(presses, [
    ["mousePressed", 1, 300, 200],
    ["mouseReleased", 1, 300, 200],
    ["mousePressed", 2, 300, 200],
    ["mouseReleased", 2, 300, 200],
  ]);
});

test("click reports what the page says about a control it cannot click", async (t) => {
  const fake = await browser(t, () =>
    evaluated({ problem: 'no control named "Aprove" (the page has: "Approve", "Deny")' }),
  );
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "click", "Aprove");
  await fake.close();
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: 'no control named "Aprove" (the page has: "Approve", "Deny")\n',
  });
  assert.equal(fake.calls.length, 1);
});

test("fill types the text into the field and checks that the field holds it", async (t) => {
  let asked = 0;
  const fake = await browser(t, (method) => {
    if (method !== "Runtime.evaluate") return { result: {} };
    // First the field gets the focus; after the typing its value is read back.
    return ++asked === 1 ? evaluated({}) : evaluated("C:/tools/claude.exe");
  });
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "fill", "Program path", "C:/tools/claude.exe");
  assert.deepEqual(run, { code: 0, stdout: 'filled "Program path"\n', stderr: "" });
  assert.ok(fake.calls[0].params.expression.includes('"Program path"'));
  assert.deepEqual(
    [fake.calls[1].method, fake.calls[1].params],
    ["Input.insertText", { text: "C:/tools/claude.exe" }],
  );

  const refused = await helper(fake.port, "fill", "Program path", "another");
  assert.deepEqual(refused, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: '"Program path" holds "C:/tools/claude.exe" after the typing\n',
  });
});

test("wait ends when the page shows the text, and fails when it never does", async (t) => {
  let asked = 0;
  const shown = await browser(t, () => evaluated(++asked >= 3));
  const found = await helper(shown.port, "wait", "Succeeded", 5);
  await shown.close();
  assert.deepEqual(found, { code: 0, stdout: "", stderr: "" });
  assert.equal(asked, 3);

  const never = await browser(t, () => evaluated(false));
  const missed = await helper(never.port, "wait", "Succeeded", 1);
  await never.close();
  assert.deepEqual(missed, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: 'the page did not show "Succeeded" within 1 s\n',
  });
});

test("a page that closes before it answers is reported", async (t) => {
  // As after invoke("restart_app"): the call is never answered and the socket goes away.
  const fake = await browser(t, () => {
    setTimeout(() => fake.close(), 50);
    return undefined;
  });
  const run = await helper(fake.port, "eval", 'invoke("restart_app")');
  assert.deepEqual(run, {
    code: 1,
    stdout: "",
    stderr: "the page closed before it answered\n",
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run them to verify they fail**

Run: `node --test tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`
Expected: FAIL, `tests 15`, `pass 3`, `fail 12`. The three that pass on the old helper: `an expression the page cannot run is reported`, `shot saves the picture the debugger gave`, `a page that closes before it answers is reported`. Among the failures, the three defects of the review: `a call the debugger refuses is an error, not 'undefined'` (the old helper prints `undefined` and exits with 0), `shot says why a capture failed and writes no file` (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'data')`), `a port nothing listens on is one line that says how to open it` (a stack trace that starts with `[TypeError: fetch failed]`).

Name the file. `node --test tools/smoke/` fails on Node 24 with "Cannot find module".

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Replace the contents of `tools/smoke/webview.mjs` with:

```js
// Drives the Dots window from a terminal, for smoke runs without a person at the screen.
// It talks to WebView2's remote-debugging port, which only exists when the app is started
// with WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=<port>.
//
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> eval "<async JS expression>"   print its JSON result
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> text                           print the visible text
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> goto inbox                     open a route (no leading
//                                                                    slash: Git Bash would turn
//                                                                    "/inbox" into a Windows path)
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> shot <file.png>                save a screenshot
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> click "<name>" [n]             click a button, tab,
//                                                                    switch or link
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> dblclick "<name>" [n]          double-click it
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> fill "<name>" "<text>"         replace what a text
//                                                                    field holds
//   node tools/smoke/webview.mjs <port> wait "<text>" [seconds]        wait until the visible
//                                                                    text has <text> (15 s)
//
// In `eval`, `invoke` is the Tauri bridge: invoke("status"), invoke("run_now", {dotId}).
// After invoke("restart_app") the page closes before it answers; the script then says so and
// exits with code 1, which is the expected outcome of that one call.
//
// `click` and `dblclick` take a control's accessible name: its `aria-label`, or else its text,
// or else the text of its label. Only what is visible and enabled is clicked, with the mouse
// events a person's click makes, at the middle of the control. When several controls have the
// name, <n> picks one, counted from 1 in page order. `dblclick` waits between its two clicks
// as a person does, and prints what was under the pointer at the second one: the page may
// have changed by then.
//
// `fill` takes a field's `aria-label`, or else the text of its label, or else its placeholder.
// The text arrives as typed text does, so the page's own handlers see it. It cannot be empty.
//
// Every failure is one line on stderr. Exit codes: 0 done, 1 failed, 2 wrong arguments.
// Needs Node 22 or newer (global fetch and WebSocket).
import { writeFileSync } from "node:fs";

const USAGE =
  'usage: webview.mjs <port> eval "<expr>" | text | goto <route> | shot <file.png> | ' +
  'click "<name>" [n] | dblclick "<name>" [n] | fill "<name>" "<text>" | wait "<text>" [seconds]';
/// The actions, and how many arguments each needs.
const ACTIONS = { eval: 1, text: 0, goto: 0, shot: 1, click: 1, dblclick: 1, fill: 2, wait: 1 };
/// Between the two clicks of `dblclick`: inside the 500 ms Windows counts as a double-click.
const DOUBLE_CLICK_PAUSE_MS = 300;
const WAIT_SECONDS = 15;
const WAIT_EVERY_MS = 250;

const [port, action, arg, extra] = process.argv.slice(2);
const needed = Object.hasOwn(ACTIONS, action ?? "") ? ACTIONS[action] : NaN;
/// The optional number after the argument: <n> or <seconds>. `NaN` when it is not one.
const count =
  needed === 2 || extra === undefined ? undefined : /^[1-9]\d*$/.test(extra) ? Number(extra) : NaN;
const given = [arg, extra].filter(Boolean).length;
if (!/^\d+$/.test(port ?? "") || !(given >= needed) || Number.isNaN(count)) {
  console.error(USAGE);
  process.exit(2);
}

function fail(line) {
  console.error(line);
  process.exit(1);
}

const targets = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/json`)
  .then((response) => response.json())
  .catch(() =>
    fail(
      `nothing answers on debugging port ${port}: start the app with ` +
        `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=${port}`,
    ),
  );
const page = Array.isArray(targets) ? targets.find((t) => t.type === "page") : undefined;
if (!page) fail(`no page on debugging port ${port}`);
const socket = new WebSocket(page.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
await new Promise((resolve) => {
  socket.onopen = resolve;
  socket.onerror = () => fail(`could not connect to the page on debugging port ${port}`);
});
// From here on an error of the socket is a page that went away, which `onclose` handles.
socket.onerror = null;

let lastId = 0;
const waiting = new Map();
socket.onmessage = (message) => {
  const reply = JSON.parse(message.data);
  waiting.get(reply.id)?.(reply);
  waiting.delete(reply.id);
};
// The page goes away when the app restarts or quits: answer what still waits, so the script
// ends instead of hanging.
socket.onclose = () => {
  for (const settle of waiting.values()) settle({ closed: true });
  waiting.clear();
};

/// One DevTools call and its result. Throws when the page closed first, and when the debugger
/// answered with an error instead of a result.
async function call(method, params = {}) {
  const reply = await new Promise((resolve) => {
    waiting.set(++lastId, resolve);
    socket.send(JSON.stringify({ id: lastId, method, params }));
  });
  if (reply.closed) throw new Error("the page closed before it answered");
  if (reply.error) {
    throw new Error(`the debugger refused ${method}: ${reply.error.message ?? "no reason given"}`);
  }
  return reply.result ?? {};
}

/// Evaluates an async expression in the page and returns its value (JSON-encoded there).
/// What the expression throws is JSON-encoded there too: a command the backend refuses
/// rejects with an object (`{ kind, message }`), of which the debugger would only say "Object".
async function evaluate(expression) {
  const result = await call("Runtime.evaluate", {
    expression: `(async () => {
      const invoke = window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__?.invoke;
      try {
        return JSON.stringify({ value: await (async () => (${expression}))() });
      } catch (thrown) {
        return JSON.stringify({ thrown: thrown instanceof Error ? String(thrown) : (thrown ?? null) });
      }
    })()`,
    awaitPromise: true,
    returnByValue: true,
  });
  // Only what the page could not run at all arrives here, such as a syntax error.
  const failure = result.exceptionDetails;
  if (failure) {
    const thrown = failure.exception?.value ?? failure.exception?.description ?? failure.text;
    throw new Error(`the page threw: ${JSON.stringify(thrown)}`);
  }
  const answer = JSON.parse(result.result?.value ?? "{}");
  if ("thrown" in answer) throw new Error(`the page threw: ${JSON.stringify(answer.thrown)}`);
  return answer.value;
}

/// A call of `fn`, which is defined here and runs in the page, with `args` as JSON.
const inPage = (fn, ...args) => `(${fn})(${args.map((a) => JSON.stringify(a ?? null)).join(", ")})`;

/// Runs in the page. Where to click the control named `name`: `{ x, y }`, the middle of it
/// after it was scrolled into view. `{ problem }` when no visible control has the name, when
/// several have it and `nth` picks none, when it is disabled, and when something covers it
/// (as the rest of the page is while a dialog is open).
function locate(name, nth) {
  const clean = (text) => (text ?? "").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  const nameOf = (el) =>
    clean(el.getAttribute("aria-label")) || clean(el.innerText) || clean(el.labels?.[0]?.innerText);
  const controls = [...document.querySelectorAll("button, a[href]")].filter(
    (el) => el.getClientRects().length > 0,
  );
  const named = controls.filter((el) => nameOf(el) === name);
  const quoted = JSON.stringify(name);
  if (named.length === 0) {
    const names = [...new Set(controls.map(nameOf).filter(Boolean))].map((n) => JSON.stringify(n));
    return { problem: `no control named ${quoted} (the page has: ${names.join(", ")})` };
  }
  if (nth === null && named.length > 1) {
    return { problem: `${named.length} controls are named ${quoted}: add a number, 1 to ${named.length}` };
  }
  const el = named[(nth ?? 1) - 1];
  if (!el) return { problem: `only ${named.length} controls are named ${quoted}` };
  if (el.disabled || el.getAttribute("aria-disabled") === "true") {
    return { problem: `${quoted} is disabled` };
  }
  el.scrollIntoView({ block: "center", inline: "center" });
  const box = el.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = Math.round(box.left + box.width / 2);
  const y = Math.round(box.top + box.height / 2);
  const top = document.elementFromPoint(x, y);
  if (!top || !(el === top || el.contains(top))) {
    return { problem: `${quoted} is covered by another element` };
  }
  return { x, y };
}

/// Runs in the page. The control at a point, as `{ name, disabled }`, or `null` for none.
function controlAt(x, y) {
  const el = document.elementFromPoint(x, y)?.closest("button, a[href]");
  if (!el) return null;
  const clean = (text) => (text ?? "").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  return {
    name:
      clean(el.getAttribute("aria-label")) || clean(el.innerText) || clean(el.labels?.[0]?.innerText),
    disabled: el.disabled === true || el.getAttribute("aria-disabled") === "true",
  };
}

/// Runs in the page. Puts the focus in the text field named `name` with all of its text
/// selected, so that typed text replaces it. `{ problem }` when there is not exactly one such
/// field that is visible and can be typed in.
function focusField(name) {
  const clean = (text) => (text ?? "").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  const nameOf = (el) =>
    clean(el.getAttribute("aria-label")) || clean(el.labels?.[0]?.innerText) || clean(el.placeholder);
  const fields = [...document.querySelectorAll("input, textarea")].filter(
    (el) => el.getClientRects().length > 0,
  );
  const named = fields.filter((el) => nameOf(el) === name);
  const quoted = JSON.stringify(name);
  if (named.length !== 1) {
    const names = [...new Set(fields.map(nameOf).filter(Boolean))].map((n) => JSON.stringify(n));
    return { problem: `${named.length} fields are named ${quoted} (the page has: ${names.join(", ")})` };
  }
  const [el] = named;
  if (el.disabled || el.readOnly) return { problem: `${quoted} cannot be typed in` };
  el.scrollIntoView({ block: "center", inline: "center" });
  el.focus();
  el.select();
  return {};
}

/// One press and release of the left button. `clickCount` 2 makes it the second click of a
/// double-click: the page's click event then has `detail` 2.
async function press(x, y, clickCount) {
  const at = { x, y, button: "left", clickCount };
  await call("Input.dispatchMouseEvent", { ...at, type: "mousePressed", buttons: 1 });
  await call("Input.dispatchMouseEvent", { ...at, type: "mouseReleased" });
}

const pause = (ms) => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));

let exitCode = 0;
try {
  if (action === "eval") {
    console.log(JSON.stringify(await evaluate(arg), null, 2));
  } else if (action === "text") {
    console.log(await evaluate("document.body.innerText"));
  } else if (action === "goto") {
    const route = (arg ?? "").replace(/^#?\/?/, "");
    await evaluate(`(location.hash = ${JSON.stringify(`#/${route}`)}, null)`);
  } else if (action === "shot") {
    const shot = await call("Page.captureScreenshot", { format: "png" });
    if (typeof shot.data !== "string") throw new Error("the debugger gave no picture");
    writeFileSync(arg, Buffer.from(shot.data, "base64"));
    console.log(`saved ${arg}`);
  } else if (action === "wait") {
    const seconds = count ?? WAIT_SECONDS;
    const end = Date.now() + seconds * 1000;
    const shows = `document.body.innerText.includes(${JSON.stringify(arg)})`;
    while (!(await evaluate(shows))) {
      if (Date.now() >= end) {
        throw new Error(`the page did not show ${JSON.stringify(arg)} within ${seconds} s`);
      }
      await pause(WAIT_EVERY_MS);
    }
  } else if (action === "fill") {
    const found = await evaluate(inPage(focusField, arg));
    if (found.problem) throw new Error(found.problem);
    await call("Input.insertText", { text: extra });
    const holds = await evaluate("document.activeElement.value");
    if (holds !== extra) {
      throw new Error(`${JSON.stringify(arg)} holds ${JSON.stringify(holds)} after the typing`);
    }
    console.log(`filled ${JSON.stringify(arg)}`);
  } else {
    const found = await evaluate(inPage(locate, arg, count));
    if (found.problem) throw new Error(found.problem);
    const { x, y } = found;
    await call("Input.dispatchMouseEvent", { type: "mouseMoved", x, y });
    await press(x, y, 1);
    let second = "";
    if (action === "dblclick") {
      await pause(DOUBLE_CLICK_PAUSE_MS);
      const met = await evaluate(inPage(controlAt, x, y));
      second = !met
        ? "; the second click met no control"
        : `; the second click met ${JSON.stringify(met.name)}${met.disabled ? ", which is disabled" : ""}`;
      await press(x, y, 2);
    }
    console.log(`clicked ${JSON.stringify(arg)}${second}`);
  }
} catch (error) {
  console.error(error.message);
  exitCode = 1;
}
socket.close();
process.exit(exitCode);
```

One thing the tests hold that is easy to break: the `onerror` handler that reports "could not connect" is cleared once the socket is open. Left in place, it turns a page that closes later (after `restart_app`) into a failed connection.

Run: `node --check tools/smoke/webview.mjs`
Expected: no output.

Run: `node --test tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs`
Expected: PASS, `tests 15`, `pass 15`, `fail 0`, in about 10 seconds.

The document below is the smoke as the plan's author ran it on 2026-10-13, on the prototype of this plan. Write it as it is. Its "Result" lines, its first lines and the sections "Findings of Part A" and "Notes on this run" describe that run; Steps 6 and 7 replace them with yours.

Create `docs/verification/2026-10-13-plan3-smoke.md`:

````markdown
# Plan 3 smoke verification

- Date: 2026-10-13
- Build: debug (`bun run tauri build --debug --no-bundle`), Plan 3c prototype branch at Task 10 plus this task's helper. Windows 11, Node 24.15, Bun 1.3.
- Part A (agent steps): 29 steps, 29 passed (A21 to A23 on their second run, see "Notes on this run"). Every one of the 35 commands answered. 3 findings, none of which fails a step: one cosmetic defect, two observations.
- Part B (person steps): 8 groups, 48 steps, all open. Nothing in Part B was run.
- Screenshots: not in the repository. On this run they are in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\claude\C--Users-<user>-dots\1d266a40-1c86-48ee-b0ed-714351f051b3\scratchpad\plan3c\smoke\` (the folder `SHOTS` below). Each result names its file and says what the picture shows.

## How the agent steps were run

Part A drives the real app from a terminal. Nothing in it needs an eye or a hand on native UI.

- The app is the debug build, started with `DOTS_DATA_DIR` set to a fresh folder under `mktemp -d`. The real data folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dots` is never used. The webview's own profile is moved too (`WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`), so `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dev.dots.app` is not touched and a second instance can have its own debugging port.
- The Claude dot runs the fake CLI (`fake-claude.exe`, copied out of `target/`), set through the `claude_path` setting. `DOTS_CONFIRM=yes` answers the native confirmation in a debug build. The app is started with a `PATH` that has no folder with the real `claude` in it, so Dots never starts the real CLI, not even for `--version`.
- `FAKE_CLAUDE_SCRIPT` names one file, `$SMOKE/script.json`. The fake CLI reads it when a run starts, so a step copies the script it wants over that file first (`use_script`).
- The window is driven through WebView2's debugging port with `tools/smoke/webview.mjs`: `click`, `dblclick` and `fill` act on a control by its accessible name, with the mouse and text events a person makes; `eval` calls a command through the Tauri bridge; `text` and `shot` read the page.
- Every block is run in Git Bash from the repository root. Shell variables do not have to survive from one block to the next: block A2 writes a state file, and every later block starts by reading it (`. "${E:?}"`, where `E` is that file's path, `$SHOTS/env.sh`). A shell that forgets variables sets `E` again before each block.
- Git Bash rewrites an argument that starts with `/` into a Windows path, also inside a quoted JavaScript expression (`split("/")` does not arrive as written). The blocks avoid such arguments: `taskkill //PID`, routes without the leading slash, `slice(-26)` for the id at the end of an address.
- Safety: block A1 says "STOP" when a `dots-app.exe` or `fake-claude.exe` is already running; then nothing more is run, and nothing is stopped. Only processes these blocks started are stopped, by their process ids. The installer and the real `claude` CLI are not run.

## Part A: agent steps

### A1. Nothing is running; build

```bash
tasklist | grep -i -E 'dots-app|fake-claude' && echo "STOP: a Dots process is running" || echo "none running"
bun run tauri build --debug --no-bundle 2>&1 | tail -2
cargo build -p dots-core --bin fake-claude 2>&1 | tail -1
node --test tools/smoke/webview.test.mjs 2>&1 | grep -E '^ℹ (tests|pass|fail)'
```

Expect: `none running`; both builds finish; the helper's 15 tests pass.

Result: pass. `none running`. The debug build finished ("Built application at: …\target\debug\dots-app.exe", 2 min 26 s), the fake CLI built, and the helper's tests gave `tests 15`, `pass 15`, `fail 0`.

### A2. Temporary folders, a scratch repository, the fake CLI's scripts, the state file

`SHOTS` is a folder outside the repository, for the screenshots and the state file.

```bash
: "${SHOTS:?set SHOTS to a folder outside the repository}"
mkdir -p "$SHOTS"; E="$SHOTS/env.sh"
SMOKE="$(mktemp -d)"
mkdir "$SMOKE/data" "$SMOKE/repo" "$SMOKE/scripts" "$SMOKE/outside"
git -C "$SMOKE/repo" init -q -b main
printf 'hello\n' > "$SMOKE/repo/README.md"
git -C "$SMOKE/repo" add .
git -C "$SMOKE/repo" -c user.email=smoke@example.com -c user.name=smoke -c commit.gpgsign=false commit -q -m init
printf 'outside\n' > "$SMOKE/outside/secret.txt"
cp target/debug/fake-claude.exe "$SMOKE/"
# A PATH without any folder that holds the real claude CLI.
CLEAN_PATH=""; IFS=':' read -ra PARTS <<< "$PATH"
for p in "${PARTS[@]}"; do
  if [ -e "$p/claude" ] || [ -e "$p/claude.exe" ] || [ -e "$p/claude.cmd" ]; then echo "left out of PATH: a folder with claude in it"
  else CLEAN_PATH="$CLEAN_PATH${CLEAN_PATH:+:}$p"; fi
done
{
  echo 'set -u'
  printf 'SHOTS=%q\nSMOKE=%q\nCLEAN_PATH=%q\n' "$SHOTS" "$SMOKE" "$CLEAN_PATH"
  printf 'E=%q\nAPP=%q\n' "$E" "$(pwd)/target/debug/dots-app.exe"
  printf 'REPO=%q\nFAKE=%q\n' "$(cygpath -m "$SMOKE/repo")" "$(cygpath -m "$SMOKE/fake-claude.exe")"
} > "$E"
cat >> "$E" <<'EOF'
W="node tools/smoke/webview.mjs 9333"
# A printed JSON string without its quotes.
val() { tr -d '"\n '; }
procs() { tasklist | grep -i -E 'dots-app|fake-claude' || echo "no dots-app.exe, no fake-claude.exe"; }
app_pid() { tasklist //FI "IMAGENAME eq dots-app.exe" //FO CSV //NH | grep -i dots-app | cut -d, -f2 | tr -d '"'; }
# Waits until the app behind a debugging port answers a command: the page exists before that.
wait_page() { for _ in $(seq 1 60); do node tools/smoke/webview.mjs "$1" eval 'invoke("startup_error")' > /dev/null 2>&1 && break; sleep 1; done; sleep 1; }
use_script() { cp "$SMOKE/scripts/$1.json" "$SMOKE/script.json"; }
# A run that leaves a child process behind in $SMOKE/<name> and then works for two minutes.
long_script() {
  mkdir "$SMOKE/$1"
  sed "s#TREE#$(cygpath -m "$SMOKE/$1")#" "$SMOKE/scripts/long.json" > "$SMOKE/script.json"
}
start_app() {
  ( PATH="$CLEAN_PATH" FAKE_CLAUDE_SCRIPT="$(cygpath -m "$SMOKE/script.json")" FAKE_CLAUDE_VERSION=2.1.999 \
    DOTS_DATA_DIR="$(cygpath -w "$SMOKE/data")" DOTS_CONFIRM=yes \
    WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER="$(cygpath -w "$SMOKE/webview")" \
    WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=9333 \
    "$APP" > "$SMOKE/app.out" 2>&1 & )
  wait_page 9333
}
EOF
cat > "$SMOKE/scripts/approve.json" <<'EOF'
[
 {"emit": {"type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "$SESSION"}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": "I'll check the **README** first.\n\n- read the file\n- then push"}]}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "tool_use", "id": "toolu_r", "name": "Read", "input": {"file_path": "README.md"}}]}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_r", "content": "hello", "is_error": false}]}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "tool_use", "id": "toolu_5", "name": "Bash", "input": {"command": "git push origin main", "description": "Publish the branch"}}]}}},
 {"approve": {"tool_name": "Bash", "input": {"command": "git push origin main", "description": "Publish the branch"}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": "Done."}]}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "result", "subtype": "success", "is_error": false, "result": "Checked the README and pushed.", "usage": {"input_tokens": 1200, "output_tokens": 340}}}
]
EOF
cat > "$SMOKE/scripts/plain.json" <<'EOF'
[
 {"emit": {"type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "$SESSION"}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": "The build failed because a test timed out."}]}}},
 {"emit": {"type": "result", "subtype": "success", "is_error": false, "result": "Answered.", "usage": {"input_tokens": 10, "output_tokens": 5}}}
]
EOF
cat > "$SMOKE/scripts/fail.json" <<'EOF'
[
 {"emit": {"type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "$SESSION"}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "tool_use", "id": "toolu_x", "name": "Read", "input": {"file_path": "README.md"}}]}}},
 {"stderr": "boom: the fake CLI gives up"},
 {"exit": 3}
]
EOF
cat > "$SMOKE/scripts/nosession.json" <<'EOF'
[
 {"stderr": "boom: no session"},
 {"exit": 3}
]
EOF
cat > "$SMOKE/scripts/long.json" <<'EOF'
[
 {"emit": {"type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "$SESSION"}},
 {"emit": {"type": "assistant", "message": {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": "Starting a long job."}]}}},
 {"orphan": "TREE"},
 {"sleep_ms": 120000},
 {"emit": {"type": "result", "subtype": "success", "is_error": false, "result": "never reached", "usage": {"input_tokens": 1, "output_tokens": 1}}}
]
EOF
. "$E"; PATH="$CLEAN_PATH" which claude 2> /dev/null || echo "no claude on the clean PATH"; ls "$SMOKE" "$SMOKE/scripts"
```

The `approve` step of `approve.json` is the sixth entry (index 5) on purpose: the fake CLI names that tool call `toolu_5`, the id of the `Bash` call above it. The child process an `orphan` step leaves behind ends by itself after 30 seconds, so a step that counts processes does it within that time.

Expect: one or more "left out of PATH" lines when the real CLI is installed; `no claude on the clean PATH`; the folders `data`, `outside`, `repo`, `scripts`, the file `fake-claude.exe`, five scripts.

Result: pass. Two "left out of PATH" lines (one folder, named twice in `PATH`: the npm folder with the `claude` shim), then `no claude on the clean PATH`. `SMOKE` was `/tmp/tmp.tp5LWc5Owl`, which is `%TEMP%\tmp.tp5LWc5Owl`. It held `data`, `fake-claude.exe`, `outside`, `repo`, `scripts`, and `scripts` held `approve.json`, `fail.json`, `long.json`, `nosession.json`, `plain.json`.

### A14. A scheduled run that parks, approved through the Inbox, continues in a new run

Spec: a Claude dot with a scheduled run and an approval round trip. The schedule is set about a minute ahead in the editor, with an approval wait of 3 seconds so that the run parks.

```bash
. "${E:?}"
use_script approve
$W goto "dots/$DOT"; $W wait "Cron expression"; sleep 1
CRON="$(date -d '+75 seconds' '+0 %-M %-H * * *')"; echo "cron $CRON, set at $(date +%H:%M:%S)"
$W fill "Cron expression" "$CRON"; $W fill "Approval wait (seconds)" "3"; $W click "Save"; $W wait "Saved"
$W click "Dots"; $W wait "Next run"; $W text | grep nightly-tests
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do S=$($W eval "(async () => { const r = (await invoke('list_runs', { dotId: '$DOT', limit: 1 }))[0]; return r.trigger + ':' + r.status; })()" | val); [ "$S" = "schedule:awaiting_approval" ] && break; sleep 2; done; echo "$S at $(date +%H:%M:%S)"
RUN=$($W eval "(await invoke('list_runs', { dotId: '$DOT', limit: 1 }))[0].id" | val)
$W eval "(async () => { const r = await invoke('get_run', { id: '$RUN' }); const a = await invoke('approvals_for_run', { runId: '$RUN' }); return [r.trigger, r.payload, r.queued_at, a.map((x) => [x.status, x.parked])]; })()"
$W goto inbox; $W wait "The run stopped waiting and is parked"; $W shot "$SHOTS/12-inbox-parked.png"
$W click "Approve"; sleep 5
$W eval "(await invoke('run_thread', { runId: '$RUN' })).map((r) => [r.trigger, r.status, r.session_id])"
$W goto "runs/$RUN"; $W wait "Continued after approval"; $W text; $W shot "$SHOTS/13-run-resumed.png"
```

Expect: the Dots list shows the next run at the set minute. Within 75 seconds `schedule:awaiting_approval`: a run with the trigger `schedule`, a payload `scheduled_for` at second 0 of that minute, queued within a second of it, and one approval `pending`, parked. The Inbox card says "The run stopped waiting and is parked. Approving continues it in a new run." After Approve the thread is `schedule succeeded` and `resume succeeded`, both with the session `fake-session`. The run view shows "Scheduled run" with "Bash … failed" (the call was queued) and "Continued after approval" with "Bash … done", "Succeeded", "2400 in, 680 out".

Result: pass. `cron 0 10 7 * * *, set at 07:08:58`. The Dots list: "nightly-tests claude · sonnet Idle 13 Oct 2026, 7:10 am". `schedule:awaiting_approval at 07:10:05`. The run: `"schedule"`, `{scheduled_for: "2026-10-13T07:10:00-04:00"}`, queued at `2026-10-13T11:10:00.010Z`, `[["pending", true]]`. `12-inbox-parked.png` shows the card with the line "The run stopped waiting and is parked. Approving continues it in a new run." above the note field. The thread after Approve: `[["schedule", "succeeded", "fake-session"], ["resume", "succeeded", "fake-session"]]`. The run view: "Succeeded", "Took 6s", "2400 in, 680 out", "Scheduled run … Succeeded" with "Bash git push origin main failed", "Continued after approval … Succeeded" with "Bash git push origin main done"; `13-run-resumed.png` shows the two parts under each other, each with its green badge, "failed" in red in the first and "done" in the second, and no review buttons (the unchanged worktree is gone).

The question's text as expected; `19-restart-asks.png` shows it in the middle of the blurred Settings screen, under the amber restart banner: "Restart Dots now?", "nightly-tests, second-dot have runs that have not ended. …", "Cancel", "Restart now". After "Cancel": `false`, and still `dots-app.exe` 32708 with `fake-claude.exe` 6456 and 16648. After "Restart now": `dots-app.exe` 4312 alone. Then `null`, `false`, `3`, `["failed", "interrupted"]`, `"awaiting_approval"`, `[["nightly-tests", "pending", true]]`; "could not listen": 0; the log: "WARN dots_core::runtime: marked 1 interrupted run(s) as failed". After Approve: `[["manual", "succeeded"], ["resume", "succeeded"]]`.

### A22. The port is held by another program: the banner after about two seconds

Spec: the port-in-use banner (start a listener on the port first). Task 4: the start waits two seconds for the port, then comes up without the server.

```bash
. "${E:?}"
rm -f "$SMOKE/stop-listener"
( cd "$SMOKE" && node -e "const fs=require('fs');const s=require('net').createServer(c=>c.end());s.listen(47321,'127.0.0.1',()=>console.log('holding 47321'));setInterval(()=>{if(fs.existsSync('stop-listener'))process.exit(0)},200)" > listener.out 2>&1 & )
sleep 1; cat "$SMOKE/listener.out"
use_script approve
start_app
grep -E "starting Dots|could not listen" "$SMOKE"/data/logs/dots.*.log | tail -2 | cut -c1-140
$W eval "(async () => { const s = await invoke('status'); const k = await invoke('get_run', { id: '$KILLED' }); return [s.port, s.server_error, [k.status, k.error]]; })()"
$W text | grep "The local server is not running"; $W shot "$SHOTS/20-port-in-use.png"
$W eval "invoke('webhook_url', { id: '$DOT' })"
RUN=$($W eval "(await invoke('run_now', { dotId: '$DOT' })).id" | val); sleep 3
$W eval "(async () => { const r = await invoke('get_run', { id: '$RUN' }); return [r.status, r.error, r.workspace_path]; })()"
touch "$SMOKE/stop-listener"; sleep 1; netstat -ano | grep -c -E '127.0.0.1:47321 .*LISTENING'
```

Expect: `holding 47321`. The log's "could not listen on 127.0.0.1:47321" line is about two seconds after its "starting Dots" line. `status.server_error` is "could not listen on 127.0.0.1:47321: Only one usage of each socket address … (os error 10048)", and the run of A21 is `failed`, "interrupted". The red banner: "The local server is not running: could not listen on 127.0.0.1:47321: …. Webhooks are off and Claude dots cannot run." `webhook_url` answers `conflict`, "webhooks are off because the local server is not running: …". A Claude run fails at once with "local server is not running: port 47321 is in use. Change the port in Settings and restart Dots." and no worktree. Then the listener is gone (count 0).

Result: pass. `holding 47321`. The log: "starting Dots 0.1.0" at 11:12:41.153, "ERROR dots_core::runtime: could not listen on 127.0.0.1:47321: Only one usage of each socket address (protocol/network address/port) is normally permitted. (os error 10048); webhooks are off and Claude dots cannot run" at 11:12:43.197: 2.04 seconds later. Status: `47321`, that text up to "(os error 10048)", `["failed", "interrupted"]`. The banner's text as expected; `20-port-in-use.png` shows it in red over two lines above the Dots list, with "Open Settings" at its right, both dots with a red "Failed" as their last result, and the badge 5 on "Inbox". `webhook_url`: `{"kind":"conflict","message":"webhooks are off because the local server is not running: could not listen on 127.0.0.1:47321: … (os error 10048)"}`. The run: `"failed"`, `"local server is not running: port 47321 is in use. Change the port in Settings and restart Dots."`, `null`. Listener count: 0.

$W fill "Model for http://127.0.0.1:1234" "google/gemma-4-12b-qat"; $W click "Test endpoint"

```bash
. "${E:?}"
LOCAL=$($W eval "(async () => { const spec = await invoke('new_dot_spec'); return (await invoke('create_dot', { spec: { ...spec, name: 'local-dot', engine: 'local', model: 'google/gemma-4-12b-qat', endpoint_url: 'http://127.0.0.1:1234', workdir: '$REPO', instructions: 'Run the command git log -1 --oneline with the Bash tool. Then say in one sentence what it printed.' } })).id; })()" | val); echo "LOCAL=$LOCAL" >> "$E"

$W click "Approve"

$W goto "runs/$RUN"; sleep 2; $W text | sed -n '/^local-dot run$/,$p'; $W shot "$SHOTS/23-local-run.png"

### A26. A local dot: a scheduled run that parks, approved through the Inbox

Spec: a local dot with a scheduled run and an approval round trip. As A14, with the local model.

```bash
. "${E:?}"
CRON="$(date -d '+75 seconds' '+0 %-M %-H * * *')"; echo "cron $CRON, set at $(date +%H:%M:%S)"
$W eval "(async () => { const { id, webhook_token, created_at, updated_at, ...spec } = await invoke('get_dot', { id: '$LOCAL' }); const d = await invoke('update_dot', { id, spec: { ...spec, schedule: '$CRON', approval_wait_secs: 3 } }); return [d.schedule, d.approval_wait_secs]; })()"
for _ in $(seq 1 240); do S=$($W eval "(async () => { const r = (await invoke('list_runs', { dotId: '$LOCAL', limit: 1 }))[0]; return r.trigger + ':' + r.status; })()" | val); [ "$S" = "schedule:awaiting_approval" ] && break; sleep 2; done; echo "$S at $(date +%H:%M:%S)"
RUN=$($W eval "(await invoke('list_runs', { dotId: '$LOCAL', limit: 1 }))[0].id" | val)
$W eval "(async () => { const r = await invoke('get_run', { id: '$RUN' }); const a = await invoke('approvals_for_run', { runId: '$RUN' }); return [r.trigger, r.payload, r.status, a.map((x) => [x.tool, x.input, x.status, x.parked])]; })()"
$W goto inbox; $W wait "The run stopped waiting and is parked"; $W shot "$SHOTS/24-inbox-local-parked.png"
$W click "Approve"; sleep 3
for _ in $(seq 1 150); do S=$($W eval "(await invoke('run_thread', { runId: '$RUN' })).map((r) => r.trigger + ':' + r.status).join(',')" | val); case "$S" in *running*|*queued*|*awaiting*) sleep 2;; *) break;; esac; done; echo "$S at $(date +%H:%M:%S)"
$W goto "runs/$RUN"; sleep 2; $W text | sed -n '/^local-dot run$/,$p'; $W shot "$SHOTS/25-local-run-resumed.png"
$W eval "(async () => { const { id, webhook_token, created_at, updated_at, ...spec } = await invoke('get_dot', { id: '$LOCAL' }); return (await invoke('update_dot', { id, spec: { ...spec, schedule: null, approval_wait_secs: 240 } })).schedule; })()"
```

Expect: the run starts at the set minute with the trigger `schedule`; the model asks for `Bash`, is told after 3 seconds that the call is queued, and ends its turn: `schedule:awaiting_approval` with one pending, parked approval. After Approve in the Inbox a `resume` run starts, the model issues `git log -1 --oneline` again, the grant lets it through without a second card, and the thread ends `schedule:succeeded,resume:succeeded`. The schedule is then removed again (`null`).

Result: pass. `cron 0 20 7 * * *, set at 07:19:14`; `["0 20 7 * * *", 3]`. `schedule:awaiting_approval at 07:22:13`. The run: `"schedule"`, `{scheduled_for: "2026-10-13T07:20:00-04:00"}`, `"awaiting_approval"`, `[["Bash", {command: "git log -1 --oneline"}, "pending", true]]`. `24-inbox-local-parked.png` shows the card "local-dot asked 1 min ago · Open the run" with the line "The run stopped waiting and is parked. Approving continues it in a new run.", above the six runs to review. After Approve: `schedule:succeeded,resume:succeeded at 07:23:12`. The run view: "Succeeded", "Took 3m 10s", "4289 in, 186 out"; "Scheduled run 13 Oct 2026, 7:20 am Succeeded" with "Bash git log -1 --oneline failed" and the model's text "The command git log -1 --oneline was queued for human approval and did not produce any output."; "Continued after approval 13 Oct 2026, 7:22 am Succeeded" with "Bash git log -1 --oneline done" and "The command printed a16e136 init."; `25-local-run-resumed.png` shows the two parts, "failed" in red in the first, "done" in the second, and the summary box with the commit line. No second card was asked for. The schedule afterwards: `null`.

### A27. The local endpoint off, then on

3b review: network off, then on. For a local dot the network is its endpoint: the server is stopped, a run is started, the server is started again. (The same with the machine's network adapter and the real `claude` CLI is person step P1.6.)

```bash
. "${E:?}"
LMS="$HOME/.lmstudio/bin/lms.exe"
timeout 60 "$LMS" server stop < /dev/null 2>&1 | tail -1
RUN=$($W eval "(await invoke('run_now', { dotId: '$LOCAL' })).id" | val)
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do S=$($W eval "(await invoke('get_run', { id: '$RUN' })).status" | val); [ "$S" != "running" ] && [ "$S" != "queued" ] && break; sleep 2; done
$W eval "(async () => { const r = await invoke('get_run', { id: '$RUN' }); return [r.status, r.error]; })()"
$W goto "runs/$RUN"; sleep 1; $W text | sed -n '/^local-dot run$/,$p'; $W shot "$SHOTS/26-local-run-endpoint-off.png"
timeout 60 "$LMS" server start < /dev/null 2>&1 | tail -1
$W eval "invoke('test_endpoint', { endpoint: 'http://127.0.0.1:1234', model: 'google/gemma-4-12b-qat' })"
```

Expect: with the server stopped the run fails within a few seconds (three attempts, 1 and 2 seconds apart) with an error that names the endpoint, and it is shown in the run view; the app stays usable. With the server started again the endpoint answers: "google/gemma-4-12b-qat returned a well-formed tool call".

Result: pass, in 68 s. "Stopped the server on port 1234." The run: `"failed"`, `"local endpoint http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1/chat/completions unreachable: error sending request for url (http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1/chat/completions) (gave up after 3 attempts)"`. The run view: "local-dot run", "Failed", "Mark reviewed", "Took 10s", "0 in, 0 out", that error; `26-local-run-endpoint-off.png` shows the red "Failed" badge, "Mark reviewed", the error in a red-bordered box over two lines, and an empty transcript under "Manual run". The app answered every command after it. "Success! Server is now running on port 1234"; `"google/gemma-4-12b-qat returned a well-formed tool call"`.

## Findings of Part A

1. **Cosmetic defect: a rule's action box is too narrow.** In the dot editor each rule row shows its action as "allo" (`04-new-dot.png`): the box shrinks beside the wide tool field and cuts "allow". "ask" and "deny" fit. The value is right; only its text is cut.
2. **Observation: a parked run's time is headed "Running for".** A run that waits for a parked approval shows the badge "Needs approval" and, beside it, "Running for 3s" (A16, `14-run-parked.png`). Task 8 says "Running for" while a run is active, and a parked run counts as active, so this is as built; the word does not fit a run whose process has ended.
3. **Observation: an interrupted run keeps its unchanged worktree.** The three `second-dot` runs that the restart (A20) and the kills (A21, twice) interrupted are "Failed · interrupted" rows in the Inbox with "Discard worktree" (`22-inbox-local-approval.png`). Their worktree folders were still there at the end, each with an empty `git status`. The spec says so (§8: "App crash/restart mid-run: `failed: interrupted`; worktree kept"); every other ended run in this smoke lost its unchanged worktree.

Nothing else differed from what the steps expect.

## Notes on this run

- The steps were first tried one by one while they were written, then run once from this document against a fresh temporary folder. The results above are from that run, with two exceptions. A2 ran with an earlier `wait_page` (it waited for the debugging port to list a page) and without `2> /dev/null` on its last line. A21 to A23 were run twice: in the first run A22 read the page before the app had loaded it, because the port lists a page about two seconds before the app can answer (the start waits that long for the taken port). `wait_page` now waits until the app answers a command, the state file of the run was changed to match, and A21 to A23 were run again; their results are from that second run. The first A22 had already shown the failed Claude run, the refused `webhook_url` and the listener gone.
- LM Studio was not running before the smoke and its server was off (`lms status`: "Server: OFF"). `lms ps`, called to look at it, woke LM Studio's background service ("Waking up LM Studio service..."), which started the server on port 1234 by itself. So `lms ps` is not a read-only call. A24 loaded the model (about 7 GB of memory), A27 stopped and started the server, A29 unloaded the model and stopped the server, and the service that `lms ps` had started was then ended by its process id. Nothing was downloaded.
- The local model is slow on this machine's CPU: 2 min 32 s to its first tool call in A25, and the scheduled run of A26 was parked 2 min 13 s after it started. The waits in A25 and A26 allow five and eight minutes.
- While the steps were being written, a block without a check on an empty variable wrote two small files and a junction into Git's own folder. They were removed at once and nothing was overwritten. The blocks here stop instead (`set -u` in the state file, `${E:?}`, `[ -d "$WS" ] &&`).

## What Part A does not show

- A notification toast, the tray, close-to-tray, autostart, the native dialogs and the installer: Part B.
- `open_run_folder` opening Explorer, the "Browse…" folder dialog and the "Copy" buttons: Part B (P8). In Part A `open_run_folder` was only seen to refuse a folder that is gone.
- A panic written to the log file (Task 5): the app has no way to make it panic. The test `a_real_panic_reaches_the_log_and_then_the_hook_before` holds it.
- The refetch when the listeners attach (Task 7) and the Dots list holding only the row whose command is pending (Task 9): both last a few milliseconds in the real app. The UI tests hold them.
- The walking tools of the local engine entering a directory unless a deny rule matches it (Task 2): the model was not asked to walk a tree.
- Any behaviour of the real `claude` CLI: Part B (P1).
````

- [ ] **Step 6: Run Part A**

The blocks are run from the document itself, so that the document holds exactly what ran. Two small scripts do that. They are not part of the repository: put them into a new folder outside it, which is also where the screenshots and the state file go.

```bash
SHOTS="$(cygpath -u "$LOCALAPPDATA")/Temp/dots-plan3-smoke"
mkdir "$SHOTS" && echo "$SHOTS"
```

Expected: a path of the form `/c/Users/<user>/AppData/Local/Temp/dots-plan3-smoke`. When `mkdir` says the folder exists, it is left from an earlier run: look at it, and use another name rather than deleting it unseen.

Save this as `extract-steps.mjs` in that folder. It writes the `bash` block of every step of Part A to its own file:

````js
// extract-steps.mjs <document> <out>: the bash block of every "### A<n>." step (also "### A23b.") goes to <out>/<step>.sh
import { mkdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
const [doc, out] = process.argv.slice(2);
mkdirSync(out, { recursive: true });
const text = readFileSync(doc, "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n");
let n = 0;
for (const part of text.split(/^### /m).slice(1)) {
  const id = part.match(/^(A\d+[a-z]?)\./)?.[1];
  const block = part.match(/```bash\n([\s\S]*?)```/)?.[1];
  if (!id || !block) continue;
  writeFileSync(`${out}/${id}.sh`, block);
  n += 1;
}
console.log(`${n} blocks`);
````

Save this as `run-step.sh` in the same folder:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# run-step.sh <step>: runs one block of Part A and keeps its output. Call it from the repo root.
SHOTS="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
node "$SHOTS/extract-steps.mjs" docs/verification/2026-10-13-plan3-smoke.md "$SHOTS/steps" || exit 1
export SHOTS E="$SHOTS/env.sh"
bash "$SHOTS/steps/$1.sh" 2>&1 | tee "$SHOTS/steps/$1.out"
```

Then run the steps in their order, each as its own command from the repo root, and read each output against the step's "Expect" line before you run the next:

```bash
bash "$(cygpath -u "$LOCALAPPDATA")/Temp/dots-plan3-smoke/run-step.sh" A1
```

and so on to `A29`. Every call first prints `29 blocks`. Nothing but the state file `env.sh` in that folder carries over from one step to the next, so a shell that forgets its variables between commands is fine.

What to know while they run:

- The whole of Part A took about 20 minutes on the author's machine: A1 (the debug build) 3, A14 (it waits for the next minute) 2, A24 to A27 (LM Studio) 11.
- A25 waits up to five minutes for the local model's first tool call and A26 up to eight for the park; on a CPU the model needed about two and a half. A26 can run longer than ten minutes: give it a long timeout, or run it in the background and read `steps/A26.out`.
- A24 starts LM Studio's server and loads the model (about 7 GB of memory). A29 unloads the model and stops the server. It does not end LM Studio's background service, which `lms` wakes; say in your report that it was left running. When `lms.exe` is missing or the model is not downloaded, do not download anything: skip A24 to A27, write "not run: <why>" as their results, and report it.
- A12 proves the double-click guard only when `dblclick` prints `the second click met "Approve"` without `, which is disabled`. Its "Expect" line says so.
- A15, A20 and A21 count processes while the fake CLI's left-behind child is alive, which is 30 seconds. On a slow machine a count can be one lower than expected; the count going to `no dots-app.exe, no fake-claude.exe` is what shows the kill.
- Each approval request and each finished or failed run shows a real Windows notification on the desktop. Nobody has to answer them.
- With the app's port taken (A22), the debugging port lists a page about two seconds before the app answers. The blocks wait with `wait_page`, which asks `invoke("startup_error")` until it answers.
- A failed step leaves the app and its temporary folder as they are. When you stop there, run `A29` to end the app and remove the folder, and check with `tasklist | grep -i -E 'dots-app|fake-claude'` that nothing is left.

## What changed while this plan was executed

The plan was executed on 2026-10-13 with one implementer and one reviewer per task, a whole-branch review, and one fix wave. Five task reviews and the whole-branch review found gaps in this plan's own code; each was fixed in a `fix` commit after its task. **The code blocks above are therefore the plan as written, not the final code**, for the places below. `git log 6a14970..HEAD` is the record.

- **Task 1.** A local `shell` call closes its job when the shell has ended, before it reads the rest of the output (the plan held it until the call returned and so waited 5 seconds for a process it then killed). When output was still open a second later, the result ends with `[a process the command left running in the background was stopped; nothing a command starts outlives it]`. The Windows description of the shell tool tells the model the same.
- **Task 2.** The walking tools judge their search root like every other entry. Before, `grep` with a file as its `path` returned the lines of a file that a `Read` deny rule covers (a gap older than this plan). A refused root is an error: `<path> is denied by this dot's policy`.
- **Task 3.** The row check (`WorkspaceManager::check_owned`) also holds the base commit (40 or 64 hexadecimal characters) and runs before any git command: in the review actions, where an unchanged worktree is cleaned up, where a worktree is restored for a follow-up, and where a run reuses its worktree. Every git command that takes a value from a row gets `--end-of-options` first. A worktree is removed also when git's own removal failed half way (git has by then unregistered it), and `repository_of` answers only for a folder with its own `.git`. The "known limit" about a folder left by an older build is gone with this.
- **Task 4.** `claude_path` also refuses every character that draws nothing (Unicode's default-ignorable code points and U+2800), not only format characters. Note for anyone who replays the plan: Task 4's block replaces `runtime.rs` whole and would drop the check that Task 3's fix added to `review_worktree`.
- **Task 5.** The panic hook is installed first thing in `run()`, before Tauri builds the app. Every instance holds a lock on `dots.lock` in its data folder; a second instance on the same folder waits up to 3 seconds (a restart) and then shows the fatal page. An instance under `DOTS_DATA_DIR` leaves the login entry alone. "Restart Dots" leaves a mark in the data folder so that the restarted app shows its window even when the old one was started with `--hidden`.
- **Task 8.** The confirmation button ignores the second click of a double-click.
- **Task 11.** The document has one more agent step, A23b (a second instance on the same data folder is refused and the first is untouched), so 30 agent steps; and three more person steps (P4.4 during the task, P4.5 and P5.8 after the final fix wave), so 51. The extractor's expression became `^(A\d+[a-z]?)\.`.
- **Numbers.** The "verify they fail" counts of Tasks 7 and 9 were one too high (a form-typing test that fails only under load had been counted) and are corrected above. The test totals printed in the steps are those of the plan as written. The real totals after the fix rounds: core 263 after Task 3, 273 from Task 4 on; app 40 after Task 5, 42 from Task 6 on; at the end, after the final fix wave, **core 285, app 46, UI 222 tests in 20 files, helper 15**; clippy without warnings, fmt and tsc clean.
- **The smoke.** Part A (30 steps, all passed, all 35 commands answered) ran on a debug build at commit `d0dced5`, before the final fix wave, and was not run again.

## After this plan

Plan 3 is complete with this part. Merge order: PR #2 (`feat/app`), PR #3 (`feat/app-ui`), then this branch.

**Still owed by a person**

- Part B of `docs/verification/2026-10-13-plan3-smoke.md`: 51 steps in 8 groups. The real `claude` CLI (a manual, a scheduled and a webhook run, an approval through the Inbox, the network off and on), the tray and close-to-tray, a notification, autostart, the native confirmation for a new Claude program, the installer, and what only a release build does. New since the plan was written: a restart of an installed Dots that was started hidden (P4.5: the window must come back, and the single-instance hand-over and the data-folder lock must not get in the way), autostart left alone under `DOTS_DATA_DIR` (P4.4), and a double-click on a confirmation (P5.8).
- One look, with the real CLI, at whether ending a run's job can interrupt a background self-update of `claude`.
- One `cargo test -p dots-core` run on Linux or macOS. `proc::Job` has a second form there that was never built.
- `workspace_test::an_untracked_symlink_is_listed_and_its_target_is_not_read` on a machine with developer mode or the symlink privilege. Here it only printed its `SKIPPED` line.

**A decision that is open**

- The per-user installer puts the program into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Dots`. Dots keeps its data in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dots`, and Windows ignores the case, so both are one folder: `dots-app.exe` and `uninstall.exe` lie next to the database, the logs and the worktrees. The uninstaller removes its two files and leaves the rest, so no data is lost; its box "Delete the application data" removes only the webview's folders (`dev.dots.app`), so a person who ticks it still has the database, the webhook tokens and the transcripts on disk. The choices are to leave it, to move the data folder (which needs a migration for existing installs), or to give the installer another folder name. Decide before anyone else installs it.

**Found by the smoke and not changed**

- In the dot editor a rule's action box is cut to "allo": the select shrinks beside the tool field (`ui/src/screens/dot-editor/PolicyEditor.tsx`).
- A run that waits for a parked approval shows "Needs approval" and, beside it, "Running for".
- A run that a restart or a crash interrupted keeps its worktree even when nothing changed in it, as spec §8 says. Each stays in the Inbox until it is discarded.
- A chat of one message shows no status badge.
- The first start of an installed build registers autostart without asking, because the setting's default is on.
- The installed program is `dots-app.exe`, not `Dots.exe`. That is the name in Task Manager.

**Known limits**

- A process that a child starts in the 35 to 72 microseconds before it is put into its job is outside the job. A process can also leave a job on purpose (through WMI or the task scheduler); the job is for leftovers, not for a model that hides.
- A rule that names a directory alone (`Read(**/secret)`) does not cover what is below it; `Read(secret/**)` does. `.git` is readable through the read tools under the read presets (a remote URL can hold a token).
- A folder-mode run's row is not checked before its folder is used or opened (`open_run_folder` included): there is nothing to compare the folder with. Only somebody who can write the database can change a row.
- The row check compares path text. A run row written under a differently spelled data folder (a relative `DOTS_DATA_DIR` in development) is refused.
- Cleaning up an unchanged worktree uses the dot's current folder as the repository. After a dot was pointed at another repository while a run was parked, the old repository keeps an empty branch and a stale worktree entry.
- A worktree that somebody locked with `git worktree lock`: a discard deletes the folder, and the branch can then not be deleted until the lock is removed by hand.
- Git 2.31 or newer is needed (`--end-of-options`, `rev-parse --path-format=absolute`); only 2.54 was run.
- A stored `claude_path` that the new rule refuses is dropped without a message in the app; the log has a line.
- When the reload of a chat thread fails, the conversation is replaced by the error note and the typed text is lost.
- The mark that makes a restart show its window is held by unit tests of its two functions; its wiring was not run in the app.
- The installer is not signed, so Windows SmartScreen will warn. Nothing updates an installed Dots.

**Left for later by the reviews** (none blocks a merge; the whole-branch review triaged each)

- `maybe_resume` holds the resume lock across the git cleanup, so every finishing run waits behind one slow `git worktree remove`.
- A discard does not exclude a follow-up that starts at the same moment.
- A first discard fails when the worktree holds a repository of its own (a dot ran `git init` there); the second works.
- `ApprovalCard` reloads nothing after a refused decision, so a card for an approval that was decided elsewhere can stay with live buttons until the next event.
- The Changes tab of a run that is still going shows the diff as first loaded.
- After a lagged event stream, runs that were already going end without a notification.
- A panic message of several lines is written as several lines, only the first with its prefix; each panic can prune the oldest log file.
- `runtime-events.ts`: one failed `listen` skips the catch-up refetch (`Promise.all` without a rejection handler).
- "Restart Dots" can skip its question on a click in the few milliseconds in which the list of active runs is being reloaded.
- `link_on_path` treats a path part it cannot examine as "not a link".
- Under a policy whose default is `deny`, a walk from the root resolves the policy twice per entry over the whole tree; the cost on a large workspace was not measured.
- Tests: wall-clock bounds in two process tests; no test for an engine's path when a job cannot be made; the command-name scan knows only the bare `#[tauri::command]`; no test holds the start-up wiring in `lib.rs`.
- The smoke document, before Part A is run again: see its section "Before Part A is run again".

**Not in this plan**

- A run view that scrolls with new output; token counts that update while a run is going; a prose description of a cron expression.
- Code signing, an updater, and packages for Linux or macOS.
